(-)\circ a:\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\longrightarrow\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x^{\prime},y),\qquad b\circ(-):\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\longrightarrow\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y^{\prime}).

Their composite

b\circ(-)\circ a:\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\longrightarrow\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

is called
whiskering
(by
a
on the right and by
b
on the left).

Lemma 2.1.6

(Whiskering is simplicial)
.

For fixed
a
and
b
, the whiskering map of Definition
2.1.5
is a
morphism of simplicial sets. Equivalently, it commutes with all face and degeneracy
operators.

Proof.

In the quasicategory model,
(x,y)\mapsto\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)
is functorial in both
variables, and the induced pre- and post-composition maps on mapping spaces are
simplicial. Composing these simplicial maps yields the whiskering map, hence it
is simplicial.
∎

2.1.7.
A contractible space of inverse data

Notations 2.1.8
.

For
A,B\in\operatorname{sSet}
, define the mapping simplicial set

\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(A,B)_{r}:=\Hom_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\bigl(A\times\Delta^{r},\,B\bigr),\qquad r\geq 0,

with faces and degeneracies induced by the cosimplicial structure of
\Delta^{\bullet}
.

Notations 2.1.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a quasicategory, that can be arbitrary for the discussion below. Write
\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}\subset\mathcal{C}
for the maximal simplicial
subset spanned by equivalences. Then
\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}
is a Kan complex

[
Lur09b
, Prop. 1.2.5.3]
.

1
1

1

We only use that
\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}
is Kan.

Set

\operatorname{Eq}(\mathcal{C})\ :=\ \Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}),

so that
\operatorname{Eq}(\mathcal{C})_{0}=\Hom_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})
is the set of equivalence-edges of
\mathcal{C}
.

Definition 2.1.10

(Inverse-data fibration)
.

Keep Notation
2.1.9
.
Put
V:=\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{0},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})=\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}
and
B:=\operatorname{Eq}(\mathcal{C})=\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})
.
Let
s,t:B\to V
be evaluation at
0,1
and let
e:V\to B
be the degeneracy (identity edge).

Put
L:=\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{2},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})
and let

r_{01},r_{12},r_{02}:L\longrightarrow B

be restriction along the three edges
(0,1)
,
(1,2)
,
(0,2)
.
Using the pushout decompositions of the outer horns, we identify

\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Lambda^{2}_{0},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})\ \cong\ B\times_{V,s}B,\qquad\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Lambda^{2}_{2},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})\ \cong\ B\times_{V,t}B,

and under these identifications the restriction maps are

(r_{01},r_{02}):L\to B\times_{V,s}B,\qquad(r_{12},r_{02}):L\to B\times_{V,t}B.

Define
\Adj(\mathcal{C})
by the pullback

\Adj(\mathcal{C})\ :=\ B\times_{\,B\times_{V,s}B\,}L\ \times_{\,B\times_{V,t}B\,}L,

where the structure maps
B\to B\times_{V,s}B
and
B\to B\times_{V,t}B
are

f\longmapsto(f,\,e(s(f)))\qquad\text{and}\qquad f\longmapsto(f,\,e(t(f))).

Let
U:\Adj(\mathcal{C})\to B=\operatorname{Eq}(\mathcal{C})
be the projection.
For
f\in\operatorname{Eq}(\mathcal{C})_{0}
set
\operatorname{Inv}(f):=\operatorname{Fib}_{f}(U)
.

Lemma 2.1.11
.

In the situation of Definition
2.1.10
, the projection

U:\Adj(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Eq}(\mathcal{C})

is a trivial Kan fibration. In particular, each
\operatorname{Inv}(f)
is a contractible Kan complex.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}^{\simeq}
is a Kan complex, the outer horn inclusions

\Lambda^{2}_{0}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{2}
and
\Lambda^{2}_{2}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{2}
are anodyne in the Kan-Quillen
model structure, hence the restriction maps

\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{2},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})\to\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Lambda^{2}_{i},\mathcal{C}^{\simeq})\qquad(i=0,2)

are trivial Kan fibrations. Under the identifications in Definition
2.1.10
, these are exactly

(r_{01},r_{02})
and
(r_{12},r_{02})
.
Therefore
U
is obtained from the product of these two trivial Kan fibrations by base change along the maps

B\to B\times_{V,s}B
and
B\to B\times_{V,t}B
, hence
U
is a trivial Kan fibration.
∎

Lemma 2.1.12

(Contractibility of inverse-equivalence data)
.

For any equivalence
f
in a quasicategory
\mathcal{C}
, the
\infty
-groupoid of inverse data for
f

is contractible.

Proof.

Definition
2.1.10
gives an explicit simplicial model
\operatorname{Inv}(f)
for the space of inverse data of
f
,
and Lemma
2.1.11
shows that
\operatorname{Inv}(f)
is a contractible Kan complex.
∎

Remark 2.1.13

(Practical use of Lemma
2.1.12
)
.

In the sequel, Lemma
2.1.12
is used only to justify that whenever a
morphism in
\mathcal{C}
is known to be an equivalence, one may choose an inverse morphism together
with unit/counit homotopies, and that all such choices form a contractible space.

2.2.
Weak Borel-Moore theories

Consider again the setting of the previous subsection: we work
over the base scheme
S
and we fix a pointed
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}

admitting all finite limits.

No monoidal structure on
\mathcal{C}
is part of the axiomatic input in this subsection.
The only structures used later are stability, mapping spaces, the specified simplices listed in
Definition
2.2.12
.

Notations 2.2.1

(Desuspension notation)
.

For
A\in\mathcal{C}
, we write

A[-1]:=\operatorname{Fib}(0\to A),

where
0
is a zero object of
\mathcal{C}
. Inductively, for
r\geq 0
, we write

A[-(r+1)]:=(A[-r])[-1],\qquad A[0]:=A.

For a morphism
f:A\to B
, we write

f[-r]:A[-r]\to B[-r]

for its induced
r
-fold desuspension.
All shifts appearing in Sections
2
-
4
are read in this convention.

Definition 2.2.2

(Principal smooth divisor datum)
.

A
principal smooth divisor datum
on
X\in\Sm_{S}
consists of a closed immersion

i:Z\hookrightarrow X
together with a global function
t\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
such that

(i)

Z=V(t)
scheme-theoretically,

(ii)

Z
is smooth over
S
.

Writing
\mathcal{I}_{Z}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}
for the ideal sheaf of
Z
, the class

\bar{t}\in\mathcal{I}_{Z}/\mathcal{I}_{Z}^{2}

of the defining equation
t
yields a canonical trivialization of the conormal line

\mathcal{N}^{\vee}_{Z/X}=\mathcal{I}_{Z}/\mathcal{I}_{Z}^{2}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{Z},\qquad\bar{t}\longmapsto 1.

Via the conormal exact sequence,
\bar{t}
maps to the differential
dt\in i^{*}\Omega^{1}_{X/S}
.
When the chosen equation
t
is understood from the context, we simply say that

i
is a
principal smooth divisor immersion
.

Remark 2.2.3
.

Principal smooth divisor immersions are stable under smooth base change:
if
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
is cut out by
t\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
and
g:X^{\prime}\to X
is smooth, then

i^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}:=Z\times_{X}X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}

is cut out by
g^{*}t
, is smooth over
S
, and its conormal line is canonically trivialized by the class

\overline{g^{*}t}\in\mathcal{I}_{Z^{\prime}}/\mathcal{I}_{Z^{\prime}}^{2},

which is the pullback of
\bar{t}
.

Definition 2.2.4

(Trivialized regular immersions)
.

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a regular closed immersion in
\Sm_{S}
of codimension
c
.
A
trivialization
of
i
is a chosen isomorphism of vector bundles on
Z

\vartheta_{i}:\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{Z}^{\oplus c}.

A
trivialized regular immersion
is a pair
(i,\vartheta_{i})
.

Remark 2.2.5

(Basic stability properties)
.

(i)

Smooth base change.

If
g:X^{\prime}\to X
is smooth and
(i,\vartheta_{i}):Z\hookrightarrow X
is trivialized regular, then the
base change

i^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}:=Z\times_{X}X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}

is again a regular immersion, with

\mathcal{N}_{Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}}\simeq(g|_{Z^{\prime}})^{*}\mathcal{N}_{Z/X},

and pulling back
\vartheta_{i}
gives a canonical trivialization

\vartheta_{i^{\prime}}:(g|_{Z^{\prime}})^{*}\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}^{\oplus c}.

(ii)

Principal smooth divisors.

If
Z=V(t)\subset X
is a principal smooth divisor, then the conormal line is canonically
trivialized by
dt
, hence the normal line
\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}
is canonically trivialized as well.
Thus every principal smooth divisor immersion carries a canonical trivialization.

(iii)

Units.

For any
X
, the identity immersion
\operatorname{id}_{X}:X\hookrightarrow X
has codimension
0
and

\mathcal{N}_{X/X}=0
, hence carries the canonical trivialization

0\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 0}.

Trivializations of normal bundles do
not
compose canonically in general:
for composable regular immersions
W\xhookrightarrow{k}Z\xhookrightarrow{i}X
, the exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{N}_{W/Z}\to\mathcal{N}_{W/X}\to k^{*}\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}\to 0

does not admit a canonical splitting. Accordingly, later composition statements must be
formulated only when a trivialization of the composite is separately specified.

Definition 2.2.6

(Equation-equipped ordered SNC pair)
.

An
equation-equipped ordered SNC pair
of length
n
is an ordered SNC pair

(X,H_{\bullet})
over
S
such that
X\in\Sm_{S}
, together with global functions

t_{i}\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\qquad(0\leq i\leq n-1)

such that
H_{i}=V(t_{i})
scheme-theoretically for all
i
.

Notations 2.2.7
.

If
K\subseteq[n-1]
and
i\notin K
, we write

\bar{t}_{i}\in\mathcal{I}_{H_{K\cup\{i\}}/H_{K}}/\mathcal{I}_{H_{K\cup\{i\}}/H_{K}}^{2}

for the class of the chosen equation
t_{i}
in the conormal sheaf of

H_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K}
.

Lemma 2.2.8

(Canonical trivializations on strata and comparison with composition)
.

Let
(X,H_{\bullet})
be an equation-equipped ordered SNC pair of length
n
, with
defining equations

t_{i}\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\qquad(0\leq i\leq n-1).

For every inclusion
K\subseteq L\subseteq[n-1]
, the closed immersion

\iota_{K,L}:H_{L}\hookrightarrow H_{K}

is a regular immersion of codimension
|L\setminus K|
. Its conormal sheaf is canonically free
on the ordered basis
(\bar{t}_{i})_{i\in L\setminus K}
, where
\bar{t}_{i}
denotes the class of

t_{i}
in

\mathcal{I}_{H_{L}/H_{K}}/\mathcal{I}_{H_{L}/H_{K}}^{2},

and hence there is a canonical trivialization

\vartheta_{K,L}:\mathcal{N}_{H_{L}/H_{K}}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{H_{L}}^{\oplus|L\setminus K|}.

For every chain
K\subseteq L\subseteq M\subseteq[n-1]
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\alpha_{K,L,M}:\mathcal{N}_{H_{M}/H_{K}}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{N}_{H_{M}/H_{L}}\oplus\iota_{L,M}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{H_{L}/H_{K}}

whose dual is the tautological decomposition of conormal sheaves according to the partition

M\setminus K=(M\setminus L)\sqcup(L\setminus K).

Under the trivializations
\vartheta_{K,M}
,
\vartheta_{L,M}
, and
\vartheta_{K,L}
,
the map
\alpha_{K,L,M}
identifies with the permutation isomorphism

\mathcal{O}_{H_{M}}^{\oplus|M\setminus K|}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{H_{M}}^{\oplus|M\setminus L|}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{H_{M}}^{\oplus|L\setminus K|}

obtained by rearranging the ordered basis
(dt_{i})_{i\in M\setminus K}
into the two ordered blocks

(\bar{t}_{i})_{i\in M\setminus L}\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\bar{t}_{i})_{i\in L\setminus K}.

Proof.

The immersion
\iota_{K,L}
is cut out in
H_{K}
by the regular sequence
(t_{i})_{i\in L\setminus K}
,
so its conormal bundle is canonically free with basis
(\bar{t}_{i})_{i\in L\setminus K}
. Dualizing gives

\vartheta_{K,L}
.

For
K\subseteq L\subseteq M
, the conormal sheaf of
\iota_{K,M}
is canonically

\bigoplus_{i\in M\setminus K}\mathcal{O}_{H_{M}}\,\bar{t}_{i},

and similarly for the two smaller immersions. Splitting according to the partition

M\setminus K=(M\setminus L)\sqcup(L\setminus K)

gives the required decomposition on conormal sheaves; dualizing yields
\alpha_{K,L,M}
.
∎

Remark 2.2.9

(Associativity of the ordered block decompositions)
.

For every chain

K\subseteq L\subseteq M\subseteq N\subseteq[n-1],

the two iterated decompositions of
\mathcal{N}_{H_{N}/H_{K}}
obtained from
Lemma
2.2.8
coincide.
Dually, both are the tautological decomposition of the conormal basis

(dt_{i})_{i\in N\setminus K}

into the three ordered blocks indexed by

N\setminus M,\qquad M\setminus L,\qquad L\setminus K.

Lemma 2.2.10

(SNC square faces satisfy the divisor-closed transversality hypotheses)
.

Let
(X,H_{\bullet})
be an equation-equipped ordered SNC pair, let

K\subseteq[n-1]
, and let
i,j\notin K
be distinct. Consider the cartesian
square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K\cup\{i,j\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K\cup\{i\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K\cup\{j\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K}.}

Then this square satisfies the transversality hypotheses of
Axiom
(wBM6)
, with the principal divisor

H_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K}
cut out by
t_{i}|_{H_{K}}
and the regular
immersion
H_{K\cup\{j\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K}
cut out by
t_{j}|_{H_{K}}
.
The compatibility of the composite normal-bundle trivializations is the
block-transposition compatibility of Lemma
2.2.8
.

Proof.

All assertions are local on
H_{K}
. The ordered SNC hypothesis says that

H_{K\cup\{i\}}
,
H_{K\cup\{j\}}
, and
H_{K\cup\{i,j\}}
are smooth over

S
, and that they are cut out in
H_{K}
by the indicated coordinate equations.
Thus the pullback of
t_{i}|_{H_{K}}
to
H_{K\cup\{j\}}
cuts out

H_{K\cup\{i,j\}}
as a principal smooth divisor, and the restrictions of

H_{K\cup\{j\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K}
to the closed and open complements remain
regular immersions with the induced coordinate trivializations.

The comparison of the two normal-bundle decompositions is precisely the
comparison of the ordered bases

(\bar{t}_{i},\bar{t}_{j})\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\bar{t}_{j},\bar{t}_{i}),

hence is the block transposition described in
Lemma
2.2.8
.
∎

Notations 2.2.11
.

Recall that we write
\Sm^{\mathit{sm}}_{S}\subset\Sm_{S}
for the wide subcategory with the same objects as
\Sm_{S}
and whose morphisms are the smooth
S
-morphisms.

Definition 2.2.12

(Weak Borel-Moore theory)
.

Let
S
be a base scheme and let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category.
For
n\geq 0
and
X\in\Sm_{S}
, write

q_{n}:X\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}=X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\longrightarrow X

for the projection.
All occurrences of
[r]
below refer to the iterated suspension notation of
Notation
2.2.1
.

A
weak Borel-Moore theory

on
\Sm_{S}
with values in
\mathcal{C}
consists of the following data:

(wBM1)

For every
X\in\Sm_{S}
and
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, an object
\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\in\mathcal{C}
.

(wBM2)

For every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, the assignment

X\longmapsto\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)

on objects of the wide subcategory
\Sm^{\mathit{sm}}_{S}\subset\Sm_{S}
of smooth morphisms is equipped with a
homotopy coherent smooth contravariance, i.e. a simplicial map

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\mathit{sm}}:N(\Sm^{\mathit{sm}}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

whose value on an edge

p:Y\to X

is a morphism

p^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y).

In particular, this includes specified
2
-simplices exhibiting

(q\circ p)^{*}\simeq p^{*}\circ q^{*},\qquad(\operatorname{id}_{X})^{*}\simeq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)},

and all higher coherences among them.

(wBM3)

For every trivialized regular immersion
(i,\vartheta_{i}):Z\hookrightarrow X
of codimension
c
,
a primitive closed pushforward

i_{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m-c}(Z)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(X).

For the identity immersion
\operatorname{id}_{X}:X\hookrightarrow X
with its canonical trivialization,
the induced map is identified with the identity on
\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)
.

Moreover, for composable trivialized regular immersions

W\xhookrightarrow{k}Z\xhookrightarrow{i}X

of codimensions
d
and
c
, together with

•

a chosen trivialization

\vartheta_{i\circ k}:\mathcal{N}_{W/X}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus(c+d)},

of the composite
i\circ k
, and

•

a chosen isomorphism of vector bundles

\alpha_{k,i}:\mathcal{N}_{W/X}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{N}_{W/Z}\oplus k^{*}\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}

such that, under
\alpha_{k,i}
, the trivialization
\vartheta_{i\circ k}
agrees with the block-sum
trivialization

\vartheta_{k}\oplus k^{*}\vartheta_{i}:\mathcal{N}_{W/Z}\oplus k^{*}\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus d}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus c}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{W}^{\oplus(c+d)},

we are given a specified homotopy

(i\circ k)_{*}\ \simeq\ i_{*}\circ k_{*}.

(wBM4)

For every cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

with
g
smooth and
(i,\vartheta_{i})
a trivialized regular immersion of codimension
c
,

where
i^{\prime}
is endowed with the induced pulled-back trivialization, a Beck-Chevalley homotopy

g^{*}\circ i_{*}\ \simeq\ i^{\prime}_{*}\circ(g^{\prime})^{*}

as morphisms
\mathcal{G}_{m-c}(Z)\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(X^{\prime})
.

(wBM5)

For every principal smooth divisor immersion
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
with open complement
j:U\hookrightarrow X
,
a fiber sequence

\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-1]\xrightarrow{\,\partial\,}\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(Z)\xrightarrow{\,i_{*}\,}\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\xrightarrow{\,j^{*}\,}\mathcal{G}_{m}(U),

functorial under smooth base change.

(wBM6)

For every cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

in
\Sm_{S}
satisfying the following transversality hypotheses:

(a)

i:Z\hookrightarrow X
is a principal smooth divisor immersion, say cut out by

t\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
;

(b)

g:X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
is a trivialized regular immersion of codimension
c
;

(c)

the pullback
i^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}=Z\times_{X}X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
is again a principal smooth
divisor immersion, cut out by
g^{*}t
;

(d)

the base changes

g^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Z,\qquad g_{U}:U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow U

are trivialized regular immersions of codimension
c
, with the trivializations induced from that of

g
;

(e)

the two ordered block-sum trivializations of the normal bundle of the
composite immersion
Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
, obtained from the two factorizations

Z^{\prime}\xhookrightarrow{i^{\prime}}X^{\prime}\xhookrightarrow{g}X\qquad\text{and}\qquad Z^{\prime}\xhookrightarrow{g^{\prime}}Z\xhookrightarrow{i}X,

are compatible through the canonical exchange isomorphism of the cartesian square.
Equivalently, after identifying both block decompositions with

\mathcal{N}_{Z^{\prime}/X}
, one has

\vartheta_{g^{\prime}}\oplus g^{\prime}{}^{*}\vartheta_{i}=\mathrm{sw}_{1,c}\circ\bigl(\vartheta_{i^{\prime}}\oplus i^{\prime}{}^{*}\vartheta_{g}\bigr),

where

\mathrm{sw}_{1,c}:\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}^{\oplus c}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}^{\oplus c}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{Z^{\prime}}

is the block transposition exchanging the divisor line with the codimension-
c

normal block. In particular, no literal equality of the two ordered block sums
is imposed before applying this exchange.

Write

U:=X\setminus Z,\qquad U^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\setminus Z^{\prime},

and let

j:U\hookrightarrow X,\qquad j^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}

be the open immersions.
Then a specified morphism of fiber sequences

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m-c}(U^{\prime})[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{g_{U*}[-1]}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m-c-1}(Z^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m-c}(X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{\prime*}}
\scriptstyle{g_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m-c}(U^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{U*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)}

is part of the data.

(wBM7)

For every equation-equipped ordered SNC pair
(X,H_{\bullet})
of length
n
and every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
,
a chosen functor

\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

such that:

(a)

for every
K\subseteq[n-1]
,

\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)(K)=\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K});

(b)

for every covering relation
K\subset K\cup\{i\}
, the corresponding edge in
\mathcal{C}
is the primitive closed pushforward attached to the principal smooth divisor immersion

\iota_{K,i}:H_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K},

namely

(\iota_{K,i})_{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|-1}(H_{K\cup\{i\}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K});

(c)

for every
K\subseteq[n-1]
and every distinct
i,j\notin K
, the restriction of

\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)
to the corresponding square in
\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}}

is the square in
\mathcal{C}
equipped with the specified homotopy coming from
Axiom
(wBM6)
, using the canonical ordered trivializations on the strata and their canonical compatibility from
Lemma
2.2.8
;

(d)

for every isomorphism of equation-equipped ordered SNC pairs

\theta:(X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(X,H_{\bullet})

compatible with the chosen equations, there is a specified isomorphism of cubes

\eta_{\theta}:\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m)

whose component at
K\subseteq[n-1]
is the smooth pullback

(\theta_{K})^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H^{\prime}_{K}),

and these specified cube isomorphisms are functorial in
\theta
.

(wBM8)

For every vector bundle projection
\pi:E\to X
, the smooth pullback

\pi^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(E)

is an equivalence.

(wBM9)

For every vector bundle projection
\pi:E\to X
, chosen inverse data

(\pi^{*})^{-1}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(E)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(X),

together with specified
2
-simplices exhibiting

(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ\pi^{*}\simeq\operatorname{id},\qquad\pi^{*}\circ(\pi^{*})^{-1}\simeq\operatorname{id}.

These inverse data are required to satisfy the following compatibilities.

(a)

For composable vector bundle projections

E^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\ \rho\ }E^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\ \pi\ }X,

a specified homotopy

((\pi\circ\rho)^{*})^{-1}\ \simeq\ (\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ(\rho^{*})^{-1},

compatible with identities and associativity.

(b)

For every cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

with
g
smooth,

a specified homotopy

(g)^{*}\circ(\pi^{*})^{-1}\ \simeq\ ((\pi^{\prime})^{*})^{-1}\circ(g_{E})^{*}.

(c)

For every cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

with
(i,\vartheta_{i})
a trivialized regular immersion,

where
i_{E}
is endowed with the induced pulled-back trivialization, a specified homotopy

(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ(i_{E})_{*}\ \simeq\ i_{*}\circ((\pi^{\prime})^{*})^{-1}.

(wBM10)

For every
n\geq 0
, every
X\in\Sm_{S}
, and every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, a
inflation map

\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\bigl(X\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\bigr)[-n],

with the convention

\beta^{(0)}_{X,m}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

(wBM11)

For every smooth morphism
f:Y\to X
and every
n\geq 0
, writing

f^{(n)}:=f\times\operatorname{id}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}}:Y\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\longrightarrow X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n},

a specified homotopy

(f^{(n)})^{*}[-n]\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(n)}_{Y,m}\circ f^{*}.

(wBM12)

For every
n\geq 0
, every
X\in\Sm_{S}
, and every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, let

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}\bigl(X\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}\bigr)[-n-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\bigl(X\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\bigr)[-n]

be the residue morphism obtained from the localization fiber sequence of

X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\times\{0\}\hookrightarrow X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}.

Then there is a specified homotopy

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{X,m}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(n)}_{X,m}.

For
n=0
, this reads

\delta_{t}\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}\ \simeq\ \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

(wBM13)

For every
X\in\Sm_{S}
and every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, an automorphism

\epsilon_{X,m}\in\Aut_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\bigr),

and for every
n\geq 2
and every adjacent transposition

s_{r}:X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\longrightarrow X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\qquad(0\leq r\leq n-2),

a specified homotopy

(s_{r})^{*}[-n]\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\circ\epsilon_{X,m}.

These homotopies are required to be coherent under the braid relations and compatible with
Axiom
(wBM11)
.

(wBM14)

inflations commute with localization for principal smooth divisors
:
for every principal smooth divisor immersion
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
with open complement
j:U\hookrightarrow X
,
and every
n\geq 0
, the inflation maps
\beta^{(n)}
induce a morphism of fiber sequences

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{(n)}_{U,m}[-1]}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{(n)}_{Z,m-1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{(n)}_{U,m}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\!\bigl(U\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\bigr)[-n-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}\!\bigl(Z\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\bigr)[-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\!\bigl(X\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\bigr)[-n]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\!\bigl(U\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\bigr)[-n]}

with the horizontal fiber sequences being the chosen ones from
Axiom
(wBM5)
.

Remark 2.2.13

(Input closed pushforwards versus output closed pullbacks)
.

The maps

i_{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m-c}(Z)\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)

for trivialized regular immersions are part of the axiomatic input.
By contrast, the higher Gysin pullbacks

i^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z)

for arbitrary closed immersions are constructed later from deformation spaces,
localization,
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance, and the inflation package.
Thus the notation
i_{*}
belongs to the input formalism, whereas
i^{*}
belongs
to the output of the higher Gysin construction.

Notations 2.2.14

(inflation package)
.

We refer collectively to Axioms
(wBM10)
-
(wBM14)

as the
inflation package
.

Remark 2.2.15
.

Examples of weak Borel-Moore theories will be given in
Section
6
; see in particular
Proposition
6.1.4
.

2.3.
Localization cubes and the coherent total boundary simplex

In this subsection, we fix a weak Borel-Moore theory
\mathcal{G}
in the sense of Definition
2.2.12
.

Construction 2.3.1

(The localization cube)
.

Let
(X,H_{\bullet})
be an equation-equipped ordered SNC pair of length
n
, indexed by

[n-1]=\{0,\dots,n-1\}
, and fix an integer
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Applying Axiom
(wBM7)
, we obtain a chosen
n
-cube

\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

with

\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)(K)=\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K}).

For every covering relation
K\subset K\cup\{i\}
, the corresponding edge is the primitive closed pushforward

(\iota_{K,i})_{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|-1}(H_{K\cup\{i\}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K})

for the principal smooth divisor immersion

\iota_{K,i}:H_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K}.

For later reference, if
K\subseteq[n-1]
and
i\notin K
, let

\iota_{K,i}:H_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K}

be the corresponding principal smooth divisor immersion, and let

j_{K,i}:H_{K}\setminus H_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K}

be its open complement. Applying Axiom
(wBM5)
in degree
m-|K|
yields a fiber sequence

(2.3.1.a)

\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}\bigl(H_{K}\setminus H_{K\cup\{i\}}\bigr)[-1]\xrightarrow{\partial_{K,i}}\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|-1}(H_{K\cup\{i\}})\xrightarrow{(\iota_{K,i})_{*}}\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K})\xrightarrow{(j_{K,i})^{*}}\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}\bigl(H_{K}\setminus H_{K\cup\{i\}}\bigr).

Construction 2.3.2

(Iterated fibers and the coherent total boundary simplex)
.

Let
\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)
be as in Construction
2.3.1
.

Since
\mathcal{C}
is pointed and admits finite limits, the functor category

\Fun(\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}},\mathcal{C})
is again pointed and admits finite limits, computed objectwise.
In particular, objectwise fibers in any fixed direction are defined.

For
i\in[n-1]
, define an
(n-1)
-cube

\operatorname{Fib}_{i}\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)

by taking objectwise fibers in the
i
-direction:

\bigl(\operatorname{Fib}_{i}(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m))\bigr)(K):=\operatorname{Fib}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|-1}(H_{K\cup\{i\}})\xrightarrow{(\iota_{K,i})_{*}}\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K})\Bigr),\qquad(i\notin K).

By the localization fiber sequence (
2.3.1.a
), there is a canonical identification

(2.3.2.a)

\bigl(\operatorname{Fib}_{i}(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m))\bigr)(K)\;\simeq\;\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}\bigl(H_{K}\setminus H_{K\cup\{i\}}\bigr)[-1].

Now iterate in the given order of the divisors:

\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr):=\operatorname{Fib}_{n-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{1}\operatorname{Fib}_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)\in\mathcal{C}.

Writing

U:=X\setminus\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}H_{i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad j:U\hookrightarrow X,

Lemma
2.3.3
yields a canonical equivalence

(2.3.2.b)

\theta_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n].

The iterated fiber tower canonically produces a morphism

\widetilde{\underline{\partial}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-n}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}\bigr),

defined as the composite of the successive residue morphisms arising in the

\operatorname{Fib}_{i}
-steps. Using (
2.3.2.b
), define the
total boundary morphism

{\underline{\partial}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):=\widetilde{\underline{\partial}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\circ\theta_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)^{-1}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-n}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}\bigr).

Finally, the iterated fiber tower determines a composable string of morphisms

\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n]=:E_{0}\longrightarrow E_{1}\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow E_{n}:=\mathcal{G}_{m-n}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}\bigr),

and hence a simplicial map

s_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

sending the vertex
j
to
E_{j}
and the edge
(j-1)\to j
to the corresponding morphism.

Here
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}\subset\Delta^{n}
denotes the spine, i.e. the simplicial subset generated by the edges

0\to 1\to\cdots\to n
.

Restriction along
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}
induces a map

\operatorname{res}:\Fun(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\Fun({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},\mathcal{C}).

Since
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}
is inner anodyne and
\mathcal{C}
is a quasicategory,
the induced map on cores

\operatorname{res}^{\simeq}:\Fun(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\longrightarrow\Fun({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}

is a trivial Kan fibration. We therefore obtain a contractible Kan complex of fillers

\operatorname{Fill}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):=\operatorname{Fib}_{\,s_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)}\bigl(\operatorname{res}^{\simeq}\bigr).

Choose, in the sense of contractible choice, a vertex

\widetilde{\underline{\partial}}^{\mathrm{sp}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\in\operatorname{Fill}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m).

Using the standard identification between
n
-simplices in
\mathcal{C}
with fixed endpoints

(E_{0},E_{n})
and
(n-1)
-simplices in the right mapping space
\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(E_{0},E_{n})
,
the chosen filler determines an element

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n],\,\mathcal{G}_{m-n}(H_{[n-1]})\bigr)_{n-1}.

Lemma 2.3.3

(Total fiber of the localization cube)
.

In the situation of Construction
2.3.2
, let

U^{(r)}:=X\setminus\bigcup_{j<r}H_{j}\qquad(0\leq r\leq n),

so that
U^{(0)}=X
and
U^{(n)}=U=X\setminus\bigcup_{j=0}^{n-1}H_{j}
.
For
K\subseteq\{r,\dots,n-1\}
, set

H_{K}^{(r)}:=H_{K}\cap U^{(r)},

and let

j_{K}^{(r)}:H_{K}^{(r)}\hookrightarrow H_{K}

denote the open immersion. Then, for each
0\leq r\leq n
, there is a canonical equivalence of

(n-r)
-cubes

\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{1}\operatorname{Fib}_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)\ \simeq\ \bigl(K\mapsto\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K}^{(r)})[-r]\bigr),

where
K\subseteq\{r,\dots,n-1\}
.
In particular,

\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)\ \simeq\ \mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n].

(For
r=0
, the iterated functor
\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}
is understood as the identity.)

Proof.

We argue by induction on
r
.

For
r=0
, the statement is tautological.

Assume the statement holds for
r
, and fix
K\subseteq\{r+1,\dots,n-1\}
.
In the
(n-r)
-cube on the right-hand side, the
r
-direction map at
K
is

\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|-1}(H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)})[-r]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K}^{(r)})[-r],

where
H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)}=H_{K\cup\{r\}}\cap U^{(r)}
is a principal smooth divisor in

H_{K}^{(r)}=H_{K}\cap U^{(r)}
, with open complement

H_{K}^{(r)}\setminus H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)}=H_{K}\cap U^{(r+1)}=H_{K}^{(r+1)}.

Applying the localization fiber sequence (
2.3.1.a
) to this pair in degree
m-|K|

gives a canonical identification

\operatorname{Fib}\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|-1}(H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)})\to\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K}^{(r)})\Bigr)\simeq\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K}^{(r+1)})[-1].

Including the existing
[-r]
-shift yields the required
[-(r+1)]
-shift, and the construction is
natural in
K
, so it assembles into an equivalence of cubes.
This proves the induction step.

For
r=n
, the only remaining index set is
K=\varnothing
, and the result is

\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)\simeq\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n].

∎

Lemma 2.3.4

(Naturality of the open-complement identification)
.

Let
(X,H_{\bullet})
and
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet})
be equation-equipped ordered SNC pairs of length
n
,
with defining equations

t_{i}\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}),\qquad t^{\prime}_{i}\in\Gamma(X^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}})\qquad(0\leq i\leq n-1).

Let

\theta:(X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(X,H_{\bullet})

be an isomorphism of ordered SNC pairs such that

\theta^{*}(t_{i})=t^{\prime}_{i}\qquad(0\leq i\leq n-1).

Write

U:=X\setminus\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}H_{i},\qquad U^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\setminus\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}H^{\prime}_{i},

and let

\eta_{\theta}:\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m)

be the induced isomorphism of localization cubes from
Axiom
(wBM7)
(d)
. Its component at

K\subseteq[n-1]
is

(\theta_{K})^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H^{\prime}_{K}).

Then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{m}(U^{\prime})[-n]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\TotFib(\eta_{\theta})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\theta_{U})^{*}[-n]}

commutes up to a canonical homotopy.

Proof.

For
0\leq r\leq n
, keep the notation of Lemma
2.3.3
:

U^{(r)}:=X\setminus\bigcup_{j<r}H_{j},\qquad U^{\prime(r)}:=X^{\prime}\setminus\bigcup_{j<r}H^{\prime}_{j},

and for
K\subseteq\{r,\dots,n-1\}
,

H_{K}^{(r)}:=H_{K}\cap U^{(r)},\qquad H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}:=H^{\prime}_{K}\cap U^{\prime(r)}.

The isomorphism
\theta
induces isomorphisms

\theta_{K}^{(r)}:H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H_{K}^{(r)}.

We claim by induction on
r
that, after transporting along the identifications constructed in the
proof of Lemma
2.3.3
, the morphism obtained from

\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\eta_{\theta})

identifies with the natural transformation of
(n-r)
-cubes

K\longmapsto(\theta_{K}^{(r)})^{*}[-r]:\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}\!\bigl(H_{K}^{(r)}\bigr)[-r]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}\!\bigl(H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}\bigr)[-r].

For
r=0
, this is tautological.
Assume the claim proved for
r
, and fix
K\subseteq\{r+1,\dots,n-1\}
.
Then we have a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}_{K\cup\{r\}}{}^{(r)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\prime}_{K}{}^{(r)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K}^{(r)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}_{K,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{K,r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{K}^{(r)}}

Because
\theta^{*}(t_{r})=t^{\prime}_{r}
, the two vertical immersions are the corresponding principal smooth
divisor immersions cut out by the matching equations
t_{r}
and
t^{\prime}_{r}
.
Hence Axiom
(wBM4)
gives a specified homotopy

\big(\theta_{K}^{(r)}\big)^{*}\circ(\iota_{K,r})_{*}\;\simeq\;(\iota^{\prime}_{K,r})_{*}\circ\big(\theta_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)}\big)^{*}.

In other words, the comparison between the
r
-direction maps of the two cubes is exactly the one
needed to pass to fibers.
Using the naturality of localization fiber sequences, the fiber in the
r
-direction therefore
identifies with

(\theta_{K}^{(r+1)})^{*}[-r-1]:\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}\!\bigl(H_{K}^{(r+1)}\bigr)[-r-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}\!\bigl(H_{K}^{\prime(r+1)}\bigr)[-r-1].

This proves the induction step.

For
r=n
, only
K=\varnothing
remains, and the induced map is

(\theta_{U})^{*}[-n]:\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(U^{\prime})[-n].

This is precisely the claimed compatibility with the identifications

\theta_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)
and
\theta_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m)
.

∎

Lemma 2.3.5

(Functoriality of
{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}
under natural transformations of cubes)
.

Let
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}
be
n
-cubes and let

\eta:\mathcal{K}\Rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\prime}

be a natural transformation.

(1)

For each
i\in[n-1]
, applying objectwise fibers in the
i
-direction defines a functor

\operatorname{Fib}_{i}:\Fun(\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}},\mathcal{C})\to\Fun(\mathcal{P}([n-1]\setminus\{i\})^{\operatorname{op}},\mathcal{C}),

and
\eta
induces a natural transformation

\operatorname{Fib}_{i}(\eta):\operatorname{Fib}_{i}(\mathcal{K})\Rightarrow\operatorname{Fib}_{i}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}).

Iterating in the fixed order yields an induced morphism

\TotFib(\eta):\TotFib(\mathcal{K})\longrightarrow\TotFib(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}).

(2)

The induced map
\TotFib(\eta)
is compatible with the total boundary morphisms

{\underline{\partial}}(\mathcal{K}):\TotFib(\mathcal{K})\to\mathcal{K}([n-1]),\qquad{\underline{\partial}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime}):\TotFib(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{K}^{\prime}([n-1]),

in the sense that the square

\textstyle{\TotFib(\mathcal{K})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\underline{\partial}}(\mathcal{K})}
\scriptstyle{\TotFib(\eta)}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}([n-1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{[n-1]}}
\textstyle{\TotFib(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\underline{\partial}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\prime}([n-1])}

commutes up to the canonical homotopy determined by naturality of connecting morphisms.

(3)

Whiskering by
\eta_{[n-1]}
and
\TotFib(\eta)
induces a canonical
n
-simplex

\mathcal{H}^{{\underline{\partial}}}_{\eta}\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}),\mathcal{K}^{\prime}([n-1])\bigr)_{n}

whose boundary gives a homotopy between the two
(n-1)
-simplices

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\circ\TotFib(\eta)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\eta_{[n-1]}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}),\mathcal{K}^{\prime}([n-1])\bigr).

The space of choices of
\mathcal{H}^{{\underline{\partial}}}_{\eta}
is contractible.

In particular, if
\eta
is an objectwise equivalence, then
\TotFib(\eta)
is an equivalence and,
after transporting endpoints along
\eta_{[n-1]}
and
\TotFib(\eta)
, the simplices

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})
and

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

For (1), fibers are functorial in morphisms of arrows in a stable
\infty
-category. Applying this
objectwise in the
i
-direction gives the functors
\operatorname{Fib}_{i}
, and iteration gives
\TotFib(\eta)
.

For (2), the morphism
{\underline{\partial}}(\mathcal{K})
is the composite of the successive
connecting morphisms in the iterated fiber tower. Connecting morphisms are natural with respect to
morphisms of fiber sequences, so the displayed square commutes up to the canonical homotopy.

For (3), the natural transformation
\eta:\mathcal{K}\Rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
induces a morphism
of the corresponding iterated fiber towers, hence a morphism between the spine diagrams used in
Construction
2.3.2
. Since

{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}

is inner anodyne and
\mathcal{C}
is a quasicategory, restriction of fillers is a trivial Kan fibration on
cores. Thus the space of fillers compatible with this morphism of spine diagrams is contractible.
Under the standard identification between
n
-simplices with fixed endpoints and
(n-1)
-simplices
in the right mapping space, this gives the asserted homotopy

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})\circ\TotFib(\eta)\simeq\eta_{[n-1]}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}),\mathcal{K}^{\prime}([n-1])\bigr)_{n-1}.

∎

Lemma 2.3.6

(Naturality of total boundary simplices under isomorphisms of equation-equipped ordered SNC pairs)
.

Let
(X,H_{\bullet})
and
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet})
be equation-equipped ordered SNC pairs of length
n
,
with defining equations

t_{i}\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}),\qquad t^{\prime}_{i}\in\Gamma(X^{\prime},\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}})\qquad(0\leq i\leq n-1).

Let

\theta:(X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(X,H_{\bullet})

be an isomorphism of ordered SNC pairs over
S
such that

\theta^{*}(t_{i})=t^{\prime}_{i}\qquad(0\leq i\leq n-1).

Fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, and write

U:=X\setminus\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}H_{i},\qquad U^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\setminus\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}H^{\prime}_{i}.

Let

\theta_{[n-1]}:H^{\prime}_{[n-1]}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H_{[n-1]},\qquad\theta_{U}:U^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }U

be the induced isomorphisms.

Then the two
(n-1)
-simplices

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m)\circ(\theta_{U})^{*}[-n]\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\theta_{[n-1]})^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n],\,\mathcal{G}_{m-n}(H^{\prime}_{[n-1]})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

By Axiom
(wBM7)
(d), the isomorphism

\theta:(X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(X,H_{\bullet})

induces a specified isomorphism of cubes

\eta_{\theta}:\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m),

whose component at
K\subseteq[n-1]
is
(\theta_{K})^{*}
.

Applying Lemma
2.3.5
to
\eta_{\theta}
gives a canonical homotopy

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m)\circ\TotFib(\eta_{\theta})\;\simeq\;(\theta_{[n-1]})^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr),\mathcal{G}_{m-n}(H^{\prime}_{[n-1]})\Bigr)_{n-1}.

By Lemma
2.3.4
, the map
\TotFib(\eta_{\theta})
identifies with

(\theta_{U})^{*}[-n]:\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(U^{\prime})[-n].

Substitution gives the claimed homotopy.
∎

Example 2.3.7

(The localization square and total boundary for
n=2
)
.

Let
(X,H_{\bullet})=(X,H_{0},H_{1})
be an ordered SNC pair of length
2
, and fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.
Write

H_{01}:=H_{0}\cap H_{1},\qquad U:=X\setminus(H_{0}\cup H_{1}).

(1) The localization
2
-cube.

The cube

\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):\mathcal{P}([1])^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}

of Construction
2.3.1
is the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{m-2}(H_{01})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(H_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(H_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}_{m}(X),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{\{0\},1})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{\{1\},0})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{\varnothing,0})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{\varnothing,1})_{*}}

where
\iota_{\varnothing,0}:H_{0}\hookrightarrow X
,
\iota_{\varnothing,1}:H_{1}\hookrightarrow X
,
and
\iota_{\{0\},1}:H_{01}\hookrightarrow H_{0}
,
\iota_{\{1\},0}:H_{01}\hookrightarrow H_{1}
.

(2) Iterated fibers and identification with the open complement.

Let

U_{0}:=X\setminus H_{0},\qquad j_{0}:U_{0}\hookrightarrow X,\qquad j_{\{1\},0}:H_{1}\setminus H_{01}\hookrightarrow H_{1}.

Taking fibers in the
0
-direction yields

\operatorname{Fib}_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)(\varnothing)\simeq\mathcal{G}_{m}(U_{0})[-1],

\operatorname{Fib}_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)(\{1\})\simeq\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(H_{1}\setminus H_{01})[-1].

The induced edge

\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\mathcal{K})(\varnothing)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\mathcal{K})(\{1\})

identifies with the closed pullback for the principal smooth divisor immersion

H_{1}\cap U_{0}=H_{1}\setminus H_{01}\hookrightarrow U_{0}.

Therefore taking
\operatorname{Fib}_{1}
gives the canonical equivalence

\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-2].

(3) The total boundary morphism and the coherent
1
-simplex.

Under the above identification, the total boundary morphism

{\underline{\partial}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-2]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-2}(H_{01})

is the composite of the two connecting morphisms determined by the order
(H_{0},H_{1})
:

\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-2]\xrightarrow{\ \partial_{H_{1}\cap U_{0}\subset U_{0}}\ }\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(H_{1}\setminus H_{01})[-1]\xrightarrow{\ \partial_{H_{01}\subset H_{1}}\ }\mathcal{G}_{m-2}(H_{01}).

Equivalently, it is the boundary for the pair
(H_{1},H_{01})
followed by the boundary for the pair

(U_{0},H_{1}\cap U_{0})
.

The simplex

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-2],\mathcal{G}_{m-2}(H_{01})\bigr)_{1}

is the coherent
1
-simplex produced by Construction
2.3.2
from the
bicartesian square above.

2.4.
Higher Gysin construction

Notations 2.4.1

(Four layers of notation in Sections
2
-
4
)
.

We distinguish four kinds of objects.

(i)

Geometric input attached to a simplex

\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}:\qquad D(\sigma),\ H_{K}(\sigma),\ U_{\sigma},\ \pi_{\sigma},\ \varphi_{\sigma}.

(ii)

Structural maps belonging to the dual axiomatic package in
\mathcal{C}
:

p^{*},\ i_{*},\ \partial,\ \beta^{(n)}_{X,m},\ \epsilon_{X,m}.

(iii)

The simplexwise higher closed-immersion pullback datum:

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\sigma)\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)_{n-1},

and the corresponding simplex

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma):\Delta^{n}\to\mathcal{C}.

(iv)

The final rigidified simplicial map:

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{m}:N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}.

Notations 2.4.2

(Geometric input)
.

Fix
n\geq 1
, a simplex

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z_{1}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},

and an integer
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.
Write

g_{\sigma}:Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z_{n}

for the composite regular closed immersion.

Let

p_{\sigma}:D(\sigma)\longrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{n}_{S}

be the higher deformation space, with standard coordinates
t_{0},\dots,t_{n-1}
on
\mathbb{A}^{n}_{S}
.
For each
i\in[n-1]
, let

H_{i}(\sigma)\subset D(\sigma)

be the Cartier divisor cut out by
t_{i}=0
. We write

H(\sigma):=\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}H_{i}(\sigma),\qquad U_{\sigma}:=D(\sigma)\setminus H(\sigma),\qquad H_{[n-1]}(\sigma):=\bigcap_{i=0}^{n-1}H_{i}(\sigma).

More generally, for each
K\subseteq[n-1]
, write

H_{K}(\sigma):=\bigcap_{k\in K}H_{k}(\sigma).

The ordering

\bigl(H_{0}(\sigma),\dots,H_{n-1}(\sigma)\bigr)

is part of the data; it fixes the order of the iterated boundary maps.

Let

\varphi_{\sigma}:U_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}

be the canonical identification of Lemma
1.4.4
, and let

\pi_{\sigma}:H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\to Z_{0}

be the vector bundle projection of Lemma
1.4.6
(2).

Idea of the definition.

Construction
2.3.2

applied to the ordered SNC pair
D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)
already produces, in the corrected
fiber-valued formalism, a simplex in
\mathcal{C}
from the open stratum to the deepest stratum. We then
precompose with the inflation map from
Z_{n}
to the open stratum and postcompose with the chosen
inverse to the vector-bundle pullback
\pi_{\sigma}^{*}
. Thus the higher closed-immersion pullback is the factorization

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)=(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n)\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}.

Remark 2.4.3

(Convention: transport along isomorphisms)
.

If
f:Y\xrightarrow{\sim}X
is an isomorphism in
\Sm_{S}
, we write

f^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y)

for the induced equivalence from smooth contravariance, and we
define

(f^{*})^{-1}:=(f^{-1})^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}(X).

All transport statements along isomorphisms use this convention.

Definition 2.4.4

(Higher Gysin simplex)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}\qquad(n\geq 1),

and fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Apply Construction
2.3.2
to the ordered SNC pair

\bigl(D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)\bigr)

with input degree
m+n
. This yields a coherent total boundary simplex

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}:={\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n)\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n],\,\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr)_{n-1}.

Define the inflation-to-the-open-stratum map

\beta_{\sigma,m}:=(\varphi_{\sigma})^{*}[-n]\circ\beta^{(n)}_{Z_{n},m}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n],

where
(\varphi_{\sigma})^{*}[-n]
denotes the shifted smooth pullback along the isomorphism

\varphi_{\sigma}:U_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}
.

Then define

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)_{n-1}

to be the whiskered composite

\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n})\xrightarrow{\beta_{\sigma,m}}\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n]\xrightarrow{{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}}\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr)\xrightarrow{(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}}\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0}).

Finally, for a vertex
\sigma=(Z)\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{0}
and an integer
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, we set trivially

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma):=\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z)\in\mathcal{C}.

Notations 2.4.5

(Closed-immersion pullback maps)
.

For a closed immersion
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
in
\Sm_{S}
and an integer
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, define

i_{m}^{*}:=\begin{cases}\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(i)\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(X),\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z)\bigr)_{0},&\text{if }i\neq\operatorname{id}_{X},\\[5.69054pt]
\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)},&\text{if }i=\operatorname{id}_{X}.\end{cases}

When
m=0
, we simply write

i^{*}:=i_{0}^{*}.

Remark 2.4.6

(Identity immersion)
.

For a nonidentity closed immersion, the notation
i_{m}^{*}
is exactly the degree-
m
closed-immersion
pullback produced by Definition
2.4.4
in simplicial degree
1
.
For the identity immersion, we impose the strict convention

(\operatorname{id}_{X})_{m}^{*}:=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

This agrees a posteriori with the explicit degree-
1
higher Gysin construction up to canonical homotopy
by degeneracy compatibility.

Remark 2.4.7

(Why face-compatibility is non-formal)
.

Although
\sigma\mapsto D(\sigma)
is compatible with degeneracies via parameter confluences
(Lemma
1.5.1
), restricting
D(\sigma)
to a coordinate panel

\{t_{k}=0\}
does
not
recover the simplicial face
d_{k}\sigma
of the nerve in general.
Rather, by Lemma
1.3.7
it produces the deformation space
of the
specialized flag

{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)
, which involves normal bundles.

Consequently, the simplicial identities for the degree-
m
higher Gysin construction require combining:
(i) these geometric comparison isomorphisms, and
(ii)
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance / vector bundle equivalences in
\mathcal{C}
to transport along the normal-bundle replacements.

2.4.8
Transition to simplicial identities.

The preceding construction assigns to every simplex

\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}

a higher Gysin simplex

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\sigma).

To obtain a simplicial functor on
N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
, it remains to compare the faces and degeneracies of

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\sigma)
with the simplices attached to the geometric faces
d_{k}\sigma
and degeneracies
s_{k}\sigma
.
This is the purpose of the next section.

3.
Simplicial identities for higher Gysin simplices

The goal of this section is to verify the simplicial identities satisfied by the higher Gysin simplices constructed in Section
2
.

More precisely, we compare the simplicial faces and degeneracies of

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\sigma)

with the simplices attached to the geometric faces and degeneracies of
\sigma
.
The resulting compatibilities are canonical only up to contractible choice; the rigidification of those choices is postponed to Section
4
.

3.1.
Face compatibilities

3.1.1.
Overview and main statements

3.1.2
Roadmap for face relations.

Fix

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Our goal is to compare the simplicial faces

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)

with the simplices attached to the geometric faces
d_{k}\sigma
.

There are three regimes:

(1)

k=0
(initial face): restriction to the chart
t_{0}\neq 0
identifies
D(\sigma)
with

D(d_{0}\sigma)\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and the extra boundary step cancels by inert
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-cancellation.

(2)

1\leq k\leq n-1
(internal faces): the
k
-panel cube, identified geometrically with the specialization
{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)
; one then collapses the
t_{k}
-direction and compares with
d_{k}\sigma
.

(3)

k=n
(terminal face): the last panel
{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)
is a vector-bundle pullback of
d_{n}\sigma
,
so
\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{n}\sigma)
is recovered from
\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))
by the bundle projection.

The proofs separate into:
(i) a fiber-category lemma comparing faces of
{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}
with panel restriction of cubes,
(ii) geometric identifications of the panel faces, and
(iii) collapse/Koszul bookkeeping for the precomposition map
\beta_{\sigma,m}
.

Remark 3.1.3
.

In this subsection, whenever a homotopy is obtained by filling a spine (or, more generally, by a lifting property
against an inner anodyne inclusion), it is canonical only up to contractible choice
(Convention
2.1.3
).
We reserve the term
specified
for simplices that are part of the structural data of the weak
Borel-Moore theory.

3.1.4.
Category input: faces of
{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}
from cube panel restriction

Remark 3.1.5

(Fibers commute with panel restriction)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a pointed
\infty
-category admitting finite limits, let

[n-1]=\{0,\dots,n-1\}
, and let

\mathcal{K}:\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

be an
n
-cube.
Fix
r\in[n-1]
, and let

\iota^{(r)}:\mathcal{P}([n-1]\setminus\{r\})^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}},\qquad L\longmapsto L\cup\{r\},

so that

\mathcal{K}^{(r)}:=\mathcal{K}\circ\iota^{(r)}

is the
r
-panel cube.

For
i\neq r
, objectwise fiber in the
i
-direction commutes with restriction along
\iota^{(r)}
:
there is a canonical equivalence of cubes

\operatorname{Fib}_{i}(\mathcal{K})^{(r)}\ \simeq\ \operatorname{Fib}_{i}(\mathcal{K}^{(r)}).

Iterating over all
i\in[n-1]\setminus\{r\}
in the induced order yields a canonical equivalence

\TotFib(\mathcal{K}^{(r)})\ \simeq\ \TotFib_{\{r+1,\dots,n-1\}}\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{<r}^{(r)}\bigr),

where

\mathcal{K}_{<r}:=\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\mathcal{K}).

Lemma 3.1.6

(Faces of
{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}
and panel restriction of cubes)
.

Let
n\geq 2
. Let
\mathcal{C}
be a pointed
\infty
-category admitting finite limits, let

\mathcal{K}:\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}

be an
n
-cube, and fix
r\in[n-1]
.
Write

\mathcal{K}_{<r}:=\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\mathcal{K}),

and let
\mathcal{K}_{<r}^{\emptyset}
and
\mathcal{K}_{<r}^{(r)}
denote the two

(n-r-1)
-faces obtained by restricting to subsets not containing
r
and containing
r
, respectively.

Applying
\TotFib
in the remaining directions
\{r+1,\dots,n-1\}
yields a canonical morphism

f_{r}:\TotFib(\mathcal{K}_{<r}^{(r)})\longrightarrow\TotFib(\mathcal{K}_{<r}^{\emptyset}).

The defining fiber step in direction
r
gives a canonical fiber sequence

\TotFib(\mathcal{K})\xrightarrow{\,\kappa_{r}\,}\TotFib(\mathcal{K}_{<r}^{(r)})\xrightarrow{\,f_{r}\,}\TotFib(\mathcal{K}_{<r}^{\emptyset}).

Using Remark
3.1.5
, we view
\kappa_{r}
as a morphism

\kappa_{r}:\TotFib(\mathcal{K})\longrightarrow\TotFib(\mathcal{K}^{(r)}).

Let

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}),\mathcal{K}([n-1])\bigr)_{n-1}

be the total boundary simplex produced from
\mathcal{K}
by the spine-filling construction of
Construction
2.3.2
, and similarly for
\mathcal{K}^{(r)}
.
Then the two
(n-2)
-simplices

d_{r}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})\bigr),\qquad{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}\bigl(\mathcal{K}^{(r)}\bigr)\circ\kappa_{r}

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}),\mathcal{K}([n-1])\bigr)

have the same restriction to the spine
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n-2}\subset\Delta^{n-2}
.
Hence they are canonically identified in the sense of
Convention
2.1.3
.

In particular,

d_{r}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})\bigr)\;\simeq\;{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}\bigl(\mathcal{K}^{(r)}\bigr)\circ\kappa_{r}

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}),\mathcal{K}([n-1])\bigr)_{n-2}.

Proof.

Set

M:=\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}),\mathcal{K}([n-1])\bigr),

which is a Kan complex.

By Construction
2.3.2
,
{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})
is obtained by choosing a filler of the spine

{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n-1}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n-1}

determined by the iterated fiber tower for
\mathcal{K}
. Applying the face map
d_{r}
amounts to restricting
this chosen filler along the coface

\delta^{r}:\Delta^{n-2}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n-1}.

On the level of spine data, this removes the
r
-th stage of the tower.

On the other hand, the
r
-panel cube
\mathcal{K}^{(r)}
carries the tower obtained by first restricting to
the face containing
r
, and then taking iterated fibers in the remaining directions. The canonical map

\kappa_{r}
precisely identifies the full tower with the panel tower after deleting the
r
-th stage. Therefore the two

(n-2)
-simplices

d_{r}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})\bigr)\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}\bigl(\mathcal{K}^{(r)}\bigr)\circ\kappa_{r}

induce the same map

{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n-2}\longrightarrow M.

Now consider the restriction map

\rho:\Map(\Delta^{n-2},M)\longrightarrow\Map({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n-2},M).

Since
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n-2}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n-2}
is inner anodyne and
M
is Kan,

\rho
is a trivial Kan fibration. Hence its fibers are contractible Kan complexes.
It follows that the two fillers above are canonically identified relative to their common spine restriction,
which is exactly the claimed homotopy

d_{r}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K})\bigr)\;\simeq\;{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}\bigl(\mathcal{K}^{(r)}\bigr)\circ\kappa_{r}.

∎

Notations 3.1.7
.

When the ambient cube is

\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n),

we write

\kappa_{r,\sigma,m}:=\kappa_{r}.

If the integer
m
is fixed in the discussion, we further abbreviate

\kappa_{r,\sigma}:=\kappa_{r,\sigma,m}.

3.1.8.
Geometric input: identify panel faces and twists

3.1.9
Panel faces of the deformation-space localization cube.

Fix

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad r\in[n-1].

The closed subscheme

H_{r}(\sigma)=p_{\sigma}^{-1}(t_{r}=0)\subset D(\sigma)

inherits an ordered SNC divisor by intersecting the remaining boundary components with
H_{r}(\sigma)
, namely

H_{\bullet}(\sigma)^{\widehat{r}}=\bigl(H_{0}(\sigma)\cap H_{r}(\sigma),\ \dots,\ H_{r-1}(\sigma)\cap H_{r}(\sigma),\ H_{r+1}(\sigma)\cap H_{r}(\sigma),\ \dots,\ H_{n-1}(\sigma)\cap H_{r}(\sigma)\bigr),

ordered as written. We denote the resulting ordered SNC pair by

\bigl(H_{r}(\sigma),\,H_{\bullet}(\sigma)^{\widehat{r}}\bigr).

The restricted coordinate functions

(t_{j}|_{H_{r}(\sigma)})_{j\neq r},

listed in the induced order obtained by deleting
r
, equip this ordered SNC pair with the
structure of an equation-equipped ordered SNC pair of length
n-1
.

As before, we identify the ordered set
[n-1]\setminus\{r\}
with
[n-2]
via the unique order-preserving bijection.

Lemma 3.1.10

(Geometric identification of panel faces)
.

For each
r\in[n-1]
, the isomorphism

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma}:H_{r}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))

of Lemma
1.3.7
refines to an isomorphism of
equation-equipped ordered SNC pairs:

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma}:\bigl(H_{r}(\sigma),\,H_{\bullet}(\sigma)^{\widehat{r}},\,(t_{j}|_{H_{r}(\sigma)})_{j\neq r}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\bigl(D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma)),\,H_{\bullet}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma)),\,\text{canonical coordinate equations}\bigr).

Proof.

By Lemma
1.3.7
, the morphism

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma}:H_{r}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\sim}D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))

is an isomorphism over the coordinate hyperplane
\{t_{r}=0\}\simeq\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}
.
For each
j\neq r
, it identifies the divisor

H_{j}(\sigma)\cap H_{r}(\sigma)\subset H_{r}(\sigma)

with the corresponding boundary divisor of
D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))
, compatibly with the order-preserving
reindexing obtained by deleting
r
.

Because the identification is over the hyperplane
\{t_{r}=0\}
, the coordinate function defining the
corresponding boundary divisor on
D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))
pulls back exactly to the restricted coordinate
function
t_{j}|_{H_{r}(\sigma)}
.
Hence
\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma}
preserves not only the ordered SNC strata but also their chosen
defining equations.
This is precisely the required statement.
∎

Lemma 3.1.11

(Identification of the panel localization cube)
.

Fix

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad r\in[n-1],\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Under the isomorphism of ordered SNC pairs of Lemma
3.1.10
, the

r
-panel of the localization cube on
D(\sigma)
identifies canonically with the localization cube
of the specialization
{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma)
:

\mathcal{K}_{D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma)),H_{\bullet}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))}(m+n-1)\;\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\;\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n)\bigr)^{(r)}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.1.10
, the isomorphism

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma}:H_{r}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))

is an isomorphism of equation-equipped ordered SNC pairs.
Hence Axiom
(wBM7)
(d) yields a specified isomorphism of cubes

\mathcal{K}_{D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma)),H_{\bullet}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))}(m+n-1)\;\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\;\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n)\bigr)^{(r)}.

This is exactly the claimed identification.
∎

Lemma 3.1.12

(Panel faces of the coherent boundary simplex)
.

Let
n\geq 2
.
Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad r\in[n-1],\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Set

\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}:=\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n).

Let

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma}:H_{r}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))

be the isomorphism of ordered SNC pairs from
Lemma
3.1.10
. Let

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma,\operatorname{deep}}:H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))

and

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma,U}:H_{r}(\sigma)\setminus\bigcup_{j\neq r}H_{j}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma)}

be the induced isomorphisms on the deepest stratum and on the open complement.

Then there is a canonical identification, in the sense of
Convention
2.1.3
,

d_{r}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)\ \simeq\ \bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma,\operatorname{deep}}\bigr)^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\kappa_{r,\sigma,m}

as simplices in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}),\ \mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr)_{n-2}.

Here

\kappa_{r,\sigma,m}:\TotFib(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m})\longrightarrow\TotFib\!\Bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)^{(r)}\Bigr)

is the quotient map from Lemma
3.1.6
.

Proof.

Apply Lemma
3.1.6
to the
n
-cube

\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}=\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n).

This gives a canonical identification

d_{r}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m})\bigr)\ \simeq\ {\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}\!\Bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)^{(r)}\Bigr)\circ\kappa_{r,\sigma,m}

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\TotFib(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}),\,\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}([n-1])\bigr)_{n-2}.

By Lemma
3.1.11
, the panel cube

\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)^{(r)}

identifies canonically with

\mathcal{K}_{D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma)),H_{\bullet}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))}(m+n-1).

By Lemma
2.3.6
, transport along the isomorphism

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma}:H_{r}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\sim}D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma))

identifies the corresponding total boundary simplices:

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}\!\Bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)^{(r)}\Bigr)\ \simeq\ \bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma,\operatorname{deep}}\bigr)^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{r}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{r,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}.

Composing these two identifications yields the claimed formula.
∎

Lemma 3.1.13

(Deepest-stratum transport for the specialized face)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Set

N_{k,k+1}:=N_{Z_{k}/Z_{k+1}},

and let

\pi_{0}:N_{k,k+1}|_{Z_{0}}\longrightarrow Z_{0}

be the bundle projection.

Under the identification

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma}:H_{k}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))

of Lemma
3.1.10
, the induced map on deepest strata is an isomorphism

\theta_{k,\sigma}:=\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,\operatorname{deep}}:H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H_{[n-1]}(\sigma).

Moreover, the deepest-stratum projections fit into a commutative diagram of smooth
S
-schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{k,k+1}|_{Z_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{0}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{k,\sigma}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\sigma}}

Consequently, the induced morphisms in
\mathcal{C}
satisfy a canonical homotopy

(\pi_{\sigma})^{*}\ \simeq\ \big(\theta_{k,\sigma}^{*}\big)^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})^{*}\circ(\pi_{0})^{*}

in the mapping space

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0}),\mathcal{G}_{m}(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma))\bigr).

Using the chosen inverse data for vector bundle projections, there is also a canonical homotopy

(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ\theta_{k,\sigma}^{*}

in the mapping space

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma),\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr).

Proof.

The commutative diagram of smooth
S
-schemes is exactly the deepest-stratum compatibility from
Lemma
1.4.15
, rewritten using the notation

N_{k,k+1}=N_{Z_{k}/Z_{k+1}}.

By smooth contravariance and its functoriality, the equality of composites

\pi_{\sigma}\circ\theta_{k,\sigma}=\pi_{0}\circ\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}

induces a canonical homotopy

\theta_{k,\sigma}^{*}\circ(\pi_{\sigma})^{*}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})^{*}\circ(\pi_{0})^{*}

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)))\bigr).

Whiskering on the left by
\big((\theta_{k,\sigma})^{*}\big)^{-1}
yields

(\pi_{\sigma})^{*}\ \simeq\ \big(\theta_{k,\sigma}^{*}\big)^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})^{*}\circ(\pi_{0})^{*}

Since
\pi_{\sigma}
,
\pi_{0}
, and
\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}
are vector bundle projections,
Axiom
(wBM9)
(a) provides chosen inverse data for their pullbacks,
compatible with composition. The map
\theta_{k,\sigma}^{*}
is an equivalence because

\theta_{k,\sigma}
is an isomorphism. Inverting the displayed homotopy using these chosen
inverse data yields

(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ\theta_{k,\sigma}^{*}

∎

Remark 3.1.14

(Boundary map on the one-parameter slice)
.

Let
V\subset D(\sigma)
be the one-parameter slice from
Lemma
1.4.5
, with principal smooth divisor

V_{0}=V(t)\hookrightarrow V

and open complement
U_{\sigma}
.
For every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, localization yields a connecting morphism

\partial_{V_{0}\subset V}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}(V_{0})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-1].

Under the canonical identification of closed/open pairs from
Lemma
1.4.5
, this is the boundary map for the corresponding
one-parameter deformation space. We will use this geometric identification later when analyzing
the
t_{k}
-collapse.

3.1.15.
Collapse calculus and precomposition transport

3.2.
Permuting coordinates before collapse

Notations 3.2.1

(Forgetting and moving a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-coordinate)
.

Let
X\in\Sm_{S}
, let
n\geq 1
, and let
0\leq k\leq n-1
.
Write

\operatorname{pr}_{k}^{X}:X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\longrightarrow X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}

for the projection forgetting the
k
-th
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factor.

Let

\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X}:X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\longrightarrow X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}

be the permutation moving the
k
-th
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factor to the last position and preserving the relative order
of the other factors. Then

\operatorname{pr}_{k}^{X}=\operatorname{pr}_{n-1}^{X}\circ\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X}.

Notations 3.2.2

(Iterated Koszul automorphisms)
.

For
X\in\Sm_{S}
,
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, and
\ell\geq 0
, set

\epsilon_{X,m}^{\,0}:=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)},\qquad\epsilon_{X,m}^{\,\ell+1}:=\epsilon_{X,m}^{\,\ell}\circ\epsilon_{X,m},

where
\epsilon_{X,m}
is the automorphism of Axiom
(wBM13)
.

Notations 3.2.3

(Collapse after moving a coordinate to the last position)
.

Let
X\in\Sm_{S}
,
n\geq 1
,
0\leq k\leq n-1
, and
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.
Define

\beta^{(n)}_{X,m;k}:=(\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X})^{*}[-n]\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\bigl(X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\bigr)[-n].

Lemma 3.2.4

(Moving a coordinate to the last position before collapse)
.

Let
X\in\Sm_{S}
,
m\in\mathbf{Z}
,
n\geq 1
, and
0\leq k\leq n-1
.
Then there is a canonical homotopy

\beta^{(n)}_{X,m;k}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\circ\epsilon_{X,m}^{\,n-1-k}.

Proof.

Write
\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X}
as the composite of the adjacent transpositions

s_{n-2}\circ\cdots\circ s_{k}.

By smooth functoriality for isomorphisms,

(\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X})^{*}\simeq(s_{k})^{*}\circ\cdots\circ(s_{n-2})^{*}.

Applying Axiom
(wBM13)
successively to the adjacent transpositions

s_{k},\dots,s_{n-2}
yields

(\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X})^{*}[-n]\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\circ\epsilon_{X,m}^{\,n-1-k},

as claimed.
∎

Notations 3.2.5

(Boundary map in the
k
-th
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-direction)
.

Let
X\in\Sm_{S}
,
m\in\mathbf{Z}
,
n\geq 1
, and
0\leq k\leq n-1
.
Write

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\bigl(X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\bigr)[-n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}\bigl(X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}\bigr)[-n+1]

for the last-factor boundary map of Axiom
(wBM12)
.

Let

\iota_{k\leftarrow n-1}^{X}:X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\longrightarrow X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}

be the permutation moving the last
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factor to the
k
-th position and preserving the relative order
of the other factors; equivalently,

\iota_{k\leftarrow n-1}^{X}=(\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X})^{-1}.

We define the boundary map in the
k
-th direction by

\delta_{t_{k}}:=\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ(\iota_{k\leftarrow n-1}^{X})^{*}[-n].

Lemma 3.2.6

(Cancellation in the
k
-th
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-direction)
.

Let
X\in\Sm_{S}
,
m\in\mathbf{Z}
,
n\geq 1
, and
0\leq k\leq n-1
.
Then there is a canonical homotopy

\delta_{t_{k}}\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m;k}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(n-1)}_{X,m}

as morphisms

\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}\bigl(X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}\bigr)[-n+1].

Proof.

By Notation
3.2.5
and Notation
3.2.3
,

\delta_{t_{k}}\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m;k}=\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ(\iota_{k\leftarrow n-1}^{X})^{*}[-n]\circ(\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X})^{*}[-n]\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}.

Since
\tau_{k\to n-1}^{X}\circ\iota_{k\leftarrow n-1}^{X}=\operatorname{id}
, functoriality of smooth pullback for
isomorphisms identifies the middle composite with the identity. Hence

\delta_{t_{k}}\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m;k}\ \simeq\ \delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}.

The right-hand side is canonically homotopic to
\beta^{(n-1)}_{X,m}
by
Axiom
(wBM12)
.
∎

Corollary 3.2.7

(Cancellation after moving the
k
-th factor)
.

Let
X\in\Sm_{S}
,
m\in\mathbf{Z}
,
n\geq 1
, and
0\leq k\leq n-1
.
There is a canonical homotopy

\delta_{t_{k}}\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\circ\epsilon_{X,m}^{\,n-1-k}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(n-1)}_{X,m}.

Proof.

Combine Lemma
3.2.4
with Lemma
3.2.6
.
∎

Lemma 3.2.8

(Closed immersions commute with inert collapse)
.

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a closed immersion in
\Sm_{S}
, let
r\geq 0
, and let
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.
Write

i^{(r)}:=i\times\operatorname{id}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}}:Z\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}\hookrightarrow X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}.

Then there is a canonical homotopy

\beta^{(r)}_{Z,m}\circ i_{m}^{*}\ \simeq\ (i^{(r)})_{m+r}^{*}[-r]\circ\beta^{(r)}_{X,m}

as morphisms

\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+r}\bigl(Z\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}\bigr)[-r].

Proof.

The case
r=0
is tautological.

Assume
r\geq 1
. Let

N:=N_{Z/X},\qquad\pi:N\to Z

be the normal bundle and its projection. Write

\delta_{i}:\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(U_{i})[-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(N)

for the localization boundary map of the closed immersion

N\hookrightarrow D(i),

and

\varphi_{i}:U_{i}\xrightarrow{\sim}X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}

for the standard open-stratum identification. By Definition
2.4.4
,

i_{m}^{*}=(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{i}\circ(\varphi_{i})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}.

By base change of deformation spaces along
(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}\to S
, there is a canonical identification

D(i^{(r)})\simeq D(i)\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}.

Under this identification, the deepest stratum is

N\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r},

with projection

\pi^{(r)}:=\pi\times\operatorname{id}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}}:N\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}\to Z\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r},

and the open stratum is identified with

U_{i}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}\xrightarrow{\ \varphi_{i}\times\operatorname{id}\ }X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}.

In this presentation, the deformation coordinate is the
0
-th
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factor.

For the right-hand side, transporting the definition of
(i^{(r)})_{m+r}^{*}
along the above
identification of
D(i^{(r)})
gives

(i^{(r)})_{m+r}^{*}[-r]\circ\beta^{(r)}_{X,m}\ \simeq\ (\pi^{(r),*})^{-1}[-r]\circ\big(\delta_{t_{0}}\circ\beta^{(r+1)}_{X,m;0}\big).

Indeed, one first collapses all
r+1

\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factors, with the deformation coordinate in position
0
,
and then takes the boundary in that deformation coordinate.

On the left-hand side, Axiom
(wBM11)
for the smooth morphism
\pi
gives

(\pi^{(r)})^{*}[-r]\circ\beta^{(r)}_{Z,m}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(r)}_{N,m}\circ\pi^{*}.

Since
\pi^{*}
and
(\pi^{(r)})^{*}
are equivalences (vector bundle projections), contractibility of
inverse data yields
