\beta^{(r)}_{N,m}\circ(\pi^{*})^{-1}\ \simeq\ (\pi^{(r),*})^{-1}[-r]\circ\beta^{(r)}_{Z,m}.

Substituting this into the formula for
i_{m}^{*}
, and transporting along the canonical
identification

D(i^{(r)})\simeq D(i)\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r},

we obtain

\beta^{(r)}_{Z,m}\circ i_{m}^{*}\ \simeq\ (\pi^{(r),*})^{-1}[-r]\circ\big(\delta_{t_{0}}\circ\beta^{(r+1)}_{X,m;0}\big).

Now Lemma
3.2.6
with
n=r+1
and
k=0
gives a specified homotopy

\delta_{t_{0}}\circ\beta^{(r+1)}_{X,m;0}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(r)}_{X,m}.

Hence both
(1)
and
(2)
are homotopic to

(\pi^{(r),*})^{-1}[-r]\circ\beta^{(r)}_{X,m},

which proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.2.9

(Postcomposition transport:
\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}
is the collapse)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Set

N_{k,n}:=N_{Z_{k}/Z_{n}},\qquad p_{k,n}:N_{k,n}\to Z_{k},\qquad i_{k,n}:Z_{k}\hookrightarrow Z_{n}.

Let

\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U}:U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H_{k}(\sigma)\setminus\bigcup_{j\neq k}H_{j}(\sigma)

be the isomorphism on open strata induced by
\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma}
.

Then there is a canonical homotopy

\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ \beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ(p_{k,n})^{*}\circ(i_{k,n})_{m}^{*}

as morphisms

\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}\bigl(U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}\bigr)[1-n].

Proof.

Write

V:=D(\sigma)\setminus\bigcup_{j\neq k}H_{j}(\sigma).

Then

H_{k}(\sigma)\cap V=H_{k}(\sigma)\setminus\bigcup_{j\neq k}H_{j}(\sigma),

and

H_{k}(\sigma)\cap V\hookrightarrow V

is the principal smooth divisor cut out by
t_{k}
, with open complement
U_{\sigma}
.

Let

i_{k,n}^{(n-1)}:=i_{k,n}\times\operatorname{id}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}},\qquad p_{k,n}^{(n-1)}:=p_{k,n}\times\operatorname{id}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}}.

Then

\displaystyle\bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U}\bigr)^{*})^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

\displaystyle\simeq\delta_{t_{k}}[1-n]\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

by (
3.2.9.a
)

\displaystyle\simeq(\varphi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})^{*}[1-n]\circ(p_{k,n}^{(n-1)})^{*}[1-n]

\displaystyle\qquad\circ\,(i_{k,n}^{(n-1)})^{*}_{m+n-1}[1-n]\circ\beta^{(n-1)}_{Z_{n},m}

by (
3.2.9.b
)

\displaystyle\simeq(\varphi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})^{*}[1-n]\circ(p_{k,n}^{(n-1)})^{*}[1-n]\circ\beta^{(n-1)}_{Z_{k},m}\circ(i_{k,n})_{m}^{*}

by Lemma
3.2.8

\displaystyle\simeq(\varphi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})^{*}[1-n]\circ\beta^{(n-1)}_{N_{k,n},m}\circ(p_{k,n})^{*}\circ(i_{k,n})_{m}^{*}

by Axiom
(wBM11)

\displaystyle=\beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ(p_{k,n})^{*}\circ(i_{k,n})_{m}^{*}.

It remains to justify the two auxiliary homotopies

(3.2.9.a)

\bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U}\bigr)^{*})^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ \delta_{t_{k}}[1-n]

and

(3.2.9.b)

\delta_{t_{k}}[1-n]\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ (\varphi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})^{*}[1-n]\circ(p_{k,n}^{(n-1)})^{*}[1-n]\circ(i_{k,n}^{(n-1)})^{*}_{m+n-1}[1-n]\circ\beta^{(n-1)}_{Z_{n},m}

For (
3.2.9.a
), apply Lemma
2.3.3

successively in all directions
j\neq k
, leaving the
k
-direction untouched.
This identifies

\TotFib\!\Bigl(\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n)\bigr)^{(k)}\Bigr)

with

\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}\bigl(H_{k}(\sigma)\cap V\bigr)[1-n],

and identifies

\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n)\bigr)

with

\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n].

Under these identifications, the quotient map

\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}

is exactly the connecting morphism

\delta_{t_{k}}[1-n]:\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}\bigl(H_{k}(\sigma)\cap V\bigr)[1-n]

for the localization triangle of the principal smooth divisor

H_{k}(\sigma)\cap V\hookrightarrow V.

Since
\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U}
identifies
U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}
with

H_{k}(\sigma)\cap V
, this gives (
3.2.9.a
).

For (
3.2.9.b
), Lemma
1.4.5

identifies the pair

\bigl(V,\ H_{k}(\sigma)\cap V\bigr)

with

\bigl(D(i_{k,n}),\,N_{k,n}\bigr)\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1},

compatibly with open complements and with the standard identification

U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}\simeq N_{k,n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}.

Under this identification,
\delta_{t_{k}}
is exactly the localization boundary morphism in the
deformation coordinate for the length-
1
simplex

i_{k,n}^{(n-1)}:Z_{k}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}\hookrightarrow Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}.

Therefore, by Definition
2.4.4
applied to this length-
1
simplex, the composite

\delta_{t_{k}}[1-n]\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

is precisely the right-hand side of (
3.2.9.b
).
This proves the lemma.
∎

3.2.10
Shifts of morphisms.

For a morphism
f:A\to B
in the pointed
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
and an integer
r\geq 0
,
we write

f[-r]:A[-r]\to B[-r]

for its image under the
r
-fold desuspension functor of
Notation
2.2.1
.
In particular, composites involving

\beta^{(r)}_{X,m}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+r}\bigl(X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}\bigr)[-r]

are understood to use the suspended version of the preceding morphism whenever necessary.

Definition 3.2.11

(Relative graph pullback for a morphism over a base)
.

Let
B\in\Sm_{S}
, let

f:Y\to X

be a morphism in
\Sm_{B}
, and let
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Write

\Gamma_{f}:Y\hookrightarrow Y\times_{B}X

for the graph of
f
, and

\operatorname{pr}_{2}:Y\times_{B}X\to X

for the second projection. Since
X\to B
is separated,
\Gamma_{f}
is a closed immersion; since

\operatorname{pr}_{1}:Y\times_{B}X\to Y
is smooth and
\Gamma_{f}
is a section of
\operatorname{pr}_{1}
, it is in fact a regular immersion.

Define the
relative graph pullback in degree
m
by

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}:=(\Gamma_{f})_{m}^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2})^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y).

Definition 3.2.12

(The graph pullback
(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}
)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Let

\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}}:H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\longrightarrow H_{[n-2]}(d_{k}\sigma)

be the morphism from Lemma
1.4.16
. Since both source and target are smooth over
Z_{0}
,
we define

(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}

to be its relative graph pullback over the base
Z_{0}
, in the sense of
Definition
3.2.11
.

Lemma 3.2.13

(Naturality of connecting morphisms in a pointed
\infty
-category with finite colimits)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a pointed
\infty
-category admitting finite colimits. Consider a commutative diagram
in
\mathcal{C}

\textstyle{C[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{c[-1]}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{C^{\prime}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\prime}}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u^{\prime}}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v^{\prime}}
\textstyle{C^{\prime}}

whose rows are fiber sequences.
Then the rightmost square commutes up to a canonical homotopy

a\circ\partial\ \simeq\ \partial^{\prime}\circ c[-1]\qquad\text{in}\quad\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(C,A^{\prime}[-1]).

Moreover, the space of such homotopies is contractible.

Proof.

In a pointed
\infty
-category admitting finite colimits, fibers define a functor

\operatorname{Fib}:\Fun(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C},\qquad(A\to B)\longmapsto B\sqcup_{A}0.

Applying
\operatorname{Fib}
to the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{\prime}}

yields the induced map

\operatorname{Fib}(u)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Fib}(u^{\prime}).

Since the two rows are fiber sequences, we may identify

A\simeq\operatorname{Fib}(u),\qquad A^{\prime}\simeq\operatorname{Fib}(u^{\prime}).

The connecting morphism

\partial:C[-1]\to A

is the canonical map from the fiber of
u
to the desuspension

C[-1]=\operatorname{Fib}(C\to 0),

and similarly for
\partial^{\prime}
. These maps are functorial in morphisms of arrows, applied to the
previous commutative square
and the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

It follows that the rightmost square commutes up to a canonical homotopy.

The contractible uniqueness statement follows because the relevant space of fillers is the fiber of a
restriction map between mapping spaces induced by a pushout square, hence a contractible Kan complex.
∎

Lemma 3.2.14

(Smooth base change for degree-
m

n=1
Gysin maps)
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

be a cartesian square in
\Sm_{S}
with
i
and
i^{\prime}
closed immersions and
g
smooth.
Let
m\in\mathbf{Z}
. Then there is a canonical homotopy

(i^{\prime})_{m}^{*}\circ g^{*}\ \simeq\ (g^{\prime})^{*}\circ i_{m}^{*}

in the mapping space

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(X),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z^{\prime})\bigr).

Proof.

Let

N:=N_{Z/X},\qquad N^{\prime}:=N_{Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}},\qquad\pi:N\to Z,\qquad\pi^{\prime}:N^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime}

be the normal bundles and their projections.
Let

D(i)\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S},\qquad D(i^{\prime})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}

be the deformation spaces, with open strata

U:=D(i)\setminus N,\qquad U^{\prime}:=D(i^{\prime})\setminus N^{\prime}.

Since
g
is smooth, the defining square is Tor-independent, and
Lemma
1.6.1
gives a canonical identification

D(i^{\prime})\ \simeq\ D(i)\times_{X}X^{\prime}

over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}
, compatible with the special fibers

N^{\prime}\simeq N\times_{Z}Z^{\prime}

and with the open-stratum identifications

\varphi_{i}:U\xrightarrow{\sim}X\times\mathbb{G}_{m},\qquad\varphi_{i^{\prime}}:U^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}X^{\prime}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}.

In particular, the induced map
U^{\prime}\to U
identifies with

g\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}:X^{\prime}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X\times\mathbb{G}_{m},

and the induced map
N^{\prime}\to N
is the smooth morphism

g_{N}:N^{\prime}\to N.

Write

\delta_{i}:\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(U)[-1]\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(N),\qquad\delta_{i^{\prime}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(U^{\prime})[-1]\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(N^{\prime})

for the localization boundary maps of the pairs
(D(i),N)
and
(D(i^{\prime}),N^{\prime})
.

Assume for the moment the three auxiliary homotopies

(3.2.14.a)

(g_{N})^{*}\circ\delta_{i}\ \simeq\ \delta_{i^{\prime}}\circ(g\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{*}[-1],

(3.2.14.b)

\beta^{(1)}_{X^{\prime},m}\circ g^{*}\ \simeq\ (g\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m},

and

(3.2.14.c)

(\pi^{\prime*})^{-1}\circ(g_{N})^{*}\ \simeq\ (g^{\prime})^{*}\circ(\pi^{*})^{-1}.

Then, using Definition
2.4.4
for the length-
1
simplices
i
and
i^{\prime}
, we compute

\displaystyle(i^{\prime})_{m}^{*}\circ g^{*}

\displaystyle=(\pi^{\prime*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{i^{\prime}}\circ(\varphi_{i^{\prime}})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X^{\prime},m}\circ g^{*}

by Definition
2.4.4

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi^{\prime*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{i^{\prime}}\circ(\varphi_{i^{\prime}})^{*}[-1]\circ(g\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}

by (
3.2.14.b
)

\displaystyle=(\pi^{\prime*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{i^{\prime}}\circ(g\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{*}[-1]\circ(\varphi_{i})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}

by functoriality of the open identifications

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi^{\prime*})^{-1}\circ(g_{N})^{*}\circ\delta_{i}\circ(\varphi_{i})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}

by (
3.2.14.a
)

\displaystyle\simeq(g^{\prime})^{*}\circ(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{i}\circ(\varphi_{i})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}

by (
3.2.14.c
)

\displaystyle=(g^{\prime})^{*}\circ i_{m}^{*}

\displaystyle\text{by Definition\penalty\ \ref{def:closed-pullback-simplex}}.

It remains to justify
(
3.2.14.a
),
(
3.2.14.b
), and
(
3.2.14.c
).

For (
3.2.14.a
), functoriality of localization gives a morphism of fiber sequences
associated with the morphism of pairs

(D(i^{\prime}),N^{\prime})\longrightarrow(D(i),N).

Lemma
3.2.13
therefore yields the canonical homotopy

(g_{N})^{*}\circ\delta_{i}\ \simeq\ \delta_{i^{\prime}}\circ(g\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{*}[-1].

The homotopy (
3.2.14.b
) is exactly smooth naturality of the collapse map in degree
m
,
applied to the smooth morphism
g:X^{\prime}\to X
.

For (
3.2.14.c
), the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}

is cartesian, with
g^{\prime}
smooth, since it is obtained from the original square by smooth base change.
Hence Axiom
(wBM9)
(b) gives the canonical homotopy

(\pi^{\prime*})^{-1}\circ(g_{N})^{*}\ \simeq\ (g^{\prime})^{*}\circ(\pi^{*})^{-1}.

This proves the lemma.
∎

Remark 3.2.15
.

Although Lemma
3.2.14
is not needed for the internal face comparisons in this section,
it shows that the explicitly constructed degree-
m

n=1
Gysin morphisms satisfy the expected smooth
Beck-Chevalley property. This is useful both for later applications and as a consistency check on the construction.

Lemma 3.2.16

(Closed pullback naturality of collapse maps for divisor immersions)
.

Let

g:X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X

be a principal smooth divisor immersion in
\Sm_{S}
in the sense of
Definition
2.2.2
, let
r\geq 0
, and let
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.
Write

g^{(r)}:=g\times\operatorname{id}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}}:X^{\prime}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}\hookrightarrow X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}.

Then there is a specified homotopy

\beta^{(r)}_{X,m}\circ g_{*}\ \simeq\ g^{(r)}_{*}[-r]\circ\beta^{(r)}_{X^{\prime},m-1}

in the mapping space

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(X^{\prime}),\ \mathcal{G}_{m+r}\bigl(X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{r}\bigr)[-r]\Bigr).

Proof.

Apply Axiom
(wBM14)
to the principal smooth divisor immersion

g:X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X

and to the integer
r
. The middle square of the resulting morphism of fiber sequences is exactly the
displayed homotopy.
∎

Lemma 3.2.17

(Tor-independence for base change of sections of smooth morphisms)
.

Let
\pi:P\to X
be a smooth morphism and let
s:X\hookrightarrow P
be a section (hence a regular immersion).
For any morphism
g:X^{\prime}\to X
, form the cartesian square
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\prime}:=P\times_{X}X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

Then the square is Tor-independent.

Proof.

Tor-independence is local on
P
for the étale topology, so it is enough to check the claim étale-locally
in a neighbourhood of the image
s(X)\subset P
.

Since
\pi:P\to X
is smooth and
s
is a section, étale-locally on
P
around every point of
s(X)

the pair
(P,s)
is isomorphic over
X
to the projection with its zero section:

(X\times\mathbb{A}^{d}\to X,\ X\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{d}).

After base change along
g:X^{\prime}\to X
, the same is true for

(P^{\prime},s^{\prime})=(P\times_{X}X^{\prime},\,X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow P\times_{X}X^{\prime}).

Thus étale-locally the cartesian square of the statement becomes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times\mathbb{A}^{d},}

where the horizontal arrows are zero sections. The zero section is cut out by the regular sequence

x_{1},\dots,x_{d},

so its Koszul complex resolves the structure sheaf of the section and remains exact after arbitrary base change.
Hence all higher Tor groups vanish.

Since this vanishing is étale-local, the original square is Tor-independent.
∎

Lemma 3.2.18

(Closed pullback base change for degree-
m

n=1
Gysin maps: divisor-step case)
.

Let
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

be a cartesian square in
\Sm_{S}
with
i
and
i^{\prime}
closed immersions, and assume the square is
Tor-independent.
Assume moreover that

g:X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X

is a principal smooth divisor immersion. Then for every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
there is a canonical homotopy

i^{*}_{m}\circ g_{*}\ \simeq\ g^{\prime}_{*}\circ i^{\prime*}_{m-1}

in the mapping space

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(X^{\prime}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z)\bigr).

Proof.

Write

D:=D(Z/X),\qquad D^{\prime}:=D(Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}),

with special fibers

N:=N_{Z/X},\qquad N^{\prime}:=N_{Z^{\prime}/X^{\prime}},

and open complements

U:=D\setminus N,\qquad U^{\prime}:=D^{\prime}\setminus N^{\prime}.

By Tor-independence and Lemma
1.6.1
, there is a canonical identification

D^{\prime}\ \simeq\ D\times_{X}X^{\prime}

over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}
, compatible with the special fibers, the open strata, and the canonical open
identifications

\varphi_{i}:U\xrightarrow{\sim}X\times\mathbb{G}_{m},\qquad\varphi_{i^{\prime}}:U^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\sim}X^{\prime}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}.

Let

g_{D}:D^{\prime}\hookrightarrow D,\qquad g_{N}:N^{\prime}\hookrightarrow N,\qquad g_{U}:U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow U

be the induced morphisms. Since
g
is a principal smooth divisor immersion, so are
g_{D}
,
g_{N}
, and
g_{U}
.
Write

\pi:N\to Z,\qquad\pi^{\prime}:N^{\prime}\to Z^{\prime}

for the vector bundle projections.

Because
g:X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
is a principal smooth divisor immersion, the induced map

g^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Z

is again a principal smooth divisor immersion, hence a trivialized regular immersion with its
canonical coordinate trivialization. Therefore the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}

is one of the closed base-change squares covered by
Axiom
(wBM9)
(c). Thus there is a canonical homotopy

(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ g_{N*}\ \simeq\ g^{\prime}_{*}\circ(\pi^{\prime*})^{-1}.

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(N^{\prime}),\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z)\bigr).

Let

\delta_{i}:\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(U)[-1]\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(N),\qquad\delta_{i^{\prime}}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(U^{\prime})[-1]\to\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(N^{\prime})

be the boundary maps of the localization sequences of the pairs
(D,N)
and
(D^{\prime},N^{\prime})
.

By Axiom
(wBM6)
, applied to the principal smooth divisor immersion

N\hookrightarrow D

and the closed pullback
g_{D}
, there is a canonical homotopy

\delta_{i}\circ g_{U*}[-1]\ \simeq\ g_{N*}\circ\delta_{i^{\prime}}.

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(U^{\prime})[-1],\mathcal{G}_{m}(N)\bigr).

By compatibility of the open identifications with base change, there is a canonical homotopy

(\varphi_{i})^{*}[-1]\circ(g\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})_{*}[-1]\ \simeq\ g_{U*}[-1]\circ(\varphi_{i^{\prime}})^{*}[-1].

Finally, Lemma
3.2.16
with
r=1
gives a canonical homotopy

\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}\circ g_{*}\ \simeq\ (g\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})_{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X^{\prime},m-1}.

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(X^{\prime}),\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-1]\bigr).

Unwinding Definition
2.4.4
for the length-
1
simplices
i
and
i^{\prime}
, we have

i_{m}^{*}=(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{i}\circ(\varphi_{i})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m},

(i^{\prime})_{m-1}^{*}=(\pi^{\prime*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{i^{\prime}}\circ(\varphi_{i^{\prime}})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X^{\prime},m-1}.

Composing
(1)
,
(2)
,
(3)
, and
(4)
yields

i_{m}^{*}\circ g_{*}\ \simeq\ (g^{\prime})_{*}\circ i_{m-1}^{\prime*},

as claimed.
∎

Lemma 3.2.19

(Relative graph pullbacks and localization for a divisor pulled back from the base)
.

Let
B\in\Sm_{S}
, let

i_{B}:D\hookrightarrow B

be a principal smooth divisor immersion with open complement

j_{B}:V\hookrightarrow B,

and let

f:X\to X^{\prime}

be a morphism in
\Sm_{B}
.

Put

Z:=X\times_{B}D,\qquad Z^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\times_{B}D,

U:=X\times_{B}V,\qquad U^{\prime}:=X^{\prime}\times_{B}V,

and let

i:Z\hookrightarrow X,\qquad i^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime},\qquad j:U\hookrightarrow X,\qquad j^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}

be the induced immersions, together with the induced
B
-morphisms

f_{Z}:Z\to Z^{\prime},\qquad f_{U}:U\to U^{\prime}.

Then, for every
d\in\mathbf{Z}
, the relative graph pullbacks fit into a morphism of localization fiber sequences

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d-1}(U^{\prime})[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{(f_{U})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d-1}[-1]}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d-1}(Z^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{(f_{Z})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d-1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d}(X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(j^{\prime})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d}(U^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f_{U})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d-1}(U)[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d-1}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d}(U).}

Here the graph pullbacks are taken relative to the common bases
V
,
B
, and
D
, respectively;
we suppress these bases from the notation.

Proof.

Write

P:=X\times_{B}X^{\prime},\qquad P_{V}:=P\times_{B}V\simeq U\times_{V}U^{\prime},\qquad P_{D}:=P\times_{B}D\simeq Z\times_{D}Z^{\prime}.

Let

\Gamma_{f}:X\hookrightarrow P,\qquad\Gamma_{f_{U}}:U\hookrightarrow P_{V},\qquad\Gamma_{f_{Z}}:Z\hookrightarrow P_{D}

be the relative graph immersions, and let

\operatorname{pr}_{2}:P\to X^{\prime},\qquad\operatorname{pr}_{2,V}:P_{V}\to U^{\prime},\qquad\operatorname{pr}_{2,D}:P_{D}\to Z^{\prime}

be the second projections. By definition,

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d}=(\Gamma_{f})_{d}^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2})^{*},\qquad(f_{U})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d}=(\Gamma_{f_{U}})_{d}^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2,V})^{*},\qquad(f_{Z})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d-1}=(\Gamma_{f_{Z}})_{d-1}^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2,D})^{*}.

The left square.

Since
P_{V}=P\times_{B}V
, there is a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{f_{U}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

where
v:P_{V}\hookrightarrow P
is the open immersion induced by
j_{B}:V\hookrightarrow B
.
In particular,
v
is smooth. Applying Lemma
3.2.14
to this cartesian square yields a canonical homotopy

(\Gamma_{f_{U}})_{d}^{*}\circ v^{*}\ \simeq\ j^{*}\circ(\Gamma_{f})_{d}^{*}.

Moreover, since

\operatorname{pr}_{2}\circ v=j^{\prime}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2,V},

smooth contravariance gives

v^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2})^{*}\ \simeq\ (\operatorname{pr}_{2,V})^{*}\circ(j^{\prime})^{*}.

Composing
(1)
and
(2)
, we obtain

(f_{U})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d}\circ(j^{\prime})^{*}\ \simeq\ j^{*}\circ f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d}.

The middle square.

Since
P_{D}=P\times_{B}D
, there is a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{f_{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

where
u:P_{D}\hookrightarrow P
is the closed immersion induced by
i_{B}:D\hookrightarrow B
.
Because
i_{B}
is a principal smooth divisor immersion and
P\to B
is smooth, the immersion
u
is again a principal smooth divisor immersion.

We claim that this square is Tor-independent. Zariski-locally on
B
, choose an equation

t\in\Gamma(B,\mathcal{O}_{B})

cutting out
D
. Then
u
is cut out on
P
by the non-zero-divisor
t_{P}:=p_{B}^{*}(t)
, where

p_{B}:P\to B

is the structural map. Pulling
t_{P}
back along
\Gamma_{f}
gives the local equation of

i:Z\hookrightarrow X.

Since
i
is a principal smooth divisor immersion, this pullback is a non-zero-divisor on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Therefore tensoring

0\to\mathcal{O}_{P}\xrightarrow{\ t_{P}\ }\mathcal{O}_{P}\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{D}}\to 0

with
\mathcal{O}_{X}
along
\Gamma_{f}
remains exact, so

\Tor^{\mathcal{O}_{P}}_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{P_{D}},\mathcal{O}_{X})=0.

Hence the square is Tor-independent.

Applying Lemma
3.2.18
to the preceding cartesian square gives a canonical homotopy

(\Gamma_{f})_{d}^{*}\circ u_{*}\ \simeq\ i_{*}\circ(\Gamma_{f_{Z}})_{d-1}^{*}.

Now consider the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{2,D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{2}}

The morphism
\operatorname{pr}_{2}:P\to X^{\prime}
is smooth, being the base change of
X\to B
. Therefore
Axiom
(wBM4)
yields a canonical homotopy

(\operatorname{pr}_{2})^{*}\circ i^{\prime}_{*}\simeq u_{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2,D})^{*}.

Composing
(4)
and
(5)
, we obtain

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d}\circ i^{\prime}_{*}\ \simeq\ i_{*}\circ(f_{Z})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d-1}.

The right square.

The homotopies
(3)
and
(6)
give a morphism between the two localization fiber sequences. Lemma
3.2.13
then identifies the induced maps on connecting morphisms. This yields the displayed morphism of fiber sequences.
∎

Lemma 3.2.20

(Open-stratum description of the total-fiber map)
.

In the situation of Lemma
1.4.16
, let

\eta:\mathcal{K}_{D(d_{k}\sigma),H_{\bullet}(d_{k}\sigma)}(m+n-1)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{K}_{D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)),H_{\bullet}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))}(m+n-1)

be the natural transformation whose
K
-component is

\eta_{K}=(\Pi_{K})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{\,m+n-1-|K|}.

Under the identifications of Lemma
2.3.3
, the induced morphism

\TotFib(\eta)

identifies with

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{U,\,m+n-1}[1-n]:\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}(U_{d_{k}\sigma})[1-n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}(U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})[1-n].

Proof.

Set

X:=D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)),\qquad X^{\prime}:=D(d_{k}\sigma).

Thus
X
and
X^{\prime}
are smooth over
\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}
, with ordered boundary divisors

H_{0},\dots,H_{n-2}\subset X,\qquad H^{\prime}_{0},\dots,H^{\prime}_{n-2}\subset X^{\prime},

and
\Pi:X\to X^{\prime}
is a morphism over
\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}
by Lemma
1.4.16
.

For
0\leq r\leq n-1
, define

U^{(r)}:=X\setminus\bigcup_{j<r}H_{j},\qquad U^{\prime(r)}:=X^{\prime}\setminus\bigcup_{j<r}H^{\prime}_{j}.

For every subset
K\subseteq\{r,\dots,n-2\}
, put

H_{K}^{(r)}:=H_{K}\cap U^{(r)},\qquad H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}:=H^{\prime}_{K}\cap U^{\prime(r)}.

Also set

B_{K}^{(r)}:=C_{K}\cap\bigcap_{j<r}\{t_{j}\neq 0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}.

Then

H_{K}^{(r)}=X\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}}B_{K}^{(r)},\qquad H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}=X^{\prime}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}}B_{K}^{(r)}.

Since
\Pi
is over
\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}
, it restricts to a morphism

\Pi_{K}^{(r)}:H_{K}^{(r)}\longrightarrow H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}

in
\Sm_{B_{K}^{(r)}}
.
When no confusion is possible, we suppress the base
B_{K}^{(r)}
from the notation for the
relative graph pullback.

For
0\leq r\leq n-1
, let

\mathcal{L}^{(r)}_{X}(K):=\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1-|K|}\bigl(H_{K}^{(r)}\bigr)[-r],\qquad\mathcal{L}^{(r)}_{X^{\prime}}(K):=\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1-|K|}\bigl(H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}\bigr)[-r]

for
K\subseteq\{r,\dots,n-2\}
. By Lemma
2.3.3
, there are canonical identifications

\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m+n-1)\bigr)\simeq\mathcal{L}^{(r)}_{X},

\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet}}(m+n-1)\bigr)\simeq\mathcal{L}^{(r)}_{X^{\prime}}.

We claim, by induction on
r
, that under these identifications the natural transformation

\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\eta)

identifies with the natural transformation

\eta^{(r)}:\mathcal{L}^{(r)}_{X^{\prime}}\Longrightarrow\mathcal{L}^{(r)}_{X}

whose
K
-component is

\eta^{(r)}_{K}=(\Pi_{K}^{(r)})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{\,m+n-1-|K|}[-r].

Base case
r=0
.

Here
H_{K}^{(0)}=H_{K}
and
H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(0)}=H^{\prime}_{K}
, and
\Pi_{K}^{(0)}=\Pi_{K}
. By definition of
\eta
,
its
K
-component is

\eta_{K}=(\Pi_{K})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{\,m+n-1-|K|},

which is exactly
(\ast_{0})
.

Induction step
r\to r+1
.

Assume
(\ast_{r})
. Fix
K\subseteq\{r+1,\dots,n-2\}
, and set

d_{K}:=m+n-1-|K|.

Inside the base
B_{K}^{(r)}
, consider the principal smooth divisor

D_{K}^{(r)}:=B_{K}^{(r)}\cap\{t_{r}=0\}=B_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)}\hookrightarrow B_{K}^{(r)},

with open complement
B_{K}^{(r+1)}
.

Its pullback to
H_{K}^{(r)}
is the principal smooth divisor

i_{K,r}:H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)}\hookrightarrow H_{K}^{(r)},

with open complement
H_{K}^{(r+1)}
; similarly on the primed side we get

i^{\prime}_{K,r}:H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}\hookrightarrow H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)},

with open complement
H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r+1)}
.

Applying Lemma
3.2.19
to the
B_{K}^{(r)}
-morphism

\Pi_{K}^{(r)}:H_{K}^{(r)}\to H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)},

we obtain a morphism of fiber sequences

\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d_{K}}\bigl(H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r+1)}\bigr)[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{(\Pi_{K}^{(r+1)})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d_{K}}[-1]}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d_{K}-1}\bigl(H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(i^{\prime}_{K,r})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{(\Pi_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d_{K}-1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d_{K}}\bigl(H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r)}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Pi_{K}^{(r)})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d_{K}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d_{K}}\bigl(H_{K}^{\prime}{}^{(r+1)}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Pi_{K}^{(r+1)})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d_{K}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d_{K}}\bigl(H_{K}^{(r+1)}\bigr)[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d_{K}-1}\bigl(H_{K\cup\{r\}}^{(r)}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(i_{K,r})_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d_{K}}\bigl(H_{K}^{(r)}\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}_{d_{K}}\bigl(H_{K}^{(r+1)}\bigr).}

After
r
desuspensions, and using the induction hypothesis
(\ast_{r})
, the two middle vertical maps are exactly the
K
- and
(K\cup\{r\})
-components of

\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\eta).

Now taking the objectwise fiber in the
r
-direction and using the canonical identification of Lemma
2.3.3
, we see that the
K
-component of

\operatorname{Fib}_{r}\bigl(\operatorname{Fib}_{r-1}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\eta)\bigr)

identifies with

(\Pi_{K}^{(r+1)})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{d_{K}}[-r-1].

Since objectwise fibers are functorial, these componentwise identifications assemble into the natural transformation
\eta^{(r+1)}
. This proves
(\ast_{r+1})
.

Conclusion.

Taking
r=n-1
, there is only the empty subset
K=\varnothing
. The base is

B_{\varnothing}^{(n-1)}=(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1},

and

H_{\varnothing}^{(n-1)}=U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)},\qquad H_{\varnothing}^{\prime}{}^{(n-1)}=U_{d_{k}\sigma}.

Moreover, the induced morphism

\Pi_{\varnothing}^{(n-1)}:U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}\to U_{d_{k}\sigma}

is exactly the open-stratum morphism
f_{U}
of Lemma
1.4.16
(4). Therefore

\TotFib(\eta)=\operatorname{Fib}_{n-2}\cdots\operatorname{Fib}_{0}(\eta)

identifies with

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{U,\,m+n-1}[1-n]:\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}(U_{d_{k}\sigma})[1-n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}(U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})[1-n].

This is the claimed formula.
∎

Lemma 3.2.21

(Normal-bundle replacement: compatibility of total boundary simplices)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Let

f_{U}:U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}\longrightarrow U_{d_{k}\sigma},\qquad\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}}:H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\longrightarrow H_{[n-2]}(d_{k}\sigma)

be the morphisms of Lemma
1.4.16
.

Then the two
(n-2)
-simplices

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{U,\,m+n-1}[1-n]\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{k}\sigma,m}

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m+n-1}\bigl(U_{d_{k}\sigma}\bigr)[1-n],\ \mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\bigr)\Bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

For each
K\subseteq[n-2]
, let

\eta_{K}:=(\Pi_{K})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{\,m+n-1-|K|},

where

\Pi_{K}:H_{K}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\to H_{K}(d_{k}\sigma)

is the restriction of the morphism

\Pi:D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\to D(d_{k}\sigma)

from Lemma
1.4.16
.

Assume for the moment that the
\eta_{K}
assemble into a natural transformation

(3.2.21.a)

\eta:\mathcal{K}_{D(d_{k}\sigma),H_{\bullet}(d_{k}\sigma)}(m+n-1)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{K}_{D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)),H_{\bullet}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))}(m+n-1),

and that

(3.2.21.b)

\eta_{[n-2]}=(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m},

(3.2.21.c)

\TotFib(\eta)=f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{U,\,m+n-1}[1-n].

Then Lemma
2.3.5
(3) gives a canonical homotopy

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ\TotFib(\eta)\ \simeq\ \eta_{[n-2]}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{k}\sigma,m}

between two
(n-2)
-simplices. Using
(
3.2.21.b
) and (
3.2.21.c
), this becomes exactly

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{U,\,m+n-1}[1-n]\ \simeq\ (\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{k}\sigma,m},

which is the required homotopy.

It remains to justify
(
3.2.21.a
),
(
3.2.21.b
), and
(
3.2.21.c
).

The identification (
3.2.21.b
) is immediate: for

K=[n-2]
, one has

m+n-1-|K|=m,

and
\Pi_{K}
is precisely the deepest-stratum morphism
\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}}
.

The identification (
3.2.21.c
) is exactly
Lemma
3.2.20
.

It remains to prove that the
\eta_{K}
define a natural transformation
(
3.2.21.a
). Fix

K\subseteq[n-2],\qquad i\notin K.

Let

C_{K}:=\bigcap_{j\in K}\{t_{j}=0\}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}.

Since
\Pi
is a morphism over
\mathbb{A}^{n-1}_{S}
, each
\Pi_{K}
is a morphism in
\Sm_{C_{K}}
, so

\eta_{K}
is the relative graph pullback over
C_{K}
.

Define the relative graph ambient spaces

P_{K}:=H_{K}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\times_{C_{K}}H_{K}(d_{k}\sigma),

P_{K\cup\{i\}}:=H_{K\cup\{i\}}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\times_{C_{K\cup\{i\}}}H_{K\cup\{i\}}(d_{k}\sigma),

with relative graph immersions

\Gamma_{K}:H_{K}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\hookrightarrow P_{K},\qquad\Gamma_{K\cup\{i\}}:H_{K\cup\{i\}}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\hookrightarrow P_{K\cup\{i\}},

and second projections

\operatorname{pr}_{2,K}:P_{K}\to H_{K}(d_{k}\sigma),\qquad\operatorname{pr}_{2,K\cup\{i\}}:P_{K\cup\{i\}}\to H_{K\cup\{i\}}(d_{k}\sigma).

Then

\eta_{K}=(\Gamma_{K})^{*}_{\,m+n-1-|K|}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2,K})^{*},

\eta_{K\cup\{i\}}=(\Gamma_{K\cup\{i\}})^{*}_{\,m+n-2-|K|}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2,K\cup\{i\}})^{*}.

Since

H_{K\cup\{i\}}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))=H_{K}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\times_{C_{K}}C_{K\cup\{i\}},

and similarly for
d_{k}\sigma
, one has a canonical identification

P_{K\cup\{i\}}\cong P_{K}\times_{C_{K}}C_{K\cup\{i\}}.

Let

u_{K,i}:P_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow P_{K}

be the induced closed immersion. Because

C_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow C_{K}

is the coordinate divisor
t_{i}=0
, and
P_{K}\to C_{K}
is smooth,

u_{K,i}
is a principal smooth divisor immersion. Moreover the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K\cup\{i\}}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{K\cup\{i\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{K,i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{K\cup\{i\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{K,i}}

is cartesian, by base change of relative graphs.

This square is Tor-independent. Indeed, Zariski-locally on
C_{K}
, the immersion

C_{K\cup\{i\}}\hookrightarrow C_{K}
is cut out by the non-zero-divisor
t_{i}
; its pullback to

P_{K}
cuts out
u_{K,i}
, and its pullback along
\Gamma_{K}
cuts out
\iota_{K,i}
.
Since
\iota_{K,i}
is an effective Cartier divisor,
t_{i}
remains a non-zero-divisor on

\mathcal{O}_{H_{K}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))}
, so the standard two-term resolution by multiplication by
t_{i}

stays exact after pullback along
\Gamma_{K}
.

Therefore Lemma
3.2.18
gives a canonical homotopy

(\Gamma_{K})^{*}_{\,m+n-1-|K|}\circ u_{K,i*}\ \simeq\ (\iota_{K,i})_{*}\circ(\Gamma_{K\cup\{i\}})^{*}_{\,m+n-2-|K|}.

On the other hand, the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{K\cup\{i\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K\cup\{i\}}(d_{k}\sigma)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H_{K}(d_{k}\sigma)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{2,K\cup\{i\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{K,i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}_{K,i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{pr}_{2,K}}

is cartesian, and
\operatorname{pr}_{2,K}
is smooth as the base change of the smooth morphism

H_{K}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\to C_{K}
. Hence Axiom
(wBM4)
gives a canonical homotopy

(\operatorname{pr}_{2,K})^{*}\circ(\iota^{\prime}_{K,i})_{*}\ \simeq\ u_{K,i*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2,K\cup\{i\}})^{*}.

Composing these two homotopies, we obtain

\eta_{K}\circ(\iota^{\prime}_{K,i})_{*}\ \simeq\ (\iota_{K,i})_{*}\circ\eta_{K\cup\{i\}}.

This is exactly the naturality required for
(
3.2.21.a
). The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 3.2.22
.

One can also factor
\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}}
as a vector bundle projection followed by a vector subbundle immersion
(Lemma
1.4.14
), and hence define an alternative factorized pullback.
We do not use that factorization here. A comparison with the graph-pullback model may be useful later,
but is not needed for the present face-comparison arguments.

Remark 3.2.23

(Sanity check when
n=2
)
.

Fix

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z_{1}\hookrightarrow Z_{2})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{2},\qquad k=1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Set

N_{1,2}:=N_{Z_{1}/Z_{2}},\qquad p_{1,2}:N_{1,2}\to Z_{1}.

Then

{\operatorname{Sp}}_{1}(\sigma)=\bigl(N_{1,2}|_{Z_{0}}\hookrightarrow N_{1,2}\bigr),\qquad d_{1}\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z_{2})

are
1
-simplices.

For a
1
-simplex
\tau=(Y\hookrightarrow X)
, the total boundary simplex

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\tau,m}

is a
0
-simplex, i.e. a morphism

{\underline{\partial}}_{\tau,m}:\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{0}(\tau)\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(U_{\tau})[-1],

where

H_{0}(\tau)\subset D(\tau),\qquad U_{\tau}=D(\tau)\setminus H_{0}(\tau).

Applied to
\tau={\operatorname{Sp}}_{1}(\sigma)
and
\tau=d_{1}\sigma
, this gives canonical identifications

U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{1}(\sigma)}\simeq N_{1,2}\times\mathbb{G}_{m},\qquad U_{d_{1}\sigma}\simeq Z_{2}\times\mathbb{G}_{m},

and the comparison on the open stratum is

f_{U}=(i_{1,2}\circ p_{1,2})\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}:N_{1,2}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\longrightarrow Z_{2}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}.

On the deepest strata one has

H_{0}(d_{1}\sigma)\simeq N_{Z_{0}/Z_{2}},

while

H_{0}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{1}(\sigma))\simeq N_{N_{1,2}|_{Z_{0}}/N_{1,2}}\simeq p_{0}^{*}N_{Z_{0}/Z_{1}}\simeq\bigl(N_{1,2}|_{Z_{0}}\bigr)\times_{Z_{0}}N_{Z_{0}/Z_{1}},

where

p_{0}:N_{1,2}|_{Z_{0}}\to Z_{0}

is the restricted bundle projection.

Let

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{Z_{0}/Z_{1}}\xrightarrow{\ \iota\ }\mathcal{N}_{Z_{0}/Z_{2}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{1,2}|_{Z_{0}}\longrightarrow 0

be the exact sequence of normal bundles for the chain
Z_{0}\subset Z_{1}\subset Z_{2}
.
Under the above identifications, the deepest-stratum comparison map

\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}}:H_{0}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{1}(\sigma))\longrightarrow H_{0}(d_{1}\sigma)

is the morphism

\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}}:\bigl(N_{1,2}|_{Z_{0}}\bigr)\times_{Z_{0}}N_{Z_{0}/Z_{1}}\longrightarrow N_{Z_{0}/Z_{2}}.

Geometrically, it factors as a vector bundle projection followed by a vector subbundle immersion. This is the

n=2
instance of the general morphism used in Lemma
3.2.21
.

3.3.
Assemble: initial, internal, terminal face relations

3.3.1
Dependency graph for internal faces.

Fix

\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

The internal face relation

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\simeq\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{k}\sigma)

is obtained by the chain

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\xrightarrow{\text{face of }{\underline{\partial}}}{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\text{normal-bundle replacement}}d_{k}\sigma.

More precisely:

(1)

Lemma
3.1.12
identifies the
k
-th face of the coherent total boundary simplex
with the total boundary simplex of
{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)
, after the canonical panel transports.

(2)

Lemma
3.1.13
rewrites the deepest-stratum vector-bundle inverse
appearing in
d_{k}(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma))
.

(3)

Lemma
3.2.9
rewrites the composite

\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

as the collapse attached to the specialization
{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)
, followed by the
n=1
Gysin map for

i_{k,n}:Z_{k}\hookrightarrow Z_{n}.

(4)

Lemma
3.2.21
compares the total boundary simplex of
{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)
with that of

d_{k}\sigma
through the normal-bundle replacement morphisms

\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad f_{U}.

Definition 3.3.2

(Collapse whiskering operator)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Write

g_{\sigma}:Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z_{n}

for the composite immersion, and set

N_{k,k+1}:=N_{Z_{k}/Z_{k+1}},\qquad\pi_{0}:N_{k,k+1}|_{Z_{0}}\to Z_{0}.

Let

\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}^{(k)}:=\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n)\bigr)^{(k)}

be the
k
-panel restriction of the localization cube.
Define the simplicial map

\operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}:\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}^{(k)}\bigr),\ \mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(N_{k,k+1}|_{Z_{0}}\bigr)\Bigr)\longrightarrow\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)

by

\operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}(y):=(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ y\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}.

Here

(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}:\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(N_{k,k+1}|_{Z_{0}}\bigr)\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})

is the chosen inverse to the vector-bundle pullback
(\pi_{0})^{*}
.

Lemma 3.3.3

(Internal faces via specialization and collapse)
.

Keep the notation of Definition
3.3.2
. Define

y_{k}:=(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}.

Then

y_{k}\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\TotFib\bigl(\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}^{(k)}\bigr),\ \mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(N_{k,k+1}|_{Z_{0}}\bigr)\Bigr)_{n-2},

and the following pairs of
(n-2)
-simplices of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic:

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ \operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}(y_{k}),\qquad\operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}(y_{k})\ \simeq\ \mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{k}\sigma).

Proof.

By Definition
2.4.4
,

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)=(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}.

Since whiskering by fixed
0
-simplices is simplicial, we obtain

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ (\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ d_{k}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}.

Next, Lemma
3.1.12
gives

d_{k}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)\ \simeq\ \bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,\operatorname{deep}}\bigr)^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}.

Substituting this yields

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ (\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,\operatorname{deep}}\bigr)^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}.

Now apply Lemma
3.1.13
to obtain the canonical homotopy

(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,\operatorname{deep}}\bigr)^{*}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}.

Hence

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ (\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}.

By the definition of
y_{k}
and of the collapse whiskering operator

\operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}
, this is exactly

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ \operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}(y_{k}).

This proves the first homotopy.

The second homotopy

\operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}(y_{k})\ \simeq\ \mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{k}\sigma)

is precisely Lemma
3.3.7
below.
∎

Lemma 3.3.4

(Gysin map of a section of a vector bundle projection)
.

Let

q:E\to X

be a vector bundle projection in
\Sm_{S}
, and let

s:X\hookrightarrow E

be a section. Then for every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
there are canonical homotopies

s_{m}^{*}\circ q^{*}\ \simeq\ \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad s_{m}^{*}\ \simeq\ (q^{*})^{-1}

in the mapping spaces

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(X),\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\bigr)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(E),\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\bigr),

where
(q^{*})^{-1}
denotes the chosen inverse to the vector-bundle pullback
q^{*}
.

Proof.

Let

0:X\hookrightarrow E

be the zero section, and let

t_{s}:E\xrightarrow{\sim}E

be translation by the section
s
. Then
t_{s}\circ 0=s
and
q\circ t_{s}=q
.

By smooth base change for degree-
m

n=1
Gysin maps, applied to the isomorphism
t_{s}
, there is a
canonical homotopy

s_{m}^{*}\ \simeq\ 0_{m}^{*}\circ(t_{s})^{*}.

For the zero section, the deformation space is canonically

D(0)\simeq E\times\mathbb{A}^{1},

with special fiber
E
, open complement
E\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and deepest-stratum projection
q:E\to X
.
Unwinding Definition
2.4.4
for the
1
-simplex
0:X\hookrightarrow E
, we obtain

0_{m}^{*}=(q^{*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{t}\circ\beta^{(1)}_{E,m}.

By Axiom
(wBM12)
,
\delta_{t}\circ\beta^{(1)}_{E,m}\simeq\operatorname{id}
, hence

0_{m}^{*}\ \simeq\ (q^{*})^{-1}.

Combining
(1)
and
(2)
, and using
q\circ t_{s}=q
, we get

s_{m}^{*}\circ q^{*}\ \simeq\ (q^{*})^{-1}\circ(t_{s})^{*}\circ q^{*}\ \simeq\ (q^{*})^{-1}\circ(q\circ t_{s})^{*}\ =\ (q^{*})^{-1}\circ q^{*}\ \simeq\ \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

Since
q^{*}
is an equivalence, this also gives

s_{m}^{*}\ \simeq\ (q^{*})^{-1}.

∎

Lemma 3.3.5

(Graph pullback over a vector-bundle base)
.

Let
B\in\Sm_{S}
, and let

f:Y\to X

be a morphism in
\Sm_{B}
. Assume that both structural morphisms

\pi_{Y}:Y\to B,\qquad\pi_{X}:X\to B

are vector bundle projections. Then for every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
there is a canonical homotopy

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}\circ(\pi_{X})^{*}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{Y})^{*}

in the mapping space

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(B),\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y)\bigr).

Proof.

Write

\Gamma_{f}:Y\hookrightarrow Y\times_{B}X

for the graph of
f
, and

\operatorname{pr}_{1}:Y\times_{B}X\to Y,\qquad\operatorname{pr}_{2}:Y\times_{B}X\to X

for the two projections. Since
X\to B
is a vector bundle projection, the morphism

\operatorname{pr}_{1}:Y\times_{B}X\to Y

is again a vector bundle projection, and
\Gamma_{f}
is a section of
\operatorname{pr}_{1}
. Hence
Lemma
3.3.4
gives

(\operatorname{pr}_{1})^{*}\circ(\Gamma_{f})_{m}^{*}\ \simeq\ \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y)}.

By Definition
3.2.11
,

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}=(\Gamma_{f})_{m}^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2})^{*}.

Therefore

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}\circ(\pi_{X})^{*}\ \simeq\ (\Gamma_{f})_{m}^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{2})^{*}\circ(\pi_{X})^{*}\ \simeq\ (\Gamma_{f})_{m}^{*}\circ(\pi_{X}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2})^{*}.

Because
f
lies over
B
, one has

\pi_{X}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}=\pi_{Y}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{1},

so the above becomes

(\Gamma_{f})_{m}^{*}\circ(\pi_{Y}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{1})^{*}\ \simeq\ (\Gamma_{f})_{m}^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}_{1})^{*}\circ(\pi_{Y})^{*}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{Y})^{*}

by
(1)
.
∎

Corollary 3.3.6

(Compatibility of
(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}
with deepest projections)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Then the graph-model pullback

(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}:\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-2]}(d_{k}\sigma)\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\bigr)

satisfies the compatibility

(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}\circ(\pi_{d_{k}\sigma})^{*}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)})^{*}\circ(\pi_{0})^{*}

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0}),\mathcal{G}_{m}(H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)))\bigr).

Proof.

By the geometric description of deepest strata, both

H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\to Z_{0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad H_{[n-2]}(d_{k}\sigma)\to Z_{0}

are vector bundle projections, namely

\pi_{0}\circ\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\pi_{d_{k}\sigma}.

Apply Lemma
3.3.5
to the morphism

\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}}:H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma))\to H_{[n-2]}(d_{k}\sigma)

over the base
Z_{0}
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.7

(Collapse identification)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 1\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Let

y_{k}=(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}

be as in Lemma
3.3.3
.

Then the two
(n-2)
-simplices

\operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}(y_{k}),\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{k}\sigma)

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

Since whiskering by fixed simplices preserves homotopies, it is enough to establish the two auxiliary homotopies

(3.3.7.a)

(\pi_{d_{k}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\simeq(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}

and

(3.3.7.b)

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{U,\,m+n-1}[1-n]\circ\beta_{d_{k}\sigma,m}.\ \simeq\ \bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

Assuming these for the moment, we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{k}\sigma)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{d_{k}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{k}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{d_{k}\sigma,m}

by Definition
2.4.4

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{k}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{d_{k}\sigma,m}

by (
3.3.7.a
)

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ f^{\mathrm{gr}}_{U,!,\,m+n-1}[n-1]\circ\beta_{d_{k}\sigma,m}

by Lemma
3.2.21

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U}\bigr)^{*})^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

by (
3.3.7.b
)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ y_{k}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

by the definition of
y_{k}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}(y_{k})

\displaystyle\text{by Definition\penalty\ \ref{def:collapse-definition}}.

It remains to justify (
3.3.7.a
) and
(
3.3.7.b
).

For (
3.3.7.a
), Corollary
3.3.6
gives

(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}\circ\pi_{d_{k}\sigma}^{*}\ \simeq\ \pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*}\circ\pi_{0}^{*}.

Hence

(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\Pi_{\operatorname{deep}})^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{m}\circ(\pi_{d_{k}\sigma})^{*}\ \simeq\ \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})}.

which identifies the two inverses by contractibility of inverse data for vector bundle pullbacks.

For (
3.3.7.b
), both sides are canonically homotopic to

\beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ(p_{k,n})^{*}\circ(i_{k,n})_{m}^{*}.

Indeed, Lemma
3.2.9
gives

\bigl(\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U}\bigr)^{*})^{-1}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ \beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ(p_{k,n})^{*}\circ(i_{k,n})_{m}^{*}.

On the other hand, under the canonical identifications

U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}\simeq N_{k,n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1},\qquad U_{d_{k}\sigma}\simeq Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1},

the morphism
f_{U}
identifies with

i_{k,n}^{(n-1)}\circ p_{k,n}^{(n-1)}.

Therefore Axiom
(wBM11)
for
p_{k,n}
and
Lemma
3.2.8
for
i_{k,n}
yield

f^{\mathrm{gr},*}_{U,\,m+n-1}[1-n]\circ\beta_{d_{k}\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ \beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ(p_{k,n})^{*}\circ(i_{k,n})_{m}^{*}.

This proves (
3.3.7.b
), hence the lemma.
∎

Lemma 3.3.8

(Initial face of the coherent boundary simplex)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad n\geq 2,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Set

\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}:=\mathcal{K}_{D(\sigma),H_{\bullet}(\sigma)}(m+n),

and let

\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}^{\neg 0}

denote its restriction to subsets
K\subseteq[n-1]
with
0\notin K
.
Under the product identification of Lemma
1.5.4
,

D(\sigma)|_{t_{0}\neq 0}\simeq D(d_{0}\sigma)\times\mathbb{G}_{m},

and the induced identification of localization cubes, there is a canonical homotopy

d_{0}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)\ \simeq\ {\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{0}\sigma,m}\circ\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\bigl(U_{d_{0}\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\bigr)[-n],\,\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-2]}(d_{0}\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr)_{n-2}.

Proof.

The initial face of the spine filler for

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}

is the tail of the iterated fiber tower, i.e. the tower attached to the restriction
of
\mathcal{K}_{\sigma,m}
to subsets not containing
0
.
By Lemma
1.5.4
and Axiom
(wBM7)
(d),
this restricted cube identifies with the localization cube of

D(d_{0}\sigma)\times\mathbb{G}_{m}

with the inert last
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factor.
Under Lemmas
2.3.3
and
2.3.4
, the resulting
total fiber identifies with

\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\bigl(U_{d_{0}\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\bigr)[-n],

and the extra final step is exactly the boundary morphism

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}.

The conclusion follows by the same spine-filling argument used in
Lemma
3.1.6
.
∎

Lemma 3.3.9

(Initial face compatibility)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad n\geq 2,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Then the two
(n-2)
-simplices

d_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr),\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{0}\sigma)

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{1})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

By Definition
2.4.4
,

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{0}\sigma)=(\pi_{d_{0}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{0}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{d_{0}\sigma,m}.

By Lemma
3.3.8
, there is a canonical homotopy

d_{0}\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\bigr)\ \simeq\ {\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{0}\sigma,m}\circ\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}.

Under the open-stratum identification

U_{\sigma}\simeq U_{d_{0}\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m},

Axiom
(wBM12)
gives

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ \beta_{d_{0}\sigma,m}.

Whiskering with
(\pi_{d_{0}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}
therefore yields

d_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ (\pi_{d_{0}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{0}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{d_{0}\sigma,m}=\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{0}\sigma).

∎

Example 3.3.10

(Face compatibilities in low dimensions in the motivic setting)
.

Work in the motivic example of the weak Borel-Moore formalism, with values in
\mathrm{SH}(S)
, and fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.

(1)

The case
n=1
: the usual deformation-to-the-normal-bundle formula.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\xhookrightarrow{i}Z_{1})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{1}.

Then the higher deformation space is the usual deformation space

D(\sigma)\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S},

with special fiber

H_{0}(\sigma)\simeq N_{Z_{0}/Z_{1}}

and open stratum

U_{\sigma}\simeq Z_{1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}.

The deepest-stratum projection is the vector bundle projection

\pi_{\sigma}:H_{0}(\sigma)\to Z_{0}.

In this case the coherent total boundary simplex is just the localization boundary map

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}:\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(U_{\sigma})[-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{0}(\sigma)\bigr).

Therefore

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)=(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{1})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})

is precisely the usual motivic Gysin map attached to the regular immersion
i
, expressed through
deformation to the normal bundle.

(2)

The case
n=2
: the construction produces an actual homotopy.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\xhookrightarrow{i}Z_{1}\xhookrightarrow{j}Z_{2})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{2}.

Then the higher deformation space is Rost’s double deformation space

D(\sigma)\to\mathbb{A}^{2}_{S}

with ordered boundary divisors
H_{0}(\sigma),H_{1}(\sigma)
, open stratum

U_{\sigma}\simeq Z_{2}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{2},

and deepest stratum

H_{01}(\sigma):=H_{0}(\sigma)\cap H_{1}(\sigma),

which is a vector bundle over
Z_{0}
with projection
\pi_{\sigma}
.

The localization cube is a square, and the coherent total boundary simplex

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\in\Map_{\mathrm{SH}(S)}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m+2}(U_{\sigma})[-2],\ \mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{01}(\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr)_{1}

is therefore a
1
-simplex, namely an explicit homotopy between the two possible
“boundary-of-boundary” composites determined by the localization square.

Whiskering with
\beta_{\sigma,m}
on the source and
(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}
on the target gives

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\in\Map_{\mathrm{SH}(S)}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{2}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)_{1}.

Thus
\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)
is an explicit homotopy between two maps

\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{2})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0}).

By the face identities proved in this subsection, its endpoints are

i^{*}\circ j^{*}\qquad\text{and}\qquad(j\circ i)^{*},

so the
n=2
construction exhibits the motivic functoriality of Gysin maps for composable regular immersions
as a coherent homotopy.

Lemma 3.3.11

(Terminal face via the last panel and the vector bundle projection)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad n\geq 2,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Write

i_{n-1,n}:Z_{n-1}\hookrightarrow Z_{n},\qquad N_{n-1,n}:=N_{Z_{n-1}/Z_{n}},\qquad p_{n-1,n}:N_{n-1,n}\to Z_{n-1},

and let

\pi_{0}:N_{n-1,n}|_{Z_{0}}\to Z_{0}

be the restriction of
p_{n-1,n}
.

Then the two
(n-2)
-simplices

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{n}\sigma)\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}\bigl({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)\bigr)\circ(p_{n-1,n})^{*}

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n-1}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic.
Moreover, these homotopies are compatible with further end-preserving faces and degeneracies.

Proof.

Let

\tilde{p}:D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))\longrightarrow D(d_{n}\sigma)

be the smooth morphism induced by the vector bundle projection
p_{n-1,n}
.
On open strata, under the canonical identifications

U_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}\simeq N_{n-1,n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1},\qquad U_{d_{n}\sigma}\simeq Z_{n-1}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1},

it identifies with

\tilde{p}_{U}=p_{n-1,n}\times\operatorname{id}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n-1}}=p_{n-1,n}^{(n-1)}.

Let

\tilde{p}_{\operatorname{deep}}:H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))\to H_{[n-2]}(d_{n}\sigma)

be the induced map on deepest strata.

It is enough to establish the three auxiliary homotopies

(3.3.11.a)

(\pi_{d_{n}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ\tilde{p}_{\operatorname{deep}}^{*},

(3.3.11.b)

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}\circ\tilde{p}_{U}^{*}[1-n]\ \simeq\ \tilde{p}_{\operatorname{deep}}^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{n}\sigma,m},

and

(3.3.11.c)

\tilde{p}_{U}^{*}[1-n]\circ\beta_{d_{n}\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ \beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}\circ(p_{n-1,n})^{*}.

Assuming these for the moment, we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{n}\sigma)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{d_{n}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{n}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{d_{n}\sigma,m}

by Definition
2.4.4

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ\tilde{p}_{\operatorname{deep}}^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{n}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{d_{n}\sigma,m}

by (
3.3.11.a
)

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}\circ\tilde{p}_{U}^{*}[1-n]\circ\beta_{d_{n}\sigma,m}

by (
3.3.11.b
)

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}\circ\beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}\circ(p_{n-1,n})^{*}

by (
3.3.11.c
)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}\bigl({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)\bigr)\circ(p_{n-1,n})^{*}

\displaystyle\text{by Definition\penalty\ \ref{def:closed-pullback-simplex}}.

It remains to justify (
3.3.11.a
),
(
3.3.11.b
), and (
3.3.11.c
).

For (
3.3.11.a
), the deepest-stratum projections fit into the commutative square

H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))\xrightarrow{\ \tilde{p}_{\operatorname{deep}}\ }H_{[n-2]}(d_{n}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{d_{n}\sigma}\ }Z_{0},

H_{[n-2]}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}\ }N_{n-1,n}|_{Z_{0}}\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{0}\ }Z_{0},

with

\pi_{d_{n}\sigma}\circ\tilde{p}_{\operatorname{deep}}=\pi_{0}\circ\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}.

By construction,
\pi_{d_{n}\sigma}
,
\pi_{0}
, and
\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}
are vector bundle projections.
Hence the left-hand side of (
3.3.11.a
) is a right inverse to

(\pi_{d_{n}\sigma})^{*}
, and contractibility of inverse data yields the claimed homotopy.

For (
3.3.11.b
), by construction of the specialization flag

{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)
, the higher deformation space

D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))
is the pullback of
D(d_{n}\sigma)
along

p_{n-1,n}\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{A}^{n-1}}:N_{n-1,n}\times\mathbb{A}^{n-1}\longrightarrow Z_{n-1}\times\mathbb{A}^{n-1}.

Thus the induced morphism

\tilde{p}:D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))\longrightarrow D(d_{n}\sigma)

is a smooth morphism of equation-equipped ordered SNC pairs, compatible with the
ordered boundary equations.

For every
K\subseteq[n-2]
, write

\tilde{p}_{K}:H_{K}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))\longrightarrow H_{K}(d_{n}\sigma)

for the induced smooth morphism on strata. Smooth pullback and
smooth-closed Beck-Chevalley assemble into a natural transformation of
localization cubes

\eta_{\tilde{p}}:\mathcal{K}_{D(d_{n}\sigma),H_{\bullet}(d_{n}\sigma)}(m+n-1)\Longrightarrow\mathcal{K}_{D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)),H_{\bullet}({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))}(m+n-1),

whose
K
-component is

\tilde{p}_{K}^{*}.

Its deepest-stratum component is

(\eta_{\tilde{p}})_{[n-2]}=\tilde{p}_{\operatorname{deep}}^{*},

and, by Lemma
2.3.3
and its naturality,

\TotFib(\eta_{\tilde{p}})=\tilde{p}_{U}^{*}[1-n].

Applying Lemma
2.3.5
(3) to

\eta_{\tilde{p}}
gives

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}\circ\tilde{p}_{U}^{*}[1-n]\ \simeq\ \tilde{p}_{\operatorname{deep}}^{*}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{d_{n}\sigma,m},

which is exactly (
3.3.11.b
).

For (
3.3.11.c
), under the above identifications of open strata, the map

\tilde{p}_{U}
is exactly
p_{n-1,n}^{(n-1)}
. Since

\beta_{d_{n}\sigma,m}=\varphi_{d_{n}\sigma}^{*}[1-n]\circ\beta^{(n-1)}_{Z_{n-1},m}

and

\beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}=\varphi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}^{*}[1-n]\circ\beta^{(n-1)}_{N_{n-1,n},m},

Axiom
(wBM11)
for the smooth morphism
p_{n-1,n}
yields
(
3.3.11.c
).

The compatibility with further end-preserving faces and degeneracies follows from the functoriality of this
construction under restriction of
\sigma
along such simplicial operators: the pullback description of

D({\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma))
over
D(d_{n}\sigma)
, the induced natural transformation of localization cubes, and
the smooth naturality of collapse maps are all preserved under further end-preserving face and degeneracy
operations.
∎

Corollary 3.3.12

(The terminal geometric face
d_{n}\sigma
)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad n\geq 2,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Write

i_{n-1,n}:Z_{n-1}\hookrightarrow Z_{n}

for the last immersion. Then the two
(n-2)
-simplices

d_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{n}\sigma)\circ(i_{n-1,n})_{m}^{*}

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

Set
k:=n-1
, and let

y_{k}=(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{k}(\sigma),m}\circ\bigl((\Theta^{\mathrm{sp}}_{k,\sigma,U})^{*}\bigr)^{-1}

be as in Lemma
3.3.3
. Then

\displaystyle d_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{col}_{k,\sigma,m}(y_{k})

by Lemma
3.3.3

\displaystyle=(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ y_{k}\circ\kappa_{k,\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

by Definition
3.3.2

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{0}^{*})^{-1}\circ(\pi_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma)}^{*})^{-1}\circ{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}\circ\beta_{{\operatorname{Sp}}_{n-1}(\sigma),m}\circ p_{n-1,n}^{*}\circ(i_{n-1,n})_{m}^{*}

by Lemma
3.2.9

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{n}\sigma)\circ(i_{n-1,n})_{m}^{*}

\displaystyle\text{by Lemma\penalty\ \ref{lemma:terminal-face-simplicial}}.

This is the claimed homotopy.
∎

Theorem 3.3.13

(Face compatibility)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad n\geq 2,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Then the higher Gysin simplices satisfy the following face identities, canonically up to
contractible choice:

(1)

d_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ \mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{0}\sigma);

(2)

for every
1\leq k\leq n-1
,

d_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ \mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{k}\sigma);

(3)

d_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)\ \simeq\ \mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(d_{n}\sigma)\circ(i_{n-1,n})_{m}^{*},

where
i_{n-1,n}:Z_{n-1}\hookrightarrow Z_{n}
is the last immersion.

Proof.

The initial-face case is Lemma
3.3.9
, the internal-face cases are given by
Lemmas
3.3.3
and
3.3.7
,
and the terminal-face case is Corollary
3.3.12
.
∎

Remark 3.3.14

(Face compatibility for the terminal geometric face)
.

Corollary
3.3.12
is the mapping-space form of the terminal face identity for the
higher Gysin assignment. Together with Lemma
3.3.9
and the internal-face comparison proved in
Lemma
3.3.7
, it gives all face compatibilities for

\sigma\longmapsto\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma).

Once the degeneracy identities are proved, these face compatibilities assemble into the desired simplicial
functoriality.

Remark 3.3.15

(Index shift in mapping-space coordinates)
.

For

\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},

the simplex

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)

lives in degree
n-1
of a mapping space. Consequently the terminal simplicial operators on

N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})
appear with index lowered by
1
after passing to mapping-space coordinates:

d_{n}(\sigma)\rightsquigarrow d_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr),\qquad s_{n}(\sigma)\rightsquigarrow s_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr).

In particular, the terminal face relation is written with
d_{n-1}
in
Corollary
3.3.12
, and the same shift must be used for terminal degeneracies below.

3.4.
Degeneracy compatibility

3.4.1.
Strategy and geometry on parameters

3.4.2
Strategy for degeneracies.

Geometrically, the degeneracy
s_{k}\sigma
repeats one immersion. On deformation parameters this is implemented by
the panelization map
\mu_{k}
, which duplicates one coordinate hyperplane and hence inserts one extra boundary component
in the ordered SNC divisor on the deformation space.

For
k=n
, this appears directly as an appended inert last divisor, so the localization tower gains one extra terminal
boundary step

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}(U\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-n-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U)[-n].

For
k<n
, the same phenomenon occurs after transporting the duplicated coordinate to the last position via the
permutation formalism of §
3.1
.
In both cases, after precomposition by the relevant collapse map, the extra inert step cancels by
Axiom
(wBM12)
, and the result is exactly the corresponding simplicial degeneracy.

Lemma 3.4.3

(Appending an inert last divisor yields the last simplicial degeneracy)
.

Let
(X,H_{\bullet})
be an ordered SNC pair of length
n
, with open complement

j:U\hookrightarrow X.

Let
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet})
be an ordered SNC pair of length
n+1
, with open complement

j^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}.

Fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
. Assume we are given:

(1)

an identification of deepest strata

H^{\prime}_{[n]}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H_{[n-1]};

(2)

an identification of open strata

\upsilon:U^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }U\times\mathbb{G}_{m};

(3)

the property that, under these identifications, the spine data defining

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet},m}

is obtained from the spine data defining

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet},m}

by appending one last edge

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}(U\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-n-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U)[-n].

Set

\operatorname{can}_{U,m}[-n]:=\beta^{(1)}_{U,m+n}[-n]:\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U)[-n]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}(U\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-n-1].

Then, after transporting endpoints along the chosen identifications, the two
n
-simplices

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet},m}\circ\operatorname{can}_{U,m}[-n]\qquad\text{and}\qquad s_{n-1}\!\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet},m}\bigr)

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}\bigr),\ \mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U)[-n]\Bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

Set

K:=\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U)[-n],\ \mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}\bigr)\Bigr),

which is a Kan complex. After transporting endpoints along the chosen identifications, define

F:={\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet},m}\circ\operatorname{can}_{U,m}[-n]\in K_{n},\qquad F^{\prime}:=s_{n-1}\!\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet},m}\bigr)\in K_{n}.

If
n=1
, then
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{1}=\Delta^{1}
, so there is no filler issue: by assumption (3),
the simplex
F
is obtained from
{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet},m}
by appending the single edge

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ\operatorname{can}_{U,m}[-1],

and Axiom
(wBM12)
identifies this edge with the identity. Hence

F\simeq F^{\prime}.

Assume from now on that
n\geq 2
.

Let

S:=F^{\prime}|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}\in\Map({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},K).

By assumption (3), the restriction of

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet},m}

to the spine is obtained from the spine of

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet},m}

by appending the final edge

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}(U\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-n-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U)[-n].

After precomposition by

\operatorname{can}_{U,m}[-n]=\beta^{(1)}_{U,m+n}[-n],

this last edge becomes

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ\operatorname{can}_{U,m}[-n].

By Axiom
(wBM12)
, this is canonically homotopic to the identity of

\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U)[-n].

Therefore there is a canonical homotopy

\gamma:F|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}\simeq S=F^{\prime}|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}\qquad\text{in }\Map({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},K).

Now consider the restriction map

p:\Map(\Delta^{n},K)\longrightarrow\Map({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},K).

Since
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}
is inner anodyne and
K
is Kan,
p
is a trivial
Kan fibration. Hence
\gamma
admits a lift

\widetilde{\gamma}:\Delta^{1}\longrightarrow\Map(\Delta^{n},K)

with

\widetilde{\gamma}(0)=F.

Write

\widetilde{F}:=\widetilde{\gamma}(1).

Then

p(\widetilde{F})=F^{\prime}|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}=p(F^{\prime}).

The fiber

p^{-1}\!\bigl(F^{\prime}|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}\bigr)

is a contractible Kan complex, since
p
is a trivial Kan fibration. Therefore
\widetilde{F}
and
F^{\prime}

are connected by a path in that fiber, unique up to contractible choice. Concatenating this path with

\widetilde{\gamma}
yields a homotopy

F\simeq F^{\prime}

unique up to contractible choice. This is exactly the claimed canonical homotopy

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet},m}\circ\operatorname{can}_{U,m}[-n]\;\simeq\;s_{n-1}\!\bigl({\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{X,H_{\bullet},m}\bigr).

∎

3.4.4.
Case
k<n

Remark 3.4.5

(Degeneracy indices in mapping-space coordinates)
.

For

\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},

the simplex

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)

lives in degree
n-1
of the mapping space

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr).

Hence for

0\leq k\leq n-1

the degeneracy index is unchanged:

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)\rightsquigarrow s_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr).

By contrast, the terminal degeneracy on the nerve becomes the terminal degeneracy in degree
n-1
of the
mapping space:

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{n}\sigma)\rightsquigarrow s_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr).

The same convention applies to the boundary simplices
{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}
.

Notations 3.4.6

(Permutation-adapted open identification for the
k
-degeneracy)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}\qquad\text{and}\qquad 0\leq k\leq n-1.

By Lemma
1.5.1
and Lemma
1.5.2
, the
canonical isomorphism

\vartheta_{k,\sigma}:D(s_{k}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\operatorname{id}_{Z_{n}}\times\mu_{k})^{*}D(\sigma)

induces an isomorphism on open strata
