U_{s_{k}\sigma}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\operatorname{id}_{Z_{n}}\times\mu_{k})^{*}U_{\sigma}.

Using the canonical open identification

\varphi_{\sigma}:U_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}

of Lemma
1.4.4
, we get an induced open-stratum identification

\varphi^{\vartheta}_{k,\sigma}:U_{s_{k}\sigma}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}.

(Here the right-hand side is the pullback of
Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}
along

\mu_{k}|_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}}
, identified tautologically with
Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}
.)

Using Notation
3.2.1
with
n+1
in place of
n
, let

\tau_{k\to n}^{Z_{n}}:Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}

be the permutation moving the
k
-th
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factor to the last position.
We define the
permutation-adapted open identification

\varphi^{\deg}_{k,\sigma}:=\tau_{k\to n}^{Z_{n}}\circ\varphi^{\vartheta}_{k,\sigma}:U_{s_{k}\sigma}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}.

Lemma 3.4.7

(Permutation-adapted collapse comparison for the
k
-degeneracy)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad 0\leq k\leq n-1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

Transport the target of

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)

along the permutation-adapted open identification

\varphi^{\deg}_{k,\sigma}:U_{s_{k}\sigma}\xrightarrow{\sim}Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}

of Notation
3.4.6
. Then the collapse map appearing in
Definition
2.4.4
identifies with

\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k}.

Moreover, under the same transport, the extra edge inserted in the degeneracy spine is the

k
-direction boundary map

\delta_{t_{k}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}\bigl(Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}\bigr)[-n-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\bigl(Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\bigr)[-n].

In particular, there is a canonical homotopy

\delta_{t_{k}}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k}\ \simeq\ \beta^{(n)}_{Z_{n},m}

by Lemma
3.2.6
.

Proof.

By construction,

\varphi^{\deg}_{k,\sigma}=\tau_{k\to n}^{Z_{n}}\circ\varphi^{\vartheta}_{k,\sigma}.

Hence transporting the target of
\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)
along

(\tau_{k\to n}^{Z_{n}})^{*}[n+1]
replaces the collapse map

\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m}

by

(\tau_{k\to n}^{Z_{n}})^{*}[-n-1]\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m}=\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k}

by Definition
3.2.3
(with
n+1
in place of
n
).

Under the same transport, the inserted inert boundary edge is the last-factor boundary moved to
the
k
-th position, hence is precisely
\delta_{t_{k}}
by Definition
3.2.5

(again with
n+1
in place of
n
).
The final homotopy is therefore exactly Lemma
3.2.6
.
∎

Lemma 3.4.8

(Degeneracy compatibility for
0\leq k\leq n-1
)
.

Let
n\geq 1
and

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z},\qquad 0\leq k\leq n-1.

After transporting along the canonical isomorphism of deformation pairs

\vartheta_{k,\sigma}:D(s_{k}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\operatorname{id}_{Z_{n}}\times\mu_{k})^{*}D(\sigma),

along the induced deepest-stratum identification, and along the permutation-adapted open
identification

\varphi^{\deg}_{k,\sigma}:U_{s_{k}\sigma}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}

of Notation
3.4.6
, the two
n
-simplices

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)\qquad\text{and}\qquad s_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

After transport along
\vartheta_{k,\sigma}
, the deepest stratum of
s_{k}\sigma
is identified
with
H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)
, and Definition
2.4.4
reads

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)=(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m},

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)=(\pi_{s_{k}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{k}\sigma,m}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k},

where the second formula uses the permutation-adapted open identification

\varphi^{\deg}_{k,\sigma}
and Lemma
3.4.7
.

It is enough to establish the two auxiliary homotopies

(3.4.8.a)

(\pi_{s_{k}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}

and

(3.4.8.b)

\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{k}\sigma,m}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k}\ \simeq\ s_{k}\bigl(\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\bigr)

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr)_{n}.

Assuming these for the moment, we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{s_{k}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{k}\sigma,m}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k}

by Definition
2.4.4

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{k}\sigma,m}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k}

by (
3.4.8.a
)

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ s_{k}\bigl(\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\bigr)

by (
3.4.8.b
)

\displaystyle\simeq s_{k}\bigl((\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\bigr)

by simpliciality of whiskering

\displaystyle=s_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)

\displaystyle\text{by Definition\penalty\ \ref{def:closed-pullback-simplex}}.

It remains to justify (
3.4.8.a
) and
(
3.4.8.b
).

For (
3.4.8.a
), under
\vartheta_{k,\sigma}
the deepest-stratum vector
bundle projection for
s_{k}\sigma
identifies with the projection
\pi_{\sigma}
for
\sigma
.
Hence contractibility of inverse data for vector bundle projections yields the claimed homotopy.

For (
3.4.8.b
), set

K:=\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr).

Consider the two
n
-simplices

F:=\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{k}\sigma,m}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k}\in K_{n},\qquad F^{\prime}:=s_{k}\bigl(\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\bigr)\in K_{n}.

By Lemma
1.5.1
, the ordered SNC divisor for
s_{k}\sigma
is
obtained from that for
\sigma
by splitting the
k
-th boundary component into two
consecutive ones. Equivalently, on the spine data defining the coherent total boundary simplex,

\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{k}\sigma,m}

is obtained from

\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}

by inserting one extra boundary edge at position
k
.
Under the permutation-adapted open identification
\varphi^{\deg}_{k,\sigma}
, this extra edge is
precisely the
k
-direction boundary map

\delta_{t_{k}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}\bigl(Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n+1}\bigr)[-n-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}\bigl(Z_{n}\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\bigr)[-n]

by Lemma
3.4.7
.
After precomposition by
\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k}
, this inserted pair becomes

\delta_{t_{k}}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m;k},

which is canonically homotopic to
\beta^{(n)}_{Z_{n},m}
by
Lemma
3.2.6
. Hence the restrictions

F|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad F^{\prime}|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}

are canonically homotopic in
\Map({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},K)
.

Now consider the restriction map

p:\Map(\Delta^{n},K)\longrightarrow\Map({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},K).

Since
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}
is inner anodyne and
K
is Kan,
p
is a trivial
Kan fibration. Therefore the homotopy between
F|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}
and
F^{\prime}|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}
lifts to a
path from
F
to some
n
-simplex
\widetilde{F}
with

p(\widetilde{F})=p(F^{\prime}).

The fiber over
p(F^{\prime})
is contractible, so
\widetilde{F}
and
F^{\prime}
are canonically connected
by a path in that fiber. Concatenating these two paths yields a canonical homotopy

F\simeq F^{\prime}\qquad\text{in }K_{n},

which is exactly (
3.4.8.b
).
∎

3.4.9.
Case
k=n

Lemma 3.4.10

(Degeneracy compatibility for
k=n
)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad n\geq 1,\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z}.

After transporting along the canonical isomorphism of deformation pairs

\vartheta_{n,\sigma}:D(s_{n}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\operatorname{id}_{Z_{n}}\times\mu_{n})^{*}D(\sigma)

of Lemma
1.5.1
, and along the induced identifications of deepest
and open strata, the two
n
-simplices

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{n}\sigma)\qquad\text{and}\qquad s_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

After transport along
\vartheta_{n,\sigma}
, the deepest stratum of
s_{n}\sigma
is identified
with
H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)
, and the open stratum is identified with

U_{s_{n}\sigma}\simeq U_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}.

Thus Definition
2.4.4
gives

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)=(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m},\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{n}\sigma)=(\pi_{s_{n}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{s_{n}\sigma,m}.

It is enough to establish the two auxiliary homotopies

(3.4.10.a)

(\pi_{s_{n}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\ \simeq\ (\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}

and

(3.4.10.b)

\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ s_{n-1}\bigl(\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\bigr)

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr)_{n}.

Assuming these for the moment, we compute

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{n}\sigma)

\displaystyle=(\pi_{s_{n}\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{s_{n}\sigma,m}

by Definition
2.4.4

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{s_{n}\sigma,m}

by (
3.4.10.a
)

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ s_{n-1}\bigl(\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\bigr)

by (
3.4.10.b
)

\displaystyle\simeq s_{n-1}\bigl((\pi_{\sigma}^{*})^{-1}\circ\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\bigr)

by simpliciality of whiskering

\displaystyle=s_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr)

\displaystyle\text{by Definition\penalty\ \ref{def:closed-pullback-simplex}}.

It remains to justify (
3.4.10.a
) and
(
3.4.10.b
).

For (
3.4.10.a
), under
\vartheta_{n,\sigma}
the deepest-stratum vector
bundle projection for
s_{n}\sigma
identifies with the projection
\pi_{\sigma}
for
\sigma
.
Hence contractibility of inverse data for vector bundle projections yields the claimed homotopy.

For (
3.4.10.b
), set

K:=\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr),

and consider the two
n
-simplices

F:=\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\in K_{n},\qquad F^{\prime}:=s_{n-1}\bigl(\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}\bigr)\in K_{n}.

By Lemma
1.5.1
, the ordered SNC divisor for
s_{n}\sigma
is
obtained from that for
\sigma
by appending one final inert boundary component. Under the
identification

U_{s_{n}\sigma}\simeq U_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m},

the first step in the localization tower defining

\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{s_{n}\sigma,m}
is therefore the last-factor boundary map

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}:\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}(U_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-n-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n].

Moreover, under the same identification,

\beta_{s_{n}\sigma,m}=\beta^{(n+1)}_{Z_{n},m},\qquad\beta_{\sigma,m}=\beta^{(n)}_{Z_{n},m}.

Hence Axiom
(wBM12)
gives a canonical homotopy

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ\beta_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\ \simeq\ \beta_{\sigma,m}.

Now the spine of
F
is obtained from the spine of

\underline{\partial}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\circ\beta_{\sigma,m}

by replacing its initial edge
\beta_{\sigma,m}
with the two-edge segment

\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n})\xrightarrow{\ \beta_{s_{n}\sigma,m}\ }\mathcal{G}_{m+n+1}(U_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-n-1]\xrightarrow{\ \delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\ }\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n],

whereas the spine of
F^{\prime}
is obtained by appending the two-edge segment

\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n})\xrightarrow{\ \operatorname{id}\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n})\xrightarrow{\ \beta_{\sigma,m}\ }\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n].

On all earlier edges the two spines agree strictly, and on the final two-edge segment they are
canonically homotopic by the previous paragraph. Therefore the restrictions

F|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad F^{\prime}|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}

are canonically homotopic in
\Map({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},K)
.

Consider the restriction map

p:\Map(\Delta^{n},K)\longrightarrow\Map({\mathbf{Spine}}_{n},K).

Since
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}
is inner anodyne and
K
is Kan,
p
is a trivial
Kan fibration. Hence the homotopy between
F|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}
and
F^{\prime}|_{{\mathbf{Spine}}_{n}}
lifts to a
path from
F
to some
n
-simplex
\widetilde{F}
with

p(\widetilde{F})=p(F^{\prime}).

The fiber over
p(F^{\prime})
is contractible, so
\widetilde{F}
and
F^{\prime}
are canonically connected
by a path in that fiber. Concatenating these two paths yields a canonical homotopy

F\simeq F^{\prime}\qquad\text{in }K_{n},

which is exactly (
3.4.10.b
).
∎

Theorem 3.4.11

(Degeneracy compatibility)
.

Let
n\geq 1
and

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n},\qquad m\in\mathbf{Z},\qquad 0\leq k\leq n.

After transporting along the canonical isomorphism of deformation pairs

\vartheta_{k,\sigma}:D(s_{k}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\operatorname{id}_{Z_{n}}\times\mu_{k})^{*}D(\sigma),

and the induced deepest-stratum identification, and using:

•

for
0\leq k\leq n-1
, the permutation-adapted open identification

\varphi^{\deg}_{k,\sigma}
of Notation
3.4.6
,

•

for
k=n
, the canonical product identification

U_{s_{n}\sigma}\simeq U_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}

of Lemma
1.5.2
,

the two
n
-simplices

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\begin{cases}s_{k}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr),&0\leq k\leq n-1,\\[5.69054pt]
s_{n-1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr),&k=n,\end{cases}

of the Kan complex

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)

are canonically homotopic.

Proof.

If
0\leq k\leq n-1
, this is Lemma
3.4.8
.
If
k=n
, this is Lemma
3.4.10
.
∎

Example 3.4.12

(Degeneracy compatibility in low degrees in the motivic setting)
.

Work in the motivic example of the dual weak formalism, with values in
\mathrm{SH}(S)
, and fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
.

(1)

Degree
n=1
: degeneracies give constant
1
-simplices.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\xhookrightarrow{i}Z_{1})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{1}.

Then

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)=i_{m}^{*}

is a
0
-simplex in

\Map_{\mathrm{SH}(S)}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{1}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr).

The two degeneracies are

s_{0}\sigma=(Z_{0}\xhookrightarrow{\operatorname{id}}Z_{0}\xhookrightarrow{i}Z_{1}),\qquad s_{1}\sigma=(Z_{0}\xhookrightarrow{i}Z_{1}\xhookrightarrow{\operatorname{id}}Z_{1}).

Theorem
3.4.11
says that

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{0}\sigma)\simeq s_{0}(i_{m}^{*}),\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{1}\sigma)\simeq s_{0}(i_{m}^{*}),

so both are constant
1
-simplices at the morphism
i^{*}
.

Geometrically, the repeated immersion inserts one extra inert boundary step in the localization tower. After
precomposition by the collapse map, this extra step is canceled by Axiom
(wBM12)
. Thus the
additional simplicial direction carries no new information: it is just an identity edge.

(2)

Degree
n=2
: degeneracies give degenerate
2
-simplices.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z_{1}\hookrightarrow Z_{2})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{2}.

Then

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\in\Map_{\mathrm{SH}(S)}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{2}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)_{1}

is a
1
-simplex, hence a homotopy between its two endpoints.

For each degeneracy
s_{k}\sigma
with
k=0,1,2
, Theorem
3.4.11
identifies

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)

with the corresponding simplicial degeneracy of
\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)
. In particular:

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{0}\sigma)\simeq s_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr),\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{1}\sigma)\simeq s_{1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr),\qquad\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{2}\sigma)\simeq s_{1}\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr).

Thus each
\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(s_{k}\sigma)
is a degenerate
2
-simplex: one of its simplicial directions is
constant, and the
2
-simplex contains no information beyond the original
1
-simplex

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)
.

Geometrically, the panelization map
\mu_{k}
duplicates one deformation parameter. On the ordered SNC divisor, this
splits one boundary component into two consecutive components, so the localization tower acquires one extra boundary
step. After precomposition by the collapse map, that extra step cancels by
Axiom
(wBM12)
, and the resulting
2
-simplex is exactly the corresponding simplicial
degeneracy of
\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\sigma)
.

3.4.13
Transition to rigidification.

The preceding section proves the face and degeneracy identities for the assignment

\sigma\longmapsto\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\sigma)

only canonically up to contractible choice.
We now organize these comparison data coherently and rigidify them to produce a strict simplicial map

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{m}:N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}.

4.
Rigidification of simplicial identities

4.1.
Simplex-coordinate preliminaries

Notations 4.1.1

(Simplex coordinates in degree
m
)
.

For
\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}
with
n\geq 1
, let

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\sigma)\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n}),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\bigr)_{n-1}

be as in Definition
2.4.4
, and let

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma):\Delta^{n}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

be the corresponding
n
-simplex.
Thus the simplex-coordinate convention is now
reversed
with respect to the geometric flag:
the source vertex of
\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)
is
\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{n})
and the target vertex is
\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})
.
For
n=0
, we set trivially

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma):=\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z)\in\mathcal{C}_{0}.

Convention 4.1.2

(Opposite-simplex indexing)
.

Let

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow Z_{1}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n})\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}

be a geometric flag. We write
\sigma^{\operatorname{op}}
for the corresponding simplex of

N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
. Under the simplex-coordinate convention of
Notation
4.1.1
, the
a
-th simplex-coordinate vertex of

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)
corresponds to the geometric vertex
Z_{n-a}
.

Consequently, for cofaces and codegeneracies one has

\sigma^{\operatorname{op}}\circ\delta^{a}=(d_{n-a}\sigma)^{\operatorname{op}},\qquad\sigma^{\operatorname{op}}\circ\varsigma^{a}=(s_{n-a}\sigma)^{\operatorname{op}}.

Equivalently, when statements are written in terms of the geometric flag
\sigma
,
the geometric operators
d_{i}
and
s_{j}
correspond respectively to the
simplex-coordinate operators
\delta^{n-i}
and
\varsigma^{n-j}
.

Remark 4.1.3
.

Throughout the remainder of this subsection, the integer
m\in\mathbf{Z}
fixed in
Notation
4.1.1
remains fixed. We rigidify the degree-
m
assignment

\sigma\longmapsto\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma).

Accordingly, all coherent choice spaces, matching objects, evaluation maps, and rigidifications
carry an implicit dependence on
m
.

4.1.4
Plan of §
4
.

We proceed in six steps.

(1)

Translate face and degeneracy compatibilities into simplex coordinates
(Corollaries
4.1.6
-
4.1.9
).

(2)

Define raw choice spaces
\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)
and the constant raw evaluation map
(Definition
4.2.1
, Lemma
4.2.4
).

(3)

Build operator-comparison complexes and transported comparison data
(Lemmas
4.2.8
-
4.2.9
).

(4)

Refine the raw choice spaces to coherent choice spaces by means of finite skeletal
sections and an inverse-limit construction
(Lemma
4.2.18
, Proposition
4.2.20
).

(5)

Prove that the global projection

p_{m}:E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}\to N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}

has the required boundary-lifting property, and use this to choose a global coherent
section extending the prescribed
1
-skeleton
(Proposition
4.2.23
,
Proposition
4.2.24
).

(6)

Extract coherent operator comparisons from the global section and use them to construct
the strict rigidification of the closed-immersion pullback package by skeletal induction
(Lemma
4.3.2
,
Theorem
4.4.8
,
Theorem
4.5.5
).

Remark 4.1.5

(Relation to the literature)
.

The rigidification result proved in this subsection is formally quite general.
We do not know a reference in the literature that states
exactly
Theorem
4.4.8
in the
form needed here, so the argument is included for completeness.

The general philosophy is however classical: one replaces homotopy-coherent simplexwise data by a
strict simplicial map after organizing the relevant comparison choices into contractible spaces and
proving suitable extension or lifting statements. Our construction is close in spirit to several
well-known rigidification and rectification results, notably:

•

Cordier-Porter on categories of homotopy coherent diagrams

[
CP86
]
,

•

Dugger-Spivak on rigidification of quasi-categories

[
DS11
]
,

•

the homotopy coherent nerve viewpoint, especially the cubical boundary-extension
description in Kerodon, Corollary 2.4.6.13, together with Riehl’s exposition

[
Lur24
,
Rie23
]
,

•

Riehl-Verity on homotopically unique extensions, where the relevant spaces of
extensions are contractible Kan complexes

[
RV16
]
,

•

and the rectification statement in Kerodon, Corollary 5.6.5.18 and
Remark 5.6.5.19

[
Lur24
]
.

Thus the subsection should be viewed as a self-contained rigidification argument adapted to the
specific coherent choice spaces arising from the higher closed-immersion pullback simplices.

Corollary 4.1.6

(Face comparisons in simplex coordinates)
.

Let
n\geq 1
and
\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}
. For each
0\leq i\leq n
, there exists an equivalence

\varepsilon_{i,m}^{\sigma}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(d_{i}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\delta^{\,n-i})^{*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)

in the Kan complex
\Fun(\Delta^{n-1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
.

If
n\geq 2
, then after fixing one such choice for each
(\sigma,i)
, for every

0\leq i<j\leq n
, the two induced equivalences from

\mathcal{G}_{m}(d_{i}d_{j}\sigma)=\mathcal{G}_{m}(d_{j-1}d_{i}\sigma)

to the common codimension-
2
restriction of
\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)
are connected by a
chosen homotopy.

Proof.

If
n=1
, the claim is tautological from the convention of
Notation
4.1.1
: the two restrictions

\delta^{0,*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma),\qquad\delta^{1,*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)

are respectively the target and source vertices of the
1
-simplex
\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)
, hence identify
canonically with
\mathcal{G}_{m}(d_{1}\sigma)
and
\mathcal{G}_{m}(d_{0}\sigma)
.

Assume now that
n\geq 2
.

This is the translation into simplex coordinates of:

•

Lemma
3.3.9
for the initial face,

•

Lemma
3.3.3
together with
Lemma
3.3.7
for the internal faces,

•

and Corollary
3.3.12
for the terminal face,

using Notation
4.1.1
, Remark
3.3.15
, and the standard identification between

(n-1)
-simplices in a mapping space and
n
-simplices in
\mathcal{C}
with fixed endpoints.

By Convention
4.1.2
, because the accepted simplicial
package is contravariant, the geometric face
d_{i}\sigma
corresponds to the
simplex-coordinate face
\delta^{n-i}
. In particular, the terminal geometric face

d_{n}\sigma
is encoded by the initial simplex-coordinate face
\delta^{0}
, together
with the right-whiskering comparison of Corollary
3.3.12
.

The codimension-
2
compatibility follows by iterating these comparison equivalences and using that
all comparison spaces involved are contractible.
∎

Notations 4.1.7
.

For the
j
-th codegeneracy in
\Delta
, we write

\varsigma^{j}:[n+1]\to[n].

Lemma 4.1.8

(Degeneracy comparison for a vertex)
.

Let
\sigma=(Z)\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{0}
. Then there is an equivalence

\mathcal{G}_{m}(s_{0}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\varsigma^{0})^{*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)

in the Kan complex
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
.

Proof.

Write
\tau:=s_{0}\sigma=(\operatorname{id}_{Z}:Z\hookrightarrow Z)
.
Using the explicit description of the deformation space of the identity immersion, one identifies

D(\tau)\simeq Z\times\mathbb{A}^{1},\qquad H_{0}(\tau)\simeq Z,\qquad U_{\tau}\simeq Z\times\mathbb{G}_{m},\qquad\pi_{\tau}=\operatorname{id}_{Z}.

Hence Definition
2.4.4
gives

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{m}(\tau)=\delta_{t}\circ\beta^{(1)}_{Z,m}.

By Axiom
(wBM12)
,

\delta_{t}\circ\beta^{(1)}_{Z,m}\simeq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z)}.

Passing from mapping-space coordinates to simplex coordinates yields the required equivalence

\mathcal{G}_{m}(s_{0}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }s_{0}(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma))=(\varsigma^{0})^{*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma).

∎

Corollary 4.1.9

(Degeneracy comparisons in simplex coordinates)
.

Let
n\geq 0
,
\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}
, and
0\leq j\leq n
.
Then there exists an equivalence

\upsilon_{j,m}^{\sigma}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(s_{j}\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\varsigma^{\,n-j})^{*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)

in the Kan complex
\Fun(\Delta^{n+1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
.

Proof.

If
n=0
, this is Lemma
4.1.8
.
For
n\geq 1
, this is the translation of Theorem
3.4.11
from mapping-space
coordinates to simplex coordinates via Notation
4.1.1
and the reversed source/target convention. By Convention
4.1.2
, the geometric degeneracy

s_{j}\sigma
corresponds to the simplex-coordinate codegeneracy

\varsigma^{n-j}
. In particular, the terminal geometric degeneracy
s_{n}\sigma

is encoded by
\varsigma^{0}
.

∎

4.2.
Raw and coherent choice spaces

Definition 4.2.1

(Raw choice space in degree
m
)
.

Let

\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}.

If
n=0
, write
\sigma=(Z_{0})
. We define

\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma):=\Delta^{0},

with unique basepoint

\theta_{\sigma,m}\in\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)_{0}.

Assume henceforth that
n\geq 1
, and write

\sigma=(Z_{0}\hookrightarrow\cdots\hookrightarrow Z_{n}).

Write

\pi_{\sigma}:H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\longrightarrow Z_{0}

for the deepest-stratum vector bundle projection.

(1) Inverse-data choices for
\pi_{\sigma}^{*}
.

Let

\operatorname{Inv}_{m}(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})

denote the Kan complex of inverse-equivalence data for the equivalence

\pi_{\sigma}^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z_{0})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr).

By Lemma
2.1.12
,
\operatorname{Inv}_{m}(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})
is a contractible Kan complex.

(2) Spine-filler choices for the total boundary simplex.

Let

\operatorname{Fill}_{m}(\sigma)

be the contractible Kan complex of spine fillers used in the construction of

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\Bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(U_{\sigma})[-n],\,\mathcal{G}_{m}\bigl(H_{[n-1]}(\sigma)\bigr)\Bigr)_{n-1}.

(3) Definition of
\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)
.

Define

\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma):=\operatorname{Inv}_{m}(\pi_{\sigma}^{*})\times\operatorname{Fill}_{m}(\sigma).

Then
\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)
is a contractible Kan complex.

(4) Role of the raw choice space.

A vertex

\theta=(\theta^{\operatorname{Inv}},\theta^{\operatorname{filler}})\in\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)_{0}

records:

•

a choice of inverse data for
\pi_{\sigma}^{*}
,

•

and a choice of filler for the spine defining

{\underline{\partial}}^{\operatorname{simp}}_{\sigma,m}
.

These raw choices are used only as bookkeeping devices in the later coherent-choice
construction. They do
not
define a varying simplex of
\mathcal{C}
: the evaluated simplex
attached to
\sigma
remains the fixed simplex

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma):\Delta^{n}\to\mathcal{C}

of Notation
4.1.1
.

(5) Chosen raw basepoint.

By contractible choice, choose a basepoint

\theta_{\sigma,m}\in\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)_{0}.

Remark 4.2.2
.

The purpose of
\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)
is to parametrize contractible auxiliary data attached to

\sigma
, not to produce a varying simplex of
\mathcal{C}
. The actual simplex attached to

\sigma
remains the fixed one

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma):\Delta^{n}\to\mathcal{C}.

This is why the raw evaluation map of Lemma
4.2.4
is constant in the choice variable.

Remark 4.2.3

(Dictionary of choice spaces)
.

For a simplex
\sigma
, we use:

•

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)
for the fixed simplex of
\mathcal{C}
,

•

\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)
for the raw contractible space of inverse-data/filler choices attached to it,

•

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma)
for the
N
-truncated coherent enhancement,

•

and
\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)
for the inverse limit of all truncated coherent enhancements.

Lemma 4.2.4

(Raw evaluation map)
.

For every simplex
\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}
, there is a canonical simplicial map

q^{\operatorname{raw}}_{\sigma,m}:\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}

which is constant with value

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)\in\Fun(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{0}.

In particular,

q^{\operatorname{raw}}_{\sigma,m}(\theta_{\sigma,m})=\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma).

Proof.

This is the unique constant simplicial map with value

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)\in\Fun(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{0}.

∎

Lemma 4.2.5
.

In
\operatorname{sSet}
, every monomorphism is a retract of a transfinite composition of pushouts
of coproducts of the boundary inclusions

\partial\Delta^{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}\qquad(n\geq 0),

where
\partial\Delta^{n}
is the simplicial boundary of Notation
2.1.2
.

Proof.

This is the usual skeletal-attachment argument in
\operatorname{sSet}
.
For
X\in\operatorname{sSet}
, let
\sk_{n}(X)\subset X
be the subobject generated by simplices of degree
\leq n

under degeneracies; then
X=\operatorname{colim}_{n}\sk_{n}(X)
and
\sk_{n-1}(X)\hookrightarrow\sk_{n}(X)
is mono.
Choosing representatives of nondegenerate
n
-simplices yields a pushout presentation
of
\sk_{n}(X)
from
\sk_{n-1}(X)
by attaching copies of
\Delta^{n}
along
\partial\Delta^{n}
.
The relative-skeleton construction for a mono
A\hookrightarrow B
gives the claimed generation
up to retract.
∎

Proposition 4.2.6

(Rigidification by a section)
.

Let
p:E\to K
be a morphism in
\operatorname{sSet}
. Assume that for every
n\geq 0
, every commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\Delta^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

admits a lift
\Delta^{n}\to E
. Then:

(1)

p
has the right lifting property with respect to all monomorphisms in
\operatorname{sSet}
.

(2)

For every monomorphism
i:B\hookrightarrow A
in
\operatorname{sSet}
, the induced map

(i^{*},p_{*}):\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(A,E)\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(B,E)\times_{\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(B,K)}\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(A,K)

is a trivial Kan fibration.

(3)

Consequently, for every monomorphism
i:B\hookrightarrow A
and every commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}

the simplicial set of lifts

\operatorname{Lift}_{(u,v)}(i,p):=\operatorname{Fib}_{(u,v)}\!\Bigl(\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(A,E)\xrightarrow{(i^{*},p_{*})}\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(B,E)\times_{\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(B,K)}\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(A,K)\Bigr)

is a contractible Kan complex.

(4)

In particular, the simplicial set of sections

\operatorname{Sec}(p):=\operatorname{Fib}_{\operatorname{id}_{K}}\!\Bigl(\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(K,E)\xrightarrow{\,p\circ(-)\,}\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(K,K)\Bigr)

is a contractible Kan complex.

Proof.

(1) RLP against all monomorphisms.

By hypothesis,
p
lifts against
\partial\Delta^{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}
for every
n\geq 0
.
By Lemma
4.2.5
and closure of the right lifting property under pushouts,
transfinite compositions, and retracts,
p
lifts against all monomorphisms in
\operatorname{sSet}
.

(2) Relative mapping spaces.

Fix a monomorphism
i:B\hookrightarrow A
. To show that

(i^{*},p_{*}):\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(A,E)\to\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(B,E)\times_{\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(B,K)}\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(A,K)

is a trivial Kan fibration, it suffices to show that it lifts against every boundary inclusion

\partial\Delta^{r}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{r}
.
By adjunction, such a lifting problem is equivalent to a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A\times\partial\Delta^{r})\cup_{B\times\partial\Delta^{r}}(B\times\Delta^{r})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\times\Delta^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

The left vertical map is a monomorphism in
\operatorname{sSet}
, hence admits a lift by (1).

By the same skeletal-attachment argument as in (1), this shows that

(i^{*},p_{*})

has the right lifting property with respect to all monomorphisms in
\operatorname{sSet}
; hence it is a trivial Kan fibration.

(3) Contractible lifting spaces.

The displayed lifting space is the pullback of the map in (2) along the vertex

(u,v):\Delta^{0}\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(B,E)\times_{\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(B,K)}\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(A,K).

Since trivial Kan fibrations are stable under pullback, the induced map

\operatorname{Lift}_{(u,v)}(i,p)\longrightarrow\Delta^{0}

is a trivial Kan fibration. Therefore
\operatorname{Lift}_{(u,v)}(i,p)
is a contractible Kan complex.

(4) Sections.

This is the special case of (3) obtained by taking
B=\varnothing
,
A=K
, and

v=\operatorname{id}_{K}
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.7

(Spine fillers in a Kan complex)
.

Let
X
be a Kan complex and let
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{r}\subset\Delta^{r}
be the spine

(0\to 1\to\cdots\to r)
. Then restriction induces a trivial Kan fibration

\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{r},X)\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{r},X).

In particular, for every map
{\mathbf{Spine}}_{r}\to X
, the simplicial set of fillers

\operatorname{Fib}\!\Bigl(\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{r},X)\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{r},X)\Bigr)

is a contractible Kan complex.

Proof.

For
r\leq 1
, this is tautological. For
r\geq 2
, it is exactly
[
Lur24
,
Tag 00JA
]
: the inclusion

{\mathbf{Spine}}_{r}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{r}
is inner anodyne, hence restriction to
any Kan complex is a trivial Kan fibration.
∎

Lemma 4.2.8

(Operator comparison complexes)
.

Let
\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}
and let
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
be a simplicial operator.
Then there exists a contractible Kan complex

\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)

together with a simplicial map

c_{\sigma,\alpha,m}:\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\Bigl(\Delta^{1},\ \Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\Bigr)

such that every vertex of
\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)
determines an equivalence

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\alpha^{*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)

in
\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
.

Proof.

If
\alpha=\operatorname{id}_{[r]}
is an identity map, define

\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\operatorname{id}_{[r]}):=\Delta^{0}.

Let

c_{\sigma,\operatorname{id},m}:\Delta^{0}\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\Bigl(\Delta^{1},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\Bigr)

classify the degenerate edge

s_{0}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)\bigr):\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma).

Then the claim is immediate.

Assume henceforth that
\alpha\neq\operatorname{id}_{[r]}
. In particular, in the normal form below one has

\ell\geq 1
.

Write
\alpha
in standard normal form as

\alpha=g_{\ell}\circ\cdots\circ g_{1},

where

g_{t}:[r_{t-1}]\to[r_{t}]\qquad(1\leq t\leq\ell),

each
g_{t}
is either a coface or a codegeneracy, and

r_{0}=r,\qquad r_{\ell}=n.

For
0\leq t\leq\ell
, define the suffix operators

\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}:=g_{\ell}\circ\cdots\circ g_{t+1}:[r_{t}]\to[n],

with the conventions

\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}=\alpha,\qquad\widetilde{\alpha}_{\ell}=\operatorname{id}_{[n]}.

For each
t=1,\dots,\ell
, put

\tau_{t}:=\sigma\circ\widetilde{\alpha}_{t}\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{r_{t}}.

Choose once and for all one comparison equivalence supplied by the relevant
corollary as follows:

•

if
g_{t}=\delta^{a}:[r_{t-1}]\hookrightarrow[r_{t}]
, apply
Corollary
4.1.6
to the geometric face

d_{r_{t}-a}\tau_{t}
, which corresponds to the simplex-coordinate coface

\delta^{a}
;

•

if
g_{t}=\varsigma^{a}:[r_{t-1}]\twoheadrightarrow[r_{t}]
, apply
Corollary
4.1.9
to the geometric degeneracy

s_{r_{t}-a}\tau_{t}
, which corresponds to the simplex-coordinate codegeneracy

\varsigma^{a}
.

Pulling back along the fixed operator

g_{t-1}\circ\cdots\circ g_{1}:[r]\to[r_{t-1}]

transports this comparison to an edge in

\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}.

Let

P_{t,m}:=\Delta^{0},

and let

c_{t,m}:P_{t,m}\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\Bigl(\Delta^{1},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\Bigr)

classify this transported edge.

Set

P_{\sigma,\alpha,m}:=\prod_{t=1}^{\ell}P_{t,m}.

The product of the maps
c_{t,m}
induces a simplicial map

u_{\sigma,\alpha,m}:P_{\sigma,\alpha,m}\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{\ell},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}),

whose image is the spine

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\alpha^{*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)

in
\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
.

Define

\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha):=\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{\ell},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq})\times_{\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{\ell},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq})}P_{\sigma,\alpha,m},

where the fiber product is taken with respect to the restriction map

\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{\ell},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq})\to\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{\ell},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq})

and the map
u_{\sigma,\alpha,m}
.
By Lemma
4.2.7
, the restriction map is a trivial Kan fibration.
Since
P_{\sigma,\alpha,m}
is a contractible Kan complex,

\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)
is also a contractible Kan complex.

Finally, restriction along the long edge
[0\to\ell]\subset\Delta^{\ell}
defines the map

c_{\sigma,\alpha,m}:\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)\to\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\Bigl(\Delta^{1},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\Bigr),

and each vertex gives an equivalence

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)\xrightarrow{\sim}\alpha^{*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma).

∎

Lemma 4.2.9

(Parametrized comparison complexes)
.

Let
\sigma\in N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})_{n}
and let
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
be a simplicial operator.
Then there exist

(1)

a simplicial set

\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha),

(2)

a trivial Kan fibration

\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma,\alpha,m}:\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)\times\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma),

(3)

and a simplicial map

c^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma,\alpha,m}:\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\Bigl(\Delta^{1},\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\Bigr),

such that for every

y\in\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)_{0},\qquad x\in\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)_{0},

the fiber of
\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma,\alpha,m}
over
(y,x)
is a contractible Kan complex whose
vertices determine equivalences

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\alpha^{*}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)

in
\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
.

Proof.

Define

\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha):=\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)\times\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)\times\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma),

and let

\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma,\alpha,m}

be the projection to the first and third factors:

\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma,\alpha,m}(y,\xi,x):=(y,x).

Since
\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)
is a contractible Kan complex
(Lemma
4.2.8
), this projection is a trivial Kan fibration.

Define

c^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma,\alpha,m}:=c_{\sigma,\alpha,m}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2},

where

\operatorname{pr}_{2}:\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)\to\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha)

is the projection to the middle factor.

For any
(y,x)
, the fiber of
\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma,\alpha,m}
over
(y,x)
is canonically

\Comp^{\operatorname{op}}_{m}(\sigma,\alpha),

and its vertices determine the required equivalences by
Lemma
4.2.8
.
∎

4.2.10
Formal packaging of coherent choices.

The next construction packages
vertexwise
coherent choices.
Given simplicial choice spaces with strictly functorial pullback maps, it produces a simplicial set
whose simplices over
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to K
record coherent families of
vertices

in the local choice spaces attached to the faces of
\sigma
.
We use this only as a bookkeeping device for strict compatibility.
It should not be confused with a Grothendieck construction whose fibers recover the full local
simplicial sets.

Construction 4.2.11

(The coherent-choice simplicial set)
.

Let
K
be a simplicial set.
Assume given, for every simplex

\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to K,

a simplicial set

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma),

and for every simplicial operator
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
, a simplicial map

\alpha^{*}:\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha),

strictly functorial in
\alpha
.

Define a simplicial set
E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}
as follows.
For
n\geq 0
, an
n
-simplex of
E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}
is a pair

(\sigma,\eta),

where
\sigma\in K_{n}
and

\eta=\{\eta_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha:[r]\to[n]}

is a family with

\eta_{\alpha}\in\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)_{0}

for every
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
, satisfying the strict coherence condition

\beta^{*}(\eta_{\alpha})=\eta_{\alpha\circ\beta}\qquad\text{for every }\beta:[s]\to[r].

Faces and degeneracies are defined by precomposition:

d_{i}(\sigma,\eta):=\bigl(\sigma\circ\delta^{i},\{\eta_{\delta^{i}\circ\alpha}\}_{\alpha}\bigr),\qquad s_{j}(\sigma,\eta):=\bigl(\sigma\circ\varsigma^{j},\{\eta_{\varsigma^{j}\circ\alpha}\}_{\alpha}\bigr).

The strict functoriality of pullback implies that this defines a simplicial set.

Let

p_{m}:E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}\longrightarrow K,\qquad(\sigma,\eta)\longmapsto\sigma

be the evident projection.

Remark 4.2.12
.

Construction
4.2.11
applied to our setting, so
K=N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
,
produces the simplicial set

E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}

and its projection

p_{m}:E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}\to N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}},

but not a canonical simplicial map

E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}\to\mathcal{C}.

Thus the results that follow concern coherent sections of
p_{m}
. The passage from such a section
to a strict simplicial map

N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}

will be obtained later by a separate rigidification argument.

Notations 4.2.13
.

For
K=N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
and the coherent choice spaces in degree
m
, let

E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}},\qquad p_{m}:E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}\to N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}

denote the simplicial set produced by Construction
4.2.11

and its projection.

Notations 4.2.14

(Truncated overcategory)
.

For integers
n,N\geq 0
, let

J_{n,N}\subset\Delta_{/[n]}

be the full subcategory spanned by the maps
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
with
r\leq N
, and set

S_{n,N}:=N(J_{n,N}).

Notations 4.2.15

(Source degrees of the vertices of a nondegenerate simplex)
.

Let

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\xleftarrow{\beta_{2}}\cdots\xleftarrow{\beta_{r}}\alpha_{r}\bigr)

be a nondegenerate simplex of some
S_{n,N}
.
For
0\leq i\leq r
, write

\operatorname{src}_{i}(\lambda)

for the source degree of
\alpha_{i}
, and set

\operatorname{src}(\lambda):=\operatorname{src}_{r}(\lambda)

for the source degree of the last vertex of
\lambda
.

Lemma 4.2.16

(Local coherent choice complexes over the truncated overcategory)
.

Fix
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
and
N\geq 0
.
For every simplex

\lambda:\Delta^{r}\longrightarrow S_{n,N}

there exist

(1)

a contractible Kan complex

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda),

(2)

for every simplicial operator
\tau:[k]\to[r]
, a simplicial map

\tau^{*}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ\tau),

strictly functorial in
\tau
,

(3)

for every nondegenerate simplex

\lambda:\Delta^{r}\longrightarrow S_{n,N},

a simplicial map

\varepsilon_{\lambda}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\bigl(\Delta^{r},\Fun(\Delta^{\operatorname{src}(\lambda)},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\bigr).

with the following additional properties.

(a)

If
\lambda=\alpha:[d]\to[n]
is a vertex, then

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\alpha)=\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha),

and

\varepsilon_{\alpha}=q^{\operatorname{raw}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha,m}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\alpha)\longrightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{d},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}.

(b)

If

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\bigr)

is a nondegenerate
1
-simplex, then

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda):=\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1}),

the pullback maps along

\delta^{0},\delta^{1}:[0]\to[1]

are the first and second coordinate maps of

\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1},m}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{1})\times\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0}),

and

\varepsilon_{\lambda}=c^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1},m}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\bigl(\Delta^{1},\Fun(\Delta^{\operatorname{src}(\lambda)},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\bigr).

(c)

If

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\xleftarrow{\beta_{2}}\cdots\xleftarrow{\beta_{r}}\alpha_{r}\bigr)

is nondegenerate of dimension
r\geq 2
,
then there exist:

(a)

a contractible Kan complex

M_{\lambda},

(b)

a trivial Kan fibration

\pi_{\lambda}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\longrightarrow M_{\lambda},

(c)

and a simplicial map

\varepsilon_{\lambda}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\bigl(\Delta^{r},\Fun(\Delta^{\operatorname{src}(\lambda)},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\bigr),

such that for every
b\in M_{\lambda}
, the fiber
\pi_{\lambda}^{-1}(b)
is exactly

\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}\!\bigl(\Delta^{r},\Fun(\Delta^{\operatorname{src}(\lambda)},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\bigr)\times_{\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{r},\Fun(\Delta^{\operatorname{src}(\lambda)},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq})}\{u_{\lambda}(b)\},

where
u_{\lambda}(b)
is the spine determined by the consecutive edge data of
b
.

Proof.

Every simplex
\mu
of
S_{n,N}
has a unique factorization

\mu=\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}\circ s_{\mu}

with
s_{\mu}
surjective and
\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}
nondegenerate.
We first define
\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)
and pullback maps for nondegenerate
\lambda
and
injective operators; then we extend to all simplices by nondegenerate factorization.

We argue by induction on the dimension of a nondegenerate simplex
\lambda
.

Dimension
0
.

If
\lambda=\alpha:[d]\to[n]
is a vertex, set

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\alpha):=\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha),\qquad\varepsilon_{\alpha}:=q^{\operatorname{raw}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha,m}.

This is a contractible Kan complex by Definition
4.2.1
.

Dimension
1
.

If

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\bigr)

is nondegenerate, set

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda):=\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1}).

By Lemma
4.2.9
, the map

\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1},m}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\to\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{1})\times\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0})

is a trivial Kan fibration over a contractible Kan base; hence

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)
is a contractible Kan complex.
Define
(\delta^{0})^{*}
and
(\delta^{1})^{*}
to be the two coordinate maps of this projection,
and define
\varepsilon_{\lambda}
to be

c^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1},m}.

Dimension
r\geq 2
.

Let

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\xleftarrow{\beta_{2}}\cdots\xleftarrow{\beta_{r}}\alpha_{r}\bigr)

be nondegenerate of dimension
r\geq 2
.
For every non-surjective simplicial operator

\tau:[k]\to[r],

the simplex
\lambda\circ\tau
has nondegenerate dimension
<r
. Hence the inductive
hypothesis already provides the contractible Kan complexes

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ\tau)

and their pullback maps for all simplices of
\partial\Delta^{r}
.

Apply the simplicial-set part of Construction
4.2.11
to

K=\partial\Delta^{r}

and the assignment

\tau\longmapsto\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ\tau)\qquad(\tau\text{ non-surjective}).

This yields a simplicial set

p^{\partial}_{\lambda}:E^{\partial}_{\lambda}\longrightarrow\partial\Delta^{r}.

We claim that
p^{\partial}_{\lambda}
has the right lifting property with respect to all boundary
inclusions. Let

\nu:\Delta^{s}\to\partial\Delta^{r}

be a simplex, and consider a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\Delta^{s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\partial}_{\lambda}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\Delta^{r}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\partial}_{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}

Set

\mu:=\lambda\circ\nu.

Since
\nu
is a simplex of
\partial\Delta^{r}
, it is non-surjective, hence
\mu
has
nondegenerate dimension
<r
.

Unwinding the definition of
E^{\partial}_{\lambda}
, the top horizontal map is exactly a coherent family
of vertices in the local complexes attached to the proper faces of
\mu
. To construct a lift, it
suffices to produce a vertex

\xi_{\mu}\in\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)_{0}

whose pullbacks along all non-surjective operators

\gamma:[k]\to[s]

recover the prescribed boundary family. Such a vertex exists by induction on the nondegenerate
dimension of
\mu
, as follows.

If
\mu
is nondegenerate of dimension
0
, then necessarily
s=0
. Hence the lifting problem
has empty boundary datum. Since

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)=\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\mu)

is nonempty, choose any vertex

\xi_{\mu}\in\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)_{0}.

If
\mu
is nondegenerate of dimension
1
, then

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)=\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1})

for a unique edge

\mu=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\bigr),

and the prescribed boundary family is exactly an endpoint pair in

\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{1})\times\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0}).

Since

\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1},m}

is a trivial Kan fibration, this endpoint pair admits a lift
\xi_{\mu}
.

If
\mu
is nondegenerate of dimension
t\geq 2
, then by the inductive construction of

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)
, the prescribed coherent boundary family is exactly a vertex of

M_{\mu}=\operatorname{Sec}(p^{\partial}_{\mu}).

Since

\pi_{\mu}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)\to M_{\mu}

is a trivial Kan fibration, this vertex lifts to a point

\xi_{\mu}\in\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)_{0}.

Finally, if
\mu
is degenerate, write

\mu=\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}\circ s_{\mu}

for its nondegenerate factorization. By definition,

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)=\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}),

and the pullback maps for
\mu
are defined through those of
\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}
. Hence the
prescribed boundary family for
\mu
induces a coherent boundary family for
\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}
,
and the previous cases provide a point

\xi_{\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}}\in\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu^{\mathrm{nd}})_{0}=\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)_{0}.

We take
\xi_{\mu}:=\xi_{\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}}
.

Using the vertex
\xi_{\mu}
and its pullbacks along all operators
[k]\to[s]
, we obtain the
required lift

\Delta^{s}\to E^{\partial}_{\lambda}.

Thus
p^{\partial}_{\lambda}
lifts against all boundary inclusions.

Proposition
4.2.6
(4) then implies that

M_{\lambda}:=\operatorname{Sec}(p^{\partial}_{\lambda})

is a contractible Kan complex.

Set

X_{\lambda}:=\Fun(\Delta^{\operatorname{src}(\lambda)},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}.

For
0\leq i\leq r
, write

\gamma_{i}:=\beta_{i+1}\cdots\beta_{r}:[\operatorname{src}(\lambda)]\to[\operatorname{src}_{i}(\lambda)],\qquad\gamma_{r}=\operatorname{id}_{[\operatorname{src}(\lambda)]}.

For
1\leq i\leq r
, let

e_{i}:[1]\hookrightarrow[r]

be the injective map with image
\{i-1,i\}
.
A point
b\in M_{\lambda}
is a coherent boundary section, so evaluating at
e_{i}
gives

b_{e_{i}}\in\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ e_{i}).

Applying
\varepsilon_{\lambda\circ e_{i}}
and then pulling back along
\gamma_{i}
gives an edge
in
X_{\lambda}
. The coherence of the boundary section implies that these
r
edges have
matching endpoints, hence assemble into a simplicial map

u_{\lambda}:M_{\lambda}\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{r},X_{\lambda}).

Define

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda):=M_{\lambda}\times_{\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{r},X_{\lambda})}\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{r},X_{\lambda}).

Let

\pi_{\lambda}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\to M_{\lambda}

be the first projection and

\varepsilon_{\lambda}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\to\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{r},X_{\lambda})

the second.
By Lemma
4.2.7
, the restriction map

\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\Delta^{r},X_{\lambda})\longrightarrow\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}({\mathbf{Spine}}_{r},X_{\lambda})

is a trivial Kan fibration. Therefore
\pi_{\lambda}
is a trivial Kan fibration, so

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)
is a contractible Kan complex, and its fiber over
b\in M_{\lambda}

is exactly the displayed filler complex.

For a proper injective simplicial operator

\iota:[k]\hookrightarrow[r],

define

\iota^{*}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ\iota)

to be the composite

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\xrightarrow{\ \pi_{\lambda}\ }M_{\lambda}=\operatorname{Sec}(p^{\partial}_{\lambda})\xrightarrow{\ \operatorname{Ev}_{\iota}\ }\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ\iota),

where
\operatorname{Ev}_{\iota}
is the simplicial map obtained as follows. Since

M_{\lambda}=\operatorname{Sec}(p^{\partial}_{\lambda})\subset\Map_{\operatorname{sSet}}(\partial\Delta^{r},E^{\partial}_{\lambda}),

evaluation at the simplex
\iota\in(\partial\Delta^{r})_{k}
gives a simplicial map

\mu_{\iota}:M_{\lambda}\longrightarrow(E^{\partial}_{\lambda})_{k}.

By the explicit definition of
E^{\partial}_{\lambda}
, a
k
-simplex of
E^{\partial}_{\lambda}

lying over
\iota
is a coherent family whose component indexed by

\operatorname{id}_{[k]}:[k]\to[k]

is a simplex of
\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ\iota)
. Projection to this component therefore
defines a simplicial map

\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{id}}:(E^{\partial}_{\lambda})_{k}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ\iota).

We set

\operatorname{Ev}_{\iota}:=\operatorname{pr}_{\operatorname{id}}\circ\mu_{\iota}.

For
\iota=\operatorname{id}_{[r]}
, set
\iota^{*}=\operatorname{id}
.
The simplicial identities follow immediately from the coherence relations satisfied by sections of

p^{\partial}_{\lambda}
.

Extension to degenerate simplices.

Now let
\mu
be an arbitrary simplex of
S_{n,N}
, with nondegenerate factorization

\mu=\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}\circ s_{\mu}.

Set

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu):=\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu^{\mathrm{nd}}).

If
\tau:[k]\to[r]
, factor

s_{\mu}\circ\tau=\iota_{\mu,\tau}\circ s_{\mu\circ\tau}

with
s_{\mu\circ\tau}
surjective and
\iota_{\mu,\tau}
injective.
Define

\tau^{*}:=\iota_{\mu,\tau}^{*}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\mu\circ\tau).

Uniqueness of the epi-mono factorization in
\Delta
implies strict functoriality in
\tau
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.17

(Inverse limits of contractible fibrant towers)
.

Let

X_{0}\xleftarrow{p_{1}}X_{1}\xleftarrow{p_{2}}X_{2}\xleftarrow{}\cdots

be a tower in
\operatorname{sSet}
such that each
X_{N}
is a contractible Kan complex and each

p_{N+1}
is a trivial Kan fibration. Then the inverse limit

X_{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{N}X_{N}

is a contractible Kan complex, and each projection

X_{\infty}\longrightarrow X_{N}

is a trivial Kan fibration.

Proof.

For each
N\geq 0
, consider the tail tower

X_{N}\xleftarrow{p_{N+1}}X_{N+1}\xleftarrow{p_{N+2}}X_{N+2}\xleftarrow{}\cdots.

Since each transition map is a trivial Kan fibration,
[
Lur24
,
Tag 01C8
]

implies that the canonical projection

X_{\infty}=\varprojlim_{k\geq N}X_{k}\longrightarrow X_{N}

is a trivial Kan fibration.

In particular, taking
N=0
, the simplicial set
X_{\infty}
is a Kan complex. Since

X_{\infty}\longrightarrow X_{0}

is a weak homotopy equivalence and
X_{0}
is contractible, it follows that
X_{\infty}
is weakly
contractible. Being also a Kan complex,
X_{\infty}
is therefore contractible.

Hence every projection

X_{\infty}\longrightarrow X_{N}

is a trivial Kan fibration, and
X_{\infty}
is a contractible Kan complex.
∎

Lemma 4.2.18

(Finite coherent enhancement over a truncated overcategory)
.

With notation as in
4.2.14
, there exists a simplicial set

p_{\sigma,N}:E_{\sigma,N}\longrightarrow S_{n,N}

with the following properties.

(1)

p_{\sigma,N}
has the right lifting property with respect to all boundary inclusions

\partial\Delta^{r}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{r}.

(2)

For every vertex
\alpha\in S_{n,N,0}
, the set of vertices of the fiber of
p_{\sigma,N}

over
\alpha
is canonically identified with

\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)_{0}.

(3)

If

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\xleftarrow{\beta_{2}}\cdots\xleftarrow{\beta_{r}}\alpha_{r}\bigr)

is a nondegenerate
r
-simplex of
S_{n,N}
with
r\geq 1
, and if one fixes:

•

a vertex in the fiber of
p_{\sigma,N}
over each vertex
\alpha_{i}
,

•

and, for each consecutive edge

\alpha_{i-1}\xleftarrow{\beta_{i}}\alpha_{i},

a point of the corresponding transported comparison complex lying over the chosen endpoint vertices,

then the simplicial set of lifts of
\lambda
in
E_{\sigma,N}
whose restrictions to those
prescribed vertices and consecutive edges are the chosen ones is a contractible Kan complex.

(4)

The raw basepoints admit compatible sections on the
1
-skeleta: for every
N
there is a
section

s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)}:\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})\longrightarrow E_{\sigma,N}

whose value at a vertex
\alpha
is the raw basepoint

\theta_{\sigma\circ\alpha,m}\in\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha),

and such that for every
N^{\prime}\geq N
,

s_{\sigma,N^{\prime}}^{(1)}|_{\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})}=s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)}.

(5)

For every simplicial operator
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
with
r\leq N
, precomposition

J_{r,N}\longrightarrow J_{n,N},\qquad\beta\longmapsto\alpha\circ\beta

induces a simplicial map on section spaces

\alpha^{*}:\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma\circ\alpha,N}),

strictly functorial in
\alpha
.

Consequently, the section space

\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N})

is a contractible Kan complex.

Moreover, if
N^{\prime}\geq N
, then restriction along the full inclusion

S_{n,N}\hookrightarrow S_{n,N^{\prime}}

induces a trivial Kan fibration

\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N^{\prime}})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N}).

Proof.

Apply Lemma
4.2.16
.
For every simplex

\lambda:\Delta^{r}\to S_{n,N}

we obtain a contractible Kan complex
\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)
, together with strictly functorial
pullback maps along simplicial operators.

We assemble these local complexes into a simplicial set over
S_{n,N}
by the explicit formula of
Construction
4.2.11
. Thus an
r
-simplex of
E_{\sigma,N}
is a pair

(\lambda,\eta),

where
\lambda\in(S_{n,N})_{r}
and

\eta=\{\eta_{\tau}\}_{\tau:[k]\to[r]}

is a coherent family with

\eta_{\tau}\in\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda\circ\tau)_{0}

for every
\tau:[k]\to[r]
, satisfying

\beta^{*}(\eta_{\tau})=\eta_{\tau\circ\beta}\qquad\text{for every }\beta:[\ell]\to[k].

Faces and degeneracies are defined by precomposition, so the projection

p_{\sigma,N}:E_{\sigma,N}\longrightarrow S_{n,N},\qquad(\lambda,\eta)\longmapsto\lambda,

is a simplicial map by construction.

We verify the stated properties.

Property (1).

This is proved exactly as in the construction of
p^{\partial}_{\lambda}
in
Lemma
4.2.16
: for each simplex
\lambda
, the boundary datum
amounts to a coherent family of vertices in the local complexes of its proper faces, and the
definition of
\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)
was arranged precisely so that such boundary data admit
a lift.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\Delta^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{\sigma,N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{n,N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\sigma,N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}

be a commutative square.
Unwinding the definition of
E_{\sigma,N}
, the top map is exactly a coherent family of points in
the local complexes attached to the proper faces of
\lambda
.

If
\lambda
is a vertex, a lift is simply a choice of a point of

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)=\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\lambda),

which exists because this simplicial set is a contractible Kan complex.

If

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\bigr)

is nondegenerate of dimension
1
, then the boundary datum is a point of

\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{1})\times\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0}),

and a lift is precisely a point of

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)=\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1})

lying over that pair. Such a point exists because

\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1},m}

is a trivial Kan fibration.

If
\lambda
is nondegenerate of dimension
r\geq 2
, then the boundary datum is a point of the
contractible Kan complex
M_{\lambda}
from
Lemma
4.2.16
, and a lift is equivalently a point of

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)

lying over that boundary datum. Such a point exists because

\pi_{\lambda}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\to M_{\lambda}

is a trivial Kan fibration.

If
\lambda
is degenerate, the definition of the pullback maps through the nondegenerate factor of

\lambda
reduces the lifting problem to the corresponding problem for that nondegenerate factor.
Therefore
p_{\sigma,N}
has the right lifting property with respect to all boundary inclusions.

Property (2).

If
\alpha\in S_{n,N,0}
is a vertex, then by construction a
0
-simplex of

\operatorname{Fib}_{\alpha}(p_{\sigma,N})
is exactly a point of

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\alpha)=\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha).

Hence

\operatorname{Fib}_{\alpha}(p_{\sigma,N})_{0}\cong\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)_{0}.

Property (3).

Let

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\xleftarrow{\beta_{2}}\cdots\xleftarrow{\beta_{r}}\alpha_{r}\bigr)

be nondegenerate.

If
r=1
, then after fixing the two endpoint choices, the simplicial set of
1
-simplices over

\lambda
is exactly the fiber of

\pi^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1},m}:\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1})\to\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{1})\times\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0}),

which is a contractible Kan complex by
Lemma
4.2.9
.

Assume now
r\geq 2
.
Fix points in the fibers over the vertices and transported comparison data along the consecutive
edges. These choices determine a section over the spine

{\mathbf{Spine}}_{r}\longrightarrow E^{\partial}_{\lambda}

of the boundary object appearing in the proof of
Lemma
4.2.16
. Since

p^{\partial}_{\lambda}:E^{\partial}_{\lambda}\to\partial\Delta^{r}

lifts against all boundary inclusions, Proposition
4.2.6
(3), applied
to the monomorphism

{\mathbf{Spine}}_{r}\hookrightarrow\partial\Delta^{r},

shows that the simplicial set of extensions of this spine datum to a full boundary section
is a contractible Kan complex.
Pulling back the trivial Kan fibration

\pi_{\lambda}:\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)\longrightarrow M_{\lambda}=\operatorname{Sec}(p^{\partial}_{\lambda})

along that extension space gives a contractible Kan complex of simplices of
E_{\sigma,N}

lying over
\lambda
.

By definition, this is exactly the simplicial set of lifts of
\lambda
in
E_{\sigma,N}

with the prescribed vertex data and prescribed transported comparison data on the consecutive edges.

Property (4).

For every nondegenerate edge

\lambda=\bigl(\alpha_{0}\xleftarrow{\beta_{1}}\alpha_{1}\bigr)

of
N(\Delta_{/[n]})
, choose once and for all a point

\xi_{\lambda}\in\Comp^{{\operatorname{para}}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},\beta_{1})

whose endpoints are

\theta_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{1},m}\quad\text{and}\quad\theta_{\sigma\circ\alpha_{0},m}.

Such a point exists by Lemma
4.2.9
.

For each
N
, define
s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)}
on
\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})
by assigning to a vertex

\alpha
the raw basepoint
\theta_{\sigma\circ\alpha,m}
, and to a nondegenerate edge

\lambda
of
S_{n,N}
the previously chosen point
\xi_{\lambda}
.
Since
\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})
has no nondegenerate simplices of dimension
>1
, this defines a section

s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)}:\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})\longrightarrow E_{\sigma,N}.

By construction, these sections are strictly compatible under truncation:
if
N^{\prime}\geq N
, then

s_{\sigma,N^{\prime}}^{(1)}|_{\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})}=s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)}.

Property (5).

Let
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
with
r\leq N
. Precomposition

J_{r,N}\to J_{n,N},\qquad\beta\mapsto\alpha\circ\beta,

induces a simplicial map

S_{r,N}\longrightarrow S_{n,N}.

For every simplex
\lambda
of
S_{r,N}
, the inductive construction of

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(-)
is compatible with precomposition by
\alpha
, and gives a canonical
identification

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\alpha\circ\lambda)\cong\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma\circ\alpha,N}(\lambda),

strictly compatible with pullback maps.
Under these identifications, restriction of coherent families along

S_{r,N}\to S_{n,N}

defines the required simplicial map on section spaces:

\alpha^{*}:\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma\circ\alpha,N}),

and strict functoriality is immediate from strict functoriality on the local complexes.

Consequences.

The five properties above establish the enumerated assertions.

In particular, Property (1) and
Proposition
4.2.6
(4)
imply that

\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N})

is a contractible Kan complex.

Now let
N^{\prime}\geq N
. For every simplex

\lambda:\Delta^{r}\to S_{n,N},

the local complex
\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N^{\prime}}(\lambda)
agrees with

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)
, since its definition depends only on

\lambda
and its faces. Hence, by the explicit formula of
Construction
4.2.11
, there is a canonical isomorphism of simplicial sets over
S_{n,N}

i^{*}E_{\sigma,N^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\sim}E_{\sigma,N},

where
i:S_{n,N}\hookrightarrow S_{n,N^{\prime}}
is the inclusion.

Since
p_{\sigma,N^{\prime}}
satisfies (1),
Proposition
4.2.6
(2)
applied to
i
yields a trivial Kan fibration

\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N^{\prime}})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N}).

∎

Remark 4.2.19
.

Conceptually, the construction of

p_{\sigma,N}:E_{\sigma,N}\to S_{n,N}

proceeds in two steps.
First, one defines for each simplex
\lambda
of
S_{n,N}
a contractible Kan complex

\operatorname{Loc}_{\sigma,N}(\lambda)
of local coherent choices, together with strict pullback maps along
all simplicial operators
(Lemma
4.2.16
).
Second, one assembles these local complexes into the global simplicial set
E_{\sigma,N}
by the
explicit simplicial-set formula of Construction
4.2.11
.
In particular, the faces and degeneracies of
E_{\sigma,N}
are built into the construction,
rather than being imposed simplexwise afterwards.

Proposition 4.2.20

(Coherent enhancement of the raw simplexwise data)
.

For every simplex
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
, the raw choice space

\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)
admits a coherent enhancement: there exist

(1)

a contractible Kan complex

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma),

(2)

a vertex-evaluation map

\operatorname{Ev}_{\operatorname{id}}:\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)_{0}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)_{0},

obtained by evaluating a coherent section at the vertex

\operatorname{id}_{[n]}\in S_{n,n,0}
,

(3)

for every simplicial operator
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
, a simplicial map

\alpha^{*}:\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha),

strictly functorial in
\alpha
,

(4)

and a chosen basepoint

\theta^{\operatorname{coh}}_{\sigma,m}\in\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)_{0}

such that

\operatorname{Ev}_{\operatorname{id}}(\theta^{\operatorname{coh}}_{\sigma,m})=\theta_{\sigma,m}.

Consequently,

q^{\operatorname{raw}}_{\sigma,m}\bigl(\operatorname{Ev}_{\operatorname{id}}(\theta^{\operatorname{coh}}_{\sigma,m})\bigr)=\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma).

Proof.

Fix
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
. For every
N\geq n
, set

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma):=\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N}),

where
p_{\sigma,N}:E_{\sigma,N}\to S_{n,N}
is given by
Lemma
4.2.18
. By that lemma,

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma)

is a contractible Kan complex, and for
N^{\prime}\geq N
the restriction map

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N^{\prime}}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma)

is a trivial Kan fibration.

Define

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma):=\varprojlim_{N\geq n}\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma).

By Lemma
4.2.17
,

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)

is a contractible Kan complex, and each projection

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma)

is a trivial Kan fibration.

For later use, define the vertex-evaluation map

\operatorname{Ev}_{\operatorname{id}}:\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)_{0}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}_{m}(\sigma)_{0},\qquad(t_{N})_{N\geq n}\longmapsto t_{n}(\operatorname{id}_{[n]}).

This is well-defined because for
N^{\prime}\geq N\geq n
, the restriction map

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N^{\prime}}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma)

preserves the vertex
\operatorname{id}_{[n]}\in S_{n,N,0}\subset S_{n,N^{\prime},0}
.

Now let
\alpha:[r]\to[n]
. For every

N\geq\max\{n,r\},

Lemma
4.2.18
(5) provides a simplicial map

\alpha^{*}_{N}:\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma\circ\alpha),

strictly functorial in
\alpha
. These maps are compatible with the restriction maps in
N
.

Since the full subposet

\{\,N\in\mathbf{N}\mid N\geq\max\{n,r\}\,\}

is cofinal both in
\{\,N\mid N\geq n\,\}
and in
\{\,N\mid N\geq r\,\}
, passing to inverse
limits over this common cofinal subsystem yields a simplicial map

\alpha^{*}:\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha).

The strict functoriality of the maps
\alpha^{*}_{N}
implies the strict functoriality of the
resulting limit maps.

Finally, let

\Ext_{\sigma,N}:=\operatorname{Fib}_{\,s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)}}\!\Bigl(\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Sec}\bigl(p_{\sigma,N}|_{\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})}\bigr)\Bigr),

the simplicial set of global sections extending the prescribed
1
-skeleton section

s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)}
of Lemma
4.2.18
(4).
By Proposition
4.2.6
(3), applied to

\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})\hookrightarrow S_{n,N},

the simplicial set
\Ext_{\sigma,N}
is a contractible Kan complex.

Choose

t_{\sigma,n}\in\Ext_{\sigma,n,0}.

Assume inductively that

t_{\sigma,N}\in\Ext_{\sigma,N,0}

has been chosen. By the final paragraph of
Lemma
4.2.18
, we may identify

E_{\sigma,N+1}|_{S_{n,N}}=E_{\sigma,N}.

Hence
t_{\sigma,N}
may be viewed as a section of
p_{\sigma,N+1}
over
S_{n,N}
.
Since
t_{\sigma,N}
extends
s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)}
, and since

s_{\sigma,N+1}^{(1)}|_{\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})}=s_{\sigma,N}^{(1)},

the sections
t_{\sigma,N}
on
S_{n,N}
and
s_{\sigma,N+1}^{(1)}
on

\sk_{1}(S_{n,N+1})
glue to a section

u_{\sigma,N+1}:S_{n,N}\cup_{\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})}\sk_{1}(S_{n,N+1})\longrightarrow E_{\sigma,N+1}.

Applying Proposition
4.2.6
(3) to the monomorphism

S_{n,N}\cup_{\sk_{1}(S_{n,N})}\sk_{1}(S_{n,N+1})\hookrightarrow S_{n,N+1},

we obtain a contractible Kan complex of extensions of
u_{\sigma,N+1}
to a section of

p_{\sigma,N+1}
. Choose one and denote it by

t_{\sigma,N+1}\in\Ext_{\sigma,N+1,0}.

In this way we obtain a compatible sequence

(t_{\sigma,N})_{N\geq n}\in\varprojlim_{N\geq n}\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N})=\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma).

Define

\theta^{\operatorname{coh}}_{\sigma,m}:=(t_{\sigma,N})_{N\geq n}.

By construction, each
t_{\sigma,N}
evaluates at the vertex
\operatorname{id}_{[n]}\in S_{n,N}
to the raw
basepoint
\theta_{\sigma,m}
, so

\operatorname{Ev}_{\operatorname{id}}(\theta^{\operatorname{coh}}_{\sigma,m})=\theta_{\sigma,m}.

Therefore

q^{\operatorname{raw}}_{\sigma,m}\bigl(\operatorname{Ev}_{\operatorname{id}}(\theta^{\operatorname{coh}}_{\sigma,m})\bigr)=q^{\operatorname{raw}}_{\sigma,m}(\theta_{\sigma,m})=\mathcal{G}_{m}(\sigma)

by Lemma
4.2.4
.

∎

Notations 4.2.21

(Finite coherent choice spaces)
.

For a simplex

\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}

and an integer
N\geq n
, we write

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma):=\operatorname{Sec}(p_{\sigma,N}),

with the notation of Lemma
4.2.18
.
Thus Proposition
4.2.20
identifies

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma)\;\simeq\;\varprojlim_{N\geq n}\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma).

Notations 4.2.22

(Boundary truncation of the overcategory)
.

Let

\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}

and let
N\geq n
.
Let

J^{\partial}_{n,N}\subset J_{n,N}

be the full subcategory spanned by the non-surjective maps

\alpha:[r]\to[n]\qquad(r\leq N).

Set

S^{\partial}_{n,N}:=N(J^{\partial}_{n,N})\subset S_{n,N}.

We write

p^{\partial}_{\sigma,N}:=p_{\sigma,N}\big|_{S^{\partial}_{n,N}}:E_{\sigma,N}\big|_{S^{\partial}_{n,N}}\longrightarrow S^{\partial}_{n,N}.

Proposition 4.2.23

(The global coherent-choice projection lifts against boundaries)
.

With the notation above, the projection

p_{m}:E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}\longrightarrow K=N(\Sm^{imm}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}

has the right lifting property with respect to every boundary inclusion

\partial\Delta^{n}\hookrightarrow\Delta^{n}.

Proof.

Fix
n\geq 0
, and consider a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\Delta^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

We construct a lift
\widetilde{u}:\Delta^{n}\to E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}
.

The boundary datum already gives coherent choices on every proper face of

\sigma
. Thus the only real problem is to extend these compatible facewise
choices to the whole simplex. We first solve this at each finite truncation
level
N
, where the boundary restriction map is a Kan fibration with
contractible fibers, and then pass to the inverse limit.

Step 1: the boundary map determines coherent boundary choices.

For each non-surjective simplicial operator

\alpha:[r]\to[n],

the restriction of
u
along
\alpha:\Delta^{r}\to\partial\Delta^{n}
is an
r
-simplex of

E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}
lying over
\sigma\circ\alpha
. Write

u_{\alpha}\in\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)_{0}

for its component indexed by
\operatorname{id}_{[r]}
. By the defining coherence relations for simplices of

E_{m}^{\operatorname{coh}}
, these vertices satisfy

\beta^{*}(u_{\alpha})=u_{\alpha\circ\beta}

for every
\beta:[s]\to[r]
.

For every
N\geq n
and every non-surjective simplicial operator

\alpha:[r]\to[n]\qquad(r\leq N),

project
u_{\alpha}
to its
N
-th truncation

u_{\alpha,\leq N}\in\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma\circ\alpha)_{0}.

Unwinding the definitions of
Construction
4.2.11
,
Lemma
4.2.18
,
and Proposition
4.2.20
, the coherent family

\{u_{\alpha,\leq N}\}_{\alpha:[r]\to[n],\,r\leq N}

assembles into a section

b_{\sigma,N}(u)\in\operatorname{Sec}\!\bigl(p^{\partial}_{\sigma,N}\bigr)_{0}.

More explicitly, a vertex of
\operatorname{Sec}(p^{\partial}_{\sigma,N})
is by definition a coherent family

\{\xi_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha:[r]\to[n]\ \text{non-surjective},\,r\leq N},\qquad\xi_{\alpha}\in\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma\circ\alpha)_{0},

such that

\beta^{*}(\xi_{\alpha})=\xi_{\alpha\circ\beta}

for every
\beta:[s]\to[r]
for which
\alpha\circ\beta
is still non-surjective.
The family
\{u_{\alpha,\leq N}\}
satisfies exactly these relations, because they already hold for
the vertices
u_{\alpha}\in\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)_{0}
, and the truncation maps

\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m}(\sigma\circ\alpha)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Choices}^{\operatorname{coh}}_{m,\leq N}(\sigma\circ\alpha)

commute strictly with pullback along simplicial operators.

Moreover, these sections are compatible under restriction in
N
.

Step 2: extend the truncated boundary sections recursively.

For
N=n
