is also top graph-composition data for
\tau\circ\alpha
. Hence the space of equivalences

T_{\tau\circ\alpha,m}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\alpha^{\vee})^{*}T_{\tau,m}

compatible with the interval graph-composition comparisons is contractible. We choose one and
denote it by

c^{\operatorname{prism}}_{\tau,\alpha,m}:\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau\circ\alpha)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\alpha^{\vee})^{*}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau).

For
\alpha=\operatorname{id}
, we choose
c^{\operatorname{prism}}_{\tau,\operatorname{id},m}=\operatorname{id}
. If
\tau
has dimension
1

and
\alpha:[0]\hookrightarrow[1]
is a proper injective, we choose

c^{\operatorname{prism}}_{\tau,\alpha,m}
to be the identity under the tautological identification of the two
vertex restrictions. Thus the dimension-
1
normalization required in
Notation
4.4.1
is satisfied.

The compatibility homotopies for identities, pairs of composable operators, and triples of
composable operators are obtained from the same contractible comparison spaces, applied to the
finite diagrams of one, two, and three composable operators, using

(\alpha\circ\beta)^{\vee}=\alpha^{\vee}\circ\beta^{\vee}.

Equivalently, one applies Proposition
4.2.6
to these finite
coherent-choice towers. This gives the required coherent operator-comparison package.
∎

Definition 5.5.12

(Normalized
1
-skeleton on
N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
)
.

Define

F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}:\sk_{1}\bigl(N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

as follows:

•

on objects,
X\mapsto\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)
;

•

on a nondegenerate edge
f:Y\to X
, set

F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}(f):=E_{m}(f);

•

on the degenerate edge
s_{0}(X)
, set

F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}\bigl(s_{0}(X)\bigr):=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

Notations 5.5.13
.

If
p:Y\to X
is a smooth morphism in
\Sm_{S}
, we write

p_{m}^{*}:=p^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y)

to emphasize the fixed degree
m
.

Lemma 5.5.14

(Deformation space of a section over the base)
.

Let

p:P\to X

be a smooth morphism in
\Sm_{S}
, and let

s:X\hookrightarrow P

be a section.
Write

D(s)\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{S}

for the deformation space of the regular immersion
s
, let

N:=N_{X/P}

be its special fiber, and let

U_{s}:=D(s)\setminus N

be its open stratum.

Then the morphism

p\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}:P\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

induces a canonical morphism

\widetilde{p}:D(s)\longrightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

which is smooth. It restricts to:

(1)

the vector bundle projection

\pi:N=N_{X/P}\to X

on the special fiber over
0
,

(2)

and

p\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}:P\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}

on the open complement
U_{s}\simeq P\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Proof.

The morphism
\widetilde{p}
is induced functorially from
p\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
by the construction of
deformation to the normal cone.
On the open complement
\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\}=\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the deformation space is canonically

U_{s}\simeq P\times\mathbb{G}_{m},

and
\widetilde{p}
is exactly
p\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
.
On the special fiber, the induced morphism is the normal bundle projection

\pi:N_{X/P}\to X.

It remains to show that
\widetilde{p}
is smooth. This is local on source and target for the étale topology.
Around every point of
s(X)\subset P
, the pair
(P,s)
is étale-locally over
X
isomorphic to the
zero section of an affine bundle

X\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{d}.

For the zero section, the deformation space is canonically

D(s)\simeq X\times\mathbb{A}^{d}\times\mathbb{A}^{1},

which is smooth over
X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
Thus
\widetilde{p}
is smooth étale-locally near the special fiber, and it is already smooth on the open
part
U_{s}
. Hence
\widetilde{p}
is smooth everywhere.
∎

Lemma 5.5.15

(Sections of smooth morphisms give right inverses)
.

Let

p:P\to X

be a smooth morphism in
\Sm_{S}
, and let

s:X\hookrightarrow P

be a section.
Then for every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
there is a canonical homotopy

s_{m}^{*}\circ p^{*}\ \simeq\ \operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\!\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(X),\,\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\bigr).

Proof.

Write

N:=N_{X/P},\qquad\pi:N\to X

for the normal bundle and its projection, and let

U_{s}:=D(s)\setminus N.

By the length-
1
case of Definition
2.4.4
, the closed pullback along

s
is the composite

\mathcal{G}_{m}(P)\xrightarrow{\beta^{(1)}_{P,m}}\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(P\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-1]\xrightarrow{(\varphi_{s})^{*}[-1]}\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(U_{s})[-1]\xrightarrow{\delta_{s}}\mathcal{G}_{m}(N)\xrightarrow{(\pi^{*})^{-1}}\mathcal{G}_{m}(X).

By Lemma
5.5.14
, there is a smooth morphism of pairs

\widetilde{p}:(D(s),N)\longrightarrow(X\times\mathbb{A}^{1},\ X\times\{0\}),

whose restriction on open complements is

p\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}:P\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}

and whose restriction on the special fiber is
\pi:N\to X
.
Let

\delta_{t}:\mathcal{G}_{m+1}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m})[-1]\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)

be the boundary map for the principal smooth divisor

X\times\{0\}\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}.

Functoriality of localization for the above smooth morphism of pairs yields a canonical homotopy

\delta_{s}\circ(\varphi_{s})^{*}[-1]\circ(p\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{*}[-1]\ \simeq\ \pi^{*}\circ\delta_{t}.

Combining this with Axiom
(wBM11)
for the smooth morphism
p
and Axiom
(wBM12)
, we get

\displaystyle s_{m}^{*}\circ p^{*}

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{s}\circ(\varphi_{s})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{P,m}\circ p^{*}

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ\delta_{s}\circ(\varphi_{s})^{*}[-1]\circ(p\times\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{*}[-1]\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}

\displaystyle\simeq(\pi^{*})^{-1}\circ\pi^{*}\circ\delta_{t}\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}

\displaystyle\simeq\delta_{t}\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}

\displaystyle\simeq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

∎

Lemma 5.5.16

(Section-cancelled smooth base change)
.

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a closed immersion in
\Sm_{S}
, let

\pi:P\to X
be smooth, and let

\widetilde{i}:Z\hookrightarrow P

be a closed immersion such that
\pi\circ\widetilde{i}=i
.
Form the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{Z}:=P\times_{X}Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

Then
\widetilde{i}
factors uniquely as

Z\xrightarrow{s}P_{Z}\xrightarrow{j}P,

where
s
is a section of the smooth morphism
\pi_{Z}
. For every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
there is a
canonical homotopy

\widetilde{i}_{m}^{*}\circ\pi^{*}\simeq i_{m}^{*}

in
\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{G}_{m}(X),\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z))
.

Proof.

The equality
\pi\circ\widetilde{i}=i
gives the unique factorization

s:Z\to P_{Z}=P\times_{X}Z.

The equality
\pi_{Z}\circ s=\operatorname{id}_{Z}
is tautological. Since
\pi_{Z}
is smooth and separated,
its section
s
is a closed immersion in
\Sm_{S}
.

The closed-immersion pullbacks of Theorem
4.5.5
are functorial for
composition. Applied to the factorization
\widetilde{i}=j\circ s
, this gives

\widetilde{i}_{m}^{*}\simeq s_{m}^{*}\circ j_{m}^{*}.

The square defining
P_{Z}
is cartesian and
\pi
is smooth, so smooth base change for the
closed-immersion pullbacks gives

j_{m}^{*}\circ\pi^{*}\simeq\pi_{Z}^{*}\circ i_{m}^{*}.

Therefore

\widetilde{i}_{m}^{*}\circ\pi^{*}\simeq s_{m}^{*}\circ j_{m}^{*}\circ\pi^{*}\simeq s_{m}^{*}\circ\pi_{Z}^{*}\circ i_{m}^{*}.

Finally Lemma
5.5.15
, applied to the smooth morphism

\pi_{Z}:P_{Z}\to Z
and its section
s
, gives

s_{m}^{*}\circ\pi_{Z}^{*}\simeq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(Z)}.

Composing these homotopies proves the assertion.
∎

Lemma 5.5.17

(Subinterval graph-prism square)
.

Let

\tau=\bigl(X_{0}\xrightarrow{f_{1}}X_{1}\to\cdots\to X_{n}\bigr)\in N(\Sm_{S})_{n},

and let
0\leq a<b\leq n
. Write

f_{a,b}:=f_{b}\circ f_{b-1}\circ\cdots\circ f_{a+1}:X_{a}\to X_{b}.

Let

\gamma_{\tau,a,b}:P_{a}(\tau)\hookrightarrow P_{b}(\tau)

be the composite closed immersion in the graph flag
\Gamma(\tau)
. Then there is a canonical
homotopy

(\gamma_{\tau,a,b})_{m}^{*}\circ q_{\tau,b}^{*}\simeq q_{\tau,a}^{*}\circ E_{m}(f_{a,b})

in

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}(X_{b}),\mathcal{G}_{m}(P_{a}(\tau))\bigr).

Proof.

Put

R_{a,b}:=P_{a}(\tau)\times_{S}X_{b},

and let

\overline{\gamma}_{\tau,a,b}:P_{a}(\tau)\hookrightarrow R_{a,b}

be the graph of

f_{a,b}\circ q_{\tau,a}:P_{a}(\tau)\to X_{b}.

Let

\pi_{a,b}:P_{b}(\tau)\to R_{a,b}

be the projection forgetting the intermediate factors

X_{a+1},\ldots,X_{b-1}.

Then
\pi_{a,b}
is smooth, and

\pi_{a,b}\circ\gamma_{\tau,a,b}=\overline{\gamma}_{\tau,a,b}.

Moreover
\gamma_{\tau,a,b}
factors through

P_{b}(\tau)\times_{R_{a,b}}P_{a}(\tau)

as a section of the smooth projection to
P_{a}(\tau)
. Hence
Lemma
5.5.16
gives

(\gamma_{\tau,a,b})_{m}^{*}\circ\pi_{a,b}^{*}\simeq(\overline{\gamma}_{\tau,a,b})_{m}^{*}.

Let

\operatorname{pr}_{2}:R_{a,b}=P_{a}(\tau)\times_{S}X_{b}\to X_{b}

be the second projection. Since

q_{\tau,b}=\operatorname{pr}_{2}\circ\pi_{a,b},

we get

(\gamma_{\tau,a,b})_{m}^{*}\circ q_{\tau,b}^{*}\simeq(\overline{\gamma}_{\tau,a,b})_{m}^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}.

Now consider the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{a}(\tau)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{a}(\tau)\times_{S}X_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{a}\times_{S}X_{b}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\gamma}_{\tau,a,b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\tau,a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\tau,a}\times\operatorname{id}_{X_{b}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{f_{a,b}}}

The right vertical morphism is smooth. Smooth base change for the closed-immersion Gysin
pullbacks gives

(\overline{\gamma}_{\tau,a,b})_{m}^{*}\circ(q_{\tau,a}\times\operatorname{id}_{X_{b}})^{*}\simeq q_{\tau,a}^{*}\circ(\Gamma_{f_{a,b}})_{m}^{*}.

Precomposing with the projection pullback

\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X_{b})\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(X_{a}\times_{S}X_{b})

and using

(q_{\tau,a}\times\operatorname{id}_{X_{b}})^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}=\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}

on
P_{a}(\tau)\times_{S}X_{b}
, we obtain

(\overline{\gamma}_{\tau,a,b})_{m}^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\simeq q_{\tau,a}^{*}\circ(\Gamma_{f_{a,b}})_{m}^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}=q_{\tau,a}^{*}\circ E_{m}(f_{a,b}).

Combining the displayed homotopies proves the claim.
∎

Remark 5.5.18

(Why the top face is not formal)
.

The side data of a graph prism do not formally determine the upper face. Indeed, the vertical
morphisms

q_{\tau,r}^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X_{r})\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(P_{r}(\tau))

are smooth pullbacks, but they are not equivalences in general. Thus one cannot cancel them
from the side squares. Already for
n=2
, a bottom triangle together with the three side
squares does not force a top triangle in an arbitrary quasicategory. Consequently the construction
of
\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)
requires a genuine graph-composition argument, not only an
inner-anodyne prism-filling argument.

Lemma 5.5.19

(The graph-formula edge of the identity)
.

For every
X\in\Sm_{S}
,

E_{m}(\operatorname{id}_{X})\simeq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

Proof.

The diagonal

\Delta_{X}:X\hookrightarrow X\times_{S}X

is a section of the smooth projection

\operatorname{pr}_{2}:X\times_{S}X\to X.

Hence the claim follows immediately from
Lemma
5.5.15
.
The simplex-coordinate reformulation is the standard translation of a homotopy in the mapping space

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathcal{G}_{m}(X),\mathcal{G}_{m}(X))

to an equivalence in
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
.
∎

Lemma 5.5.20

(The graph-formula edge of a smooth morphism)
.

Let
p:Y\to X
be smooth. Then

E_{m}(p)\simeq p^{*}.

Proof.

Write

\Gamma_{p}:Y\hookrightarrow Y\times_{S}X,\qquad\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{2}:Y\times_{S}X\to X

for the graph of
p
and the second projection. By definition,

E_{m}(p)=(\Gamma_{p})_{m}^{*}\circ(\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{2})^{*}.

Consider the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times_{S}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X\times_{S}X.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\times\operatorname{id}_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{X}}

The right vertical morphism is smooth. Smooth base change for closed-immersion Gysin pullbacks (see Lemma
3.2.14
) gives

(\Gamma_{p})_{m}^{*}\circ(p\times\operatorname{id}_{X})^{*}\simeq p^{*}\circ(\Delta_{X})_{m}^{*}.

Precomposing with

\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(X\times_{S}X)

and using

(\operatorname{pr}^{\prime}_{2})^{*}=(p\times\operatorname{id}_{X})^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*},

we get

E_{m}(p)\simeq p^{*}\circ(\Delta_{X})_{m}^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}.

By Lemma
5.5.19
,

(\Delta_{X})_{m}^{*}\circ\operatorname{pr}_{2}^{*}\simeq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

Hence
E_{m}(p)\simeq p^{*}
.
∎

Lemma 5.5.21

(The graph-formula edge of a closed immersion)
.

Let
i:Y\hookrightarrow X
be a closed immersion. Then

E_{m}(i)\simeq i_{m}^{*}.

Proof.

Write

\Gamma_{i}:Y\hookrightarrow Y\times_{S}X,\qquad q_{i}:Y\times_{S}X\to X

for the graph immersion and the projection to
X
. By definition,

E_{m}(i)=(\Gamma_{i})_{m}^{*}\circ q_{i}^{*}.

The projection

q_{i}:Y\times_{S}X\to X

is smooth, and the equality

q_{i}\circ\Gamma_{i}=i

puts us exactly in the situation of
Lemma
5.5.16
, with

\pi=q_{i},\qquad\widetilde{i}=\Gamma_{i}.

Hence

(\Gamma_{i})_{m}^{*}\circ q_{i}^{*}\simeq i_{m}^{*}.

This proves
E_{m}(i)\simeq i_{m}^{*}
.
∎

5.6.
Prismatic operator comparisons

The graph simplex

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{simp}}(\Gamma(\tau))\circ q_{\tau}^{*}

does not have the correct faces: it only corrects the terminal source vertex and leaves the
intermediate product vertices
P_{r}(\tau)
. We therefore use the prismatic simplices

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)
supplied by
Proposition
5.5.11
.

For a general simplicial operator

\alpha:[r]\to[n],

the coherent comparison has the form

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau\circ\alpha)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\alpha^{\vee})^{*}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau),

where
\alpha^{\vee}
is the opposite simplex-coordinate operator of
Notation
5.5.2
. In particular, for cofaces and
codegeneracies this gives

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(d_{i}\tau)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\delta^{\,n-i})^{*}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau),

and

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(s_{i}\tau)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }(\varsigma^{\,n-i})^{*}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau).

This is the simplex-coordinate convention of
Corollaries
4.1.6
and

4.1.9
.

5.7.
Main results

Lemma 5.7.1

(Initial
1
-skeleton comparison data for the prismatic lci simplices)
.

Set

K:=N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}.

For every simplex

\bar{\tau}\in K_{r}\qquad(r\leq 1),

represented by a simplex

\tau\in N(\Sm_{S})_{r},

there is a canonical equivalence

\epsilon^{(1),\operatorname{lci}}_{\bar{\tau}}:F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}(\bar{\tau})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)

in
\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}
, compatible with proper injectives in the sense required by
Theorem
4.4.8
.

Proof.

If
\bar{\tau}=X
is a vertex, both sides are
\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)
, and we take the identity
equivalence.

If
\bar{\tau}
is represented by a nondegenerate morphism

f:Y\to X

in
N(\Sm_{S})
, then

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(f)=E_{m}(f)=F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}(\bar{\tau}),

and we take the identity equivalence.

If
\bar{\tau}=s_{0}(X)
is the degenerate edge, then

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(s_{0}X)=E_{m}(\operatorname{id}_{X}).

The required comparison

F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}(s_{0}X)=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }E_{m}(\operatorname{id}_{X})

is the inverse of the homotopy of Lemma
5.5.19
.

For
r\leq 1
, the only proper injectives are the two vertex inclusions. Under the
identification of
K=N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
with the opposite simplex-coordinate convention of
Notation
5.5.2
, these are the corresponding opposite
vertex restrictions. The compatibility is tautological for vertices and nondegenerate edges. For
the degenerate edge, it is exactly the source and target compatibility of the homotopy

E_{m}(\operatorname{id}_{X})\simeq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

∎

Theorem 5.7.2

(Prismatic homotopy coherent contravariant gluing on
\Sm_{S}
in degree
m
)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a weak Borel–Moore theory on
\Sm_{S}
with values in the stable

\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
. Fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
. Then there exists a simplicial map

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}:N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}

whose restriction to
0
-simplices is exactly

X\longmapsto\mathcal{G}_{m}(X).

Moreover:

(1)

if
p:Y\to X
is smooth, then
\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}(p)\simeq p^{*}
;

(2)

if
i:Y\hookrightarrow X
is a closed immersion, then

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}(i)\simeq i_{m}^{*};

(3)

for an arbitrary
f:Y\to X
, the edge
\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}(f)
is represented by the
graph formula

\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\xrightarrow{\ q_{f}^{*}\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y\times_{S}X)\xrightarrow{\ (\Gamma_{f})_{m}^{*}\ }\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y).

Equivalently,
\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}
is a strict simplicial contravariant extension to all of
\Sm_{S}

of the given smooth pullback functoriality together with the rigidified closed-immersion pullbacks
of Sections
2
–
4
.

Proof.

We apply the abstract rigidification theorem to

K:=N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}.

An
r
-simplex of
K
is represented by an
r
-simplex

\tau=\bigl(X_{0}\to X_{1}\to\cdots\to X_{r}\bigr)

of
N(\Sm_{S})
, with simplex coordinates reversed. We assign to this simplex the prismatic
raw simplex

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau):\Delta^{r}\to\mathcal{C}

of Proposition
5.5.11
.

Let

\alpha:[s]\to[r]

be a simplicial operator in
K=N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
. Under the representation of an

r
-simplex of
K
by a covariant simplex

\tau=(X_{0}\to X_{1}\to\cdots\to X_{r})

of
N(\Sm_{S})
, the restricted simplex
\bar{\tau}\circ\alpha
of
K
is represented by

\tau\circ\alpha^{\vee}

in
N(\Sm_{S})
. Therefore, applying
Proposition
5.5.11
to the covariant operator

\alpha^{\vee}
, we get

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau\circ\alpha^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }((\alpha^{\vee})^{\vee})^{*}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)=\alpha^{*}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau).

These are exactly the operator comparisons required by
Notation
4.4.1
for the simplicial set
K
.

The dimension-
0
normalization gives

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(X)=\mathcal{G}_{m}(X).

The dimension-
1
normalization gives

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(f)=E_{m}(f)

for every nondegenerate edge
f:Y\to X
, and the degenerate edge is normalized by
Lemma
5.5.19
.

Apply Theorem
4.4.8
with

K:=N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}},\qquad\Phi_{m}:=F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m},\qquad\tau\longmapsto\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau),

using the initial
1
-skeleton comparison data of
Lemma
5.7.1
. This gives a simplicial map

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}:N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}

whose restriction to the
1
-skeleton is
F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}
. Hence its edge attached to an
arbitrary nondegenerate morphism
f
is
E_{m}(f)
.

If
p
is smooth, Lemma
5.5.20
identifies
E_{m}(p)
with

p^{*}
. If
i
is a closed immersion, Lemma
5.5.21
identifies

E_{m}(i)
with
i_{m}^{*}
. The identity case is Lemma
5.5.19
.
∎

Proposition 5.7.3

(Shared prismatic lci rigidification after identifying the formal input)
.

Fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, and set
2
2

2

When a simplex of
K
is represented by a simplex of
N(\Sm_{S})
, the operator

\alpha
on
K
corresponds to the opposite simplex-coordinate operator
\alpha^{\vee}
of
Notation
5.5.2
on the displayed covariant simplex.

K:=N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}.

Assume that two constructions in the same target
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
determine the
same formal prismatic lci rigidification input, namely:

(1)

the same normalized
1
-skeleton

F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}:\sk_{1}(K)\to\mathcal{C};

(2)

the same prismatic raw simplices

\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)\in\Fun(\Delta^{r},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}_{0}\qquad(\tau\in K_{r});

(3)

the same coherent operator comparisons

c^{\operatorname{prism}}_{\tau,\alpha,m}:\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau\circ\alpha)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\alpha^{*}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau),

together with the same composition and triple-composition homotopies required in
Notation
4.4.1
;

(4)

the same initial
1
-skeleton comparison data

\epsilon^{(1),\operatorname{lci}}_{\tau}:F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}(\tau)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)\qquad(\dim\tau\leq 1),

including the same compatibility homotopies for every proper injective

\alpha:[s]\hookrightarrow[r],\qquad r\leq 1,

as required in Theorem
4.4.8
.

Then the skeletal construction in the proof of
Theorem
4.4.8
may be carried out simultaneously for the two
constructions, using the same choices at every nondegenerate simplex and every skeletal stage.
In this way one obtains a single simplicial map

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}:N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}

together with equivalences

\epsilon^{\operatorname{rig},\operatorname{lci}}_{\tau}:\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}(\tau)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)\qquad(\tau\in K_{r},\ r\geq 0),

which serves as the rigidified contravariant extension for both constructions.

Proof.

We synchronize the proof of Theorem
4.4.8
.

The
1
-skeleton is common by assumption:

F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}:\sk_{1}(K)\to\mathcal{C},

and the comparison data

\epsilon^{(1),\operatorname{lci}}_{\tau}:F^{\operatorname{lci}}_{1,m}(\tau)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)

for
\dim\tau\leq 1
are also common.

Assume inductively that the two constructions have already been made identical on

\sk_{n-1}(K)
. Let

\sigma\in K_{n}^{\mathrm{nd}}

be a nondegenerate
n
-simplex. The boundary simplex

G_{\sigma}:\partial\Delta^{n}\to\mathcal{C}

already constructed on
\sk_{n-1}(K)
, the lower-dimensional comparison equivalences, and the
operator comparisons

c^{\operatorname{prism}}_{\sigma,\alpha,m}

are the same in the two constructions. Hence
Lemma
4.4.6
gives the same boundary comparison

e_{\sigma}:G_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\rho_{n}\bigl(\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\sigma)\bigr)

for both constructions.

Now use the same lift in the Kan fibration

\rho_{n}^{\simeq}:\Fun(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}\to\Fun(\partial\Delta^{n},\mathcal{C})^{\simeq}.

That is, choose once and for all an equivalence

\epsilon^{\operatorname{rig},\operatorname{lci}}_{\sigma}:x_{\sigma}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\sigma)

whose boundary is
e_{\sigma}
, and use this same choice in both constructions.

By Lemma
4.4.4
, the chosen
x_{\sigma}
, as
\sigma
runs through
nondegenerate
n
-simplices, determine a unique extension of the common

(n-1)
-skeleton to a common
n
-skeleton. For degenerate
n
-simplices, the comparison
equivalences are then determined identically by Lemma
4.4.7
.
This completes the induction.

Passing to the colimit over skeleta gives a single simplicial map

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}:K\to\mathcal{C}

and equivalences

\epsilon^{\operatorname{rig},\operatorname{lci}}_{\tau}:\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}(\tau)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{M}_{m}^{\operatorname{prism}}(\tau)

for all simplices
\tau
. By construction this is simultaneously the output of both rigidification
procedures.
∎

Definition 5.7.4

(Pullback map for a morphism of smooth
S
-schemes in degree
m
)
.

Fix once and for all a choice of simplicial map

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}:N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}

as in Theorem
5.7.2
. Since

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}(X)=\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\qquad(X\in\Sm_{S})

strictly on objects, for every morphism

f:Y\to X

in
\Sm_{S}
we define

f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}:=\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}(f):\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y).

Corollary 5.7.5

(Independence of immersion–smooth factorization in degree
m
)
.

Fix
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, and let
f:Y\to X
be a morphism in
\Sm_{S}
.
Choose two factorizations

f=p\circ i=p^{\prime}\circ i^{\prime}

with
i,i^{\prime}
closed immersions and
p,p^{\prime}
smooth.
Then the morphism

f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\to\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y)

comes equipped with specified homotopies

f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\simeq i_{m}^{*}\circ p^{*},\qquad f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\simeq i_{m}^{\prime*}\circ(p^{\prime})^{*}.

In particular, the two composites

i_{m}^{*}\circ p^{*}\qquad\text{and}\qquad i_{m}^{\prime*}\circ(p^{\prime})^{*}

are canonically identified up to contractible choice.

Proof.

Let
f=p\circ i
be one of the chosen factorizations, with intermediate object
P
.
The corresponding
2
-simplex of
N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}
yields a specified
2
-simplex in
\mathcal{C}
with boundary

f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\ \Rightarrow\ i_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\circ p_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}.

By Theorem
5.7.2
,

p_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\simeq p^{*},\qquad i_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\simeq i_{m}^{*}.

Composing these specified homotopies yields

f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\simeq i_{m}^{*}\circ p^{*}.

The same argument applied to the factorization

f=p^{\prime}\circ i^{\prime}

gives

f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\simeq i_{m}^{\prime*}\circ(p^{\prime})^{*}.

Composing the two displayed homotopies gives the claimed identification between the two composites.
∎

Remark 5.7.6
.

Since
\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}\colon N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathcal{C}
is a simplicial map, every composable pair

X\xrightarrow{f}Y\xrightarrow{g}Z

determines a specified
2
-simplex in
\mathcal{C}
with boundary

(g\circ f)_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\ \Rightarrow\ f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\circ g_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}.

In particular, one obtains a homotopy

(g\circ f)_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\simeq f_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}\circ g_{m}^{*,\operatorname{lci}}

well defined up to contractible choice. The higher simplices of
\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{lci}}
provide the corresponding coherent associativity data.

6.
Application: representability of Rost-Schmid complexes

6.1.
Rost-Schmid complexes of MW-homodules

Notations 6.1.1
.

We record the main cycle-theoretic source of examples for the weak Borel-Moore
formalism of Definition
2.2.12
. Throughout this subsection, let

S
be a noetherian excellent scheme equipped with a dimension function

\delta
, and let
\mathcal{M}
be an
R
-linear homological Milnor-Witt cycle
module over
S
, or MW-homodule, in the sense of

[
DFJ25
, Def. 1.2.2]
. We write
\mathcal{D}(R)
for the derived

\infty
-category of
R
-modules.

Let
X
be an essentially lci
S
-scheme. We use the cohomological
Rost-Schmid convention of
[
DFJ25
, 1.3.7]
: for a line bundle

\mathcal{L}
on
X
, the cocycle complex is

C^{p}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q},\mathcal{L})=\bigoplus_{x\in X^{(p)}}\mathcal{M}_{q-p}\bigl(\kappa(x),\omega_{X/S,x}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\vee}\bigr),

with differential induced by the residues of
\mathcal{M}
. We abbreviate

C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q}):=C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q},\mathcal{O}_{X}).

Thus, if
X/S
is smooth of relative dimension
d
, this convention is
related to the homological Rost-Schmid complex by

C^{p}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q},\mathcal{L})=C_{d-p}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{q-d},\omega_{X/S}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\bigr).

The shift in the following definition is part of the normalization. It is the
normalization for which smooth pullbacks have degree zero, closed immersions of
codimension
c
give maps
m-c\to m
without any extra suspension, and the

\mathbb{G}_{m}
-contractions have precisely the shift required in
Definition
2.2.12
.

Definition 6.1.2

(The Rost-Schmid weak Borel-Moore object)
.

For
X\in\Sm_{S}
and
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, set

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{RS}}_{m}(X):=C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})[m]\quad\in\quad\mathcal{D}(R).

When no confusion is possible, we write simply
\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)
for

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{RS}}_{m}(X)
.

Remark 6.1.3

(Why the shift)
.

With cohomological conventions, an essentially smooth morphism

p:Y\to X
induces a pullback

p^{!}:C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}(Y,\mathcal{M}_{m})

of degree zero. A regular closed immersion
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
of
codimension
c
, together with a trivialization of
\det\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}
,
induces a cohomological proper pushforward

C^{\bullet}(Z,\mathcal{M}_{m-c})\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})[c].

After shifting the source by
[m-c]
and the target by
[m]
, this becomes a
morphism

\mathcal{G}_{m-c}(Z)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(X).

Likewise, multiplication by
n
ordered units raises the Milnor-Witt degree
from
m
to
m+n
, and the identity

\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n})[-n]=C^{\bullet}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n},\mathcal{M}_{m+n})[m]

explains the shift appearing in the contraction maps.

Proposition 6.1.4

(Rost-Schmid complexes give a weak Borel-Moore theory)
.

Consider the setting of the preceding definition.
Then the assignment

X\longmapsto\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)=C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})[m]

for
X\in\Sm_{S}
and
m\in\mathbf{Z}
is a weak Borel-Moore theory on
\Sm_{S}

with values in
\mathcal{D}(R)
, in the sense of Definition
2.2.12
.

Proof.

We verify the fourteen pieces of Definition
2.2.12
. The basic maps on
Rost-Schmid complexes are those of
[
DFJ25
, §1.4]
: proper
pushforward, essentially smooth pullback, multiplication by units, multiplication
by
\eta
, and boundary maps. The facts used below are the compatibilities of

[
DFJ25
, §1.5]
, the localization sequence

[
DFJ25
, 1.5.8]
, and homotopy invariance for vector bundles

[
DFJ25
, Thm. 2.1.4]
. Equalities of maps of complexes give
constant specified homotopies in
\mathcal{D}(R)
; whenever an inverse to a
quasi-isomorphism is needed, we choose coherent inverse data by
Definition
2.1.10
and Lemma
2.1.11
.

Axiom (wBM1): assignment.

This is Definition
6.1.2
.

Axiom (wBM2): smooth contravariance.

Let
p:Y\to X
be a smooth morphism in
\Sm_{S}
. The essentially smooth
pullback of
[
DFJ25
, 1.4.2]
, in cohomological conventions, gives
a morphism of complexes

p^{!}:C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}(Y,\mathcal{M}_{m}).

After the common shift
[m]
, we denote the resulting morphism by

p^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(Y).

For composable smooth morphisms
Z\xrightarrow{q}Y\xrightarrow{p}X
,

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1(2)]
gives the equality

(p\circ q)^{!}=q^{!}\circ p^{!}
. Together with the evident identity statement,
these equalities define a strict, hence homotopy coherent, simplicial map

\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\mathit{sm}}:N(\Sm^{\mathit{sm}}_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}(R).

Axiom (wBM3): closed covariance for trivialized regular immersions.

Let
(i,\vartheta_{i}):Z\hookrightarrow X
be a trivialized regular immersion of
codimension
c
. The cohomological form of proper pushforward for the closed
immersion
i
, using the trivialization of
\det\mathcal{N}_{Z/X}
to remove
the determinant twist, gives a morphism of complexes

i_{*}:C^{\bullet}(Z,\mathcal{M}_{m-c})\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})[c].

After shifting source and target, this is a morphism in
\mathcal{D}(R)

i_{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m-c}(Z)=C^{\bullet}(Z,\mathcal{M}_{m-c})[m-c]\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})[m]=\mathcal{G}_{m}(X).

For the identity immersion this is the identity map. For a composable pair

W\xhookrightarrow{k}Z\xhookrightarrow{i}X

with compatible trivializations in the sense of Axiom
(wBM3)
, the equality

(i\circ k)_{*}=i_{*}\circ k_{*}
follows from functoriality of proper pushforward,

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1(1)]
, together with the chosen
identification of determinant twists induced by the normal-bundle exact sequence.
This equality supplies the required specified homotopy.

Axiom (wBM4): smooth-closed Beck-Chevalley.

Consider a cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

with
g
smooth and
(i,\vartheta_{i})
a trivialized regular immersion of
codimension
c
. The pulled-back immersion
i^{\prime}
is endowed with the pulled
back trivialization. Proposition
[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1(3)]

gives the base-change equality

g^{!}\circ i_{*}=i^{\prime}_{*}\circ g^{\prime!}

on the underlying cohomological Rost-Schmid complexes, with the determinant
identifications supplied by the cartesian square. After the shifts
[m-c]
and

[m]
, this is the required homotopy

g^{*}\circ i_{*}\simeq i^{\prime}_{*}\circ(g^{\prime})^{*}.

Axiom (wBM5): localization for principal smooth divisors.

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a principal smooth divisor with open complement

j:U\hookrightarrow X
. The splitting of the Rost-Schmid complex according
to points in
Z
and in
U
, together with the boundary map of

[
DFJ25
, 1.4.6]
, gives the localization triangle of

[
DFJ25
, 1.5.8]
. In the present shifted normalization this is
exactly

\mathcal{G}_{m}(U)[-1]\xrightarrow{\,\partial\,}\mathcal{G}_{m-1}(Z)\xrightarrow{\,i_{*}\,}\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\xrightarrow{\,j^{*}\,}\mathcal{G}_{m}(U).

Functoriality of this triangle under smooth base change is precisely

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.4(2)]
, combined with smooth/proper
base change from
[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1(3)]
.

Axiom (wBM6): divisor-closed base change.

Consider a cartesian square satisfying the transversality hypotheses of
Axiom
(wBM6)
. Thus
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
and

i^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X^{\prime}
are principal smooth divisor immersions, while

g:X^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
,
g^{\prime}:Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow Z
, and

g_{U}:U^{\prime}\hookrightarrow U
are trivialized regular immersions of the same
codimension
c
. The block-transposition condition in
Axiom
(wBM6)
(e) identifies the determinant
twists attached to the two factorizations of
Z^{\prime}\hookrightarrow X
.

The four vertical arrows in that axiom are the cohomological proper
pushforwards attached to
g_{U}
,
g^{\prime}
, and
g
, with the determinant twists
removed by the chosen trivializations. Compatibility of proper pushforward with
boundary maps is
[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.4(1)]
; compatibility with
the closed-pushforward arrows and with the open restriction follows from

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1(1)]
, using the determinant-line symmetry
corresponding to the above block transposition. After applying the shifts
defining
\mathcal{G}
, these equalities assemble into the required morphism of fiber
sequences.

Axiom (wBM7): localization cubes.

Let
(X,H_{\bullet})
be an equation-equipped ordered SNC pair of length
n
.
For
K\subseteq[n-1]
, set

\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m)(K):=\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K}).

For a covering relation
K\subset K\cup\{a\}
, the edge in

\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}}
is the primitive closed pushforward associated with the
principal smooth divisor

\iota_{K,a}:H_{K\cup\{a\}}\hookrightarrow H_{K},

namely

(\iota_{K,a})_{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|-1}(H_{K\cup\{a\}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m-|K|}(H_{K}).

For a two-dimensional face indexed by distinct
a,b\notin K
, the required

2
-simplex is the divisor-closed base-change compatibility proved in the
preceding paragraph, applied to the SNC square face. The comparison of
trivializations is the block transposition between the two ordered normal-line
decompositions.
This gives the required functor

\mathcal{K}_{X,H_{\bullet}}(m):\mathcal{P}([n-1])^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}(R).

If
\theta:(X^{\prime},H^{\prime}_{\bullet})\simeq(X,H_{\bullet})
is an isomorphism of
equation-equipped ordered SNC pairs, the cube isomorphism is induced componentwise
by the smooth pullbacks
(\theta_{K})^{!}
, and functoriality follows from

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1(2)]
.

Axiom (wBM8):
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-invariance.

Let
\pi:E\to X
be a vector bundle projection. The smooth pullback

\pi^{*}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m}(E)

is the shift of
\pi^{!}:C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})\to C^{\bullet}(E,\mathcal{M}_{m})
. Homotopy
invariance for MW-homodules,
[
DFJ25
, Thm. 2.1.4]
, says that
this map is a quasi-isomorphism; hence it is an equivalence in
\mathcal{D}(R)
.

Axiom (wBM9): coherent inverse data for vector bundles.

The vector-bundle pullbacks just considered define a simplicial map from the
indexing simplicial set of vector bundle projections and the smooth/closed
base-change squares appearing in Definition
2.2.12
to

\operatorname{Eq}(\mathcal{D}(R))
. The required compatibilities for composition, for smooth base
change, and for closed base change are consequences of

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1]
. Since

U:\Adj(\mathcal{D}(R))\longrightarrow\operatorname{Eq}(\mathcal{D}(R))

is a trivial Kan fibration by Lemma
2.1.11
, this simplicial
map admits a lift. Choosing such a lift gives the inverse maps
(\pi^{*})^{-1}

and all specified homotopies required in Axiom
(wBM9)
.

Axiom (wBM10): contractions.

Let

q_{n}:X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}\longrightarrow X

be the projection, with ordered coordinates
t_{0},\ldots,t_{n-1}
. Define

\widetilde{\beta}^{(n)}_{X,m}:=[t_{0},\ldots,t_{n-1}]\circ q_{n}^{!}:C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}\bigl(X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n},\mathcal{M}_{m+n}\bigr).

Here
q_{n}^{!}
is the smooth pullback of
[
DFJ25
, 1.4.2]
, and

[t_{0},\ldots,t_{n-1}]
is multiplication by units as in

[
DFJ25
, 1.4.4]
. Shifting by
[m]
, and using

\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n})[-n]=C^{\bullet}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n},\mathcal{M}_{m+n})[m],

we obtain the required map

\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}:\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{m+n}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n})[-n].

For
n=0
, both
q_{0}^{!}
and the empty product of units are the identity.

Axiom (wBM11): smooth naturality of contractions.

Let
f:Y\to X
be smooth, and set

f^{(n)}=f\times\operatorname{id}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}}
. Functoriality of smooth pullback gives

(f^{(n)})^{!}\circ q_{n}^{!}=q_{n}^{!}\circ f^{!}

by
[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1(2)]
. Moreover,

(f^{(n)})^{*}(t_{a})=t_{a}
, so multiplication by the ordered units commutes with

(f^{(n)})^{!}
by
[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.3(1)]
. After the shifts,
this is exactly the homotopy

(f^{(n)})^{*}[-n]\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\simeq\beta^{(n)}_{Y,m}\circ f^{*}.

Axiom (wBM12): last-factor cancellation.

For
n=0
, apply the identity of
[
DFJ25
, Lem. 1.5.5]
to

X\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to X
, with section
X\times\{0\}
and open complement

X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
. It gives the chain-level equality

\partial\circ[t]\circ q^{!}=\operatorname{id}

for the boundary map associated with the divisor
t=0
. In the shifted
normalization this reads

\delta_{t}\circ\beta^{(1)}_{X,m}\simeq\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)}.

For general
n
, apply the same identity over
X\times(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{n}
and use the
smooth naturality from the previous paragraph to identify the added affine-line
coordinate with the last
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factor. This gives

\delta_{t}^{\operatorname{last}}\circ\beta^{(n+1)}_{X,m}\simeq\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}.

Axiom (wBM13): Koszul/
\epsilon
-commutativity.

Let
\epsilon_{X,m}
be the automorphism of
\mathcal{G}_{m}(X)
induced by
multiplication by the element
\epsilon\in K^{MW}_{0}
. Milnor-Witt

K
-theory is
\epsilon
-graded commutative, and
\mathcal{M}
is a module over
Milnor-Witt
K
-theory. Hence interchanging two adjacent degree-one symbols
in

[t_{0},\ldots,t_{n-1}]
changes multiplication by the action of
\epsilon
.
Consequently, for the adjacent transposition
s_{r}
of the
r
-th and

(r+1)
-st
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-factors, the equality

(s_{r})^{!}\circ[t_{0},\ldots,t_{n-1}]=[t_{0},\ldots,t_{n-1}]\circ\epsilon

induces, after shifting, the specified homotopy

(s_{r})^{*}[-n]\circ\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\simeq\beta^{(n)}_{X,m}\circ\epsilon_{X,m}.

The braid coherences are the standard coherences for the symmetric action on the
ordered product of Milnor-Witt symbols.

Axiom (wBM14): compatibility of contractions with localization.

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a principal smooth divisor with open complement

j:U\hookrightarrow X
. Smooth base change for boundary maps,

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.4(2)]
, gives compatibility of the boundary
maps with
q_{n}^{!}
. Compatibility of boundary maps with multiplication by
units is
[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.3(2)]
:

\partial\circ[j^{*}a]=-[i^{*}a]\circ\partial

for each global unit
a
. Applying this successively to the ordered units

t_{0},\ldots,t_{n-1}
, and taking into account the shifts in the definition of

\mathcal{G}_{m}
, gives a morphism from the localization fiber sequence for

(Z,X,U)
to the localization fiber sequence for

(Z\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n},X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n},U\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n})
. This is exactly the diagram
required by Axiom
(wBM14)
.

The fourteen axioms of Definition
2.2.12
are therefore satisfied.
∎

Extending the results of Rost
[
Ros96
]
,
we deduce a higher coherent contravariant functoriality in the more general
case of Rost-Schmid complexes.

Corollary 6.1.5

(Homotopy coherent Gysin functoriality for MW-homodules)
.

For every
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, the weak Borel-Moore theory

X\mapsto C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})[m]
of Proposition
6.1.4

admits the homotopy coherent contravariant extension constructed in
Theorem
5.7.2
. Thus there is a strict simplicial
functor

\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{RS}}\bigr)^{\operatorname{lci}}:N(\Sm_{S})^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}(R)

whose value on a smooth morphism is the usual smooth pullback on cohomological
Rost-Schmid complexes and whose closed-immersion part is the higher coherent
Gysin functoriality produced by Sections
2
-
5
.

Proof.

This is the direct application of Theorem
5.7.2
to the weak
Borel-Moore theory of Proposition
6.1.4
. The smooth-edge identification
is the smooth normalization in Theorem
5.7.2
; the closed-immersion
edge identification is Lemma
5.5.21
together with the
closed-immersion rigidification of Theorem
4.5.5
.
∎

6.2.
The motivic complex attached to an MW-homodule

Notations 6.2.1
.

We keep the notation and hypotheses of Notation
6.1.1
.
Thus
S
is noetherian excellent, equipped with a dimension function
\delta
,
and
\mathcal{M}
is an
R
-linear MW-homodule over
S
in the sense of

[
DFJ25
, Def. 1.2.2]
. For an essentially lci
S
-scheme

X
, a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
, and integers
p,q
, we write

A^{p}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q},\mathcal{L}):=H^{p}\bigl(C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q},\mathcal{L})\bigr),\qquad A_{p}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q},\mathcal{L}):=H_{p}\bigl(C_{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q},\mathcal{L})\bigr),

with the homological and cohomological Rost-Schmid conventions of

[
DFJ25
, 1.3.3, 1.3.7]
. In particular, if
X/S
is smooth
of relative dimension
d
, then

C^{p}(X,\mathcal{M}_{q},\mathcal{L})\simeq C_{d-p}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{q-d},\omega_{X/S}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\bigr).

We use the following standard notation. Let

{\Der_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}(S,R):=\operatorname{Sp}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\bigl(L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\operatorname{Sh}_{{\mathrm{Nis}}}(\Sm_{S},\mathcal{D}(R))\bigr)

be the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-stable
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-derived category of Nisnevich sheaves of

R
-complexes on
\Sm_{S}
. If
F
is a presheaf of complexes on
\Sm_{S}
,
we put

F_{-1}(X):=\operatorname{Fib}\Bigl(F(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m})\xrightarrow{1^{*}}F(X)\Bigr),

where
1:X\to X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
is the unit section.

Definition 6.2.2

(The motivic complex of an MW-homodule)
.

For
m\in\mathbf{Z}
, define a derived presheaf

\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m}:\Sm_{S}^{\operatorname{op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}(R)

by

\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m}(X):=C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m}).

For a morphism
f:Y\to X
in
\Sm_{S}
, the pullback

\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m}(X)\to\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m}(Y)

is the shift by
[-m]
of the lci pullback edge

\bigl(\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{RS}}_{m}\bigr)^{\operatorname{lci}}(f):\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{RS}}_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{RS}}_{m}(Y)

constructed in Corollary
6.1.5
. Thus it is the
usual smooth pullback when
f
is smooth, and it is the higher coherent Gysin
pullback constructed in Sections
2
-
5

for an arbitrary morphism of smooth
S
-schemes.

Theorem 6.2.3

(The MW-homodule realization)
.

The collection
\{\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbf{Z}}
, together with the contraction
maps induced by Proposition
6.1.4
, defines an

\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
-spectrum in
{\Der_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}(S,R)
. This construction is functorial in

\mathcal{M}
, and gives a functor

\mathbf{H}:\operatorname{MW-Homod}_{S}\longrightarrow{\Der_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}(S,R),\qquad\mathcal{M}\longmapsto\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M}),

where
\operatorname{MW-Homod}_{S}
denotes the category of MW-homodules over
S
.
When we want to be precise about the base scheme, we will write

\mathbf{H}_{S}(\mathcal{M})
.

Proof.

We first construct the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-spectrum structure. Recall that

\mathcal{G}^{\operatorname{RS}}_{m}(X)=C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})[m].

The contraction map of Axiom
(wBM10)
, as constructed in the
proof of Proposition
6.1.4
, is the shifted map induced by

[t]\circ q^{!}:C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}(X\times\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{M}_{m+1}),

where
q:X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X
is the projection and
t
is the coordinate on

\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Since the restriction of
[t]
along the unit section is
[1]=0
,
this map factors through

\bar{\beta}_{X,m}:\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m}(X)\longrightarrow\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m+1,-1}(X).

The smooth naturality of the contraction maps, Axiom
(wBM11)
,
shows that
\bar{\beta}_{m}
is natural in
X
.

We next check that
\bar{\beta}_{m}
is an equivalence. Apply localization to the
principal smooth divisor

X\times\{0\}\hookrightarrow X\times\mathbf{A}^{1}

with open complement
X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Homotopy invariance for the affine-line
projection identifies
C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m})
with

C^{\bullet}(X\times\mathbf{A}^{1},\mathcal{M}_{m})
. Under this identification, the residue
morphism

\delta_{t}:\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m+1,-1}(X)\longrightarrow\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m}(X)

is inverse to
\bar{\beta}_{X,m}
: one side is the equality

\delta_{t}\circ[t]\circ q^{!}=\operatorname{id}
, which is

[
DFJ25
, Lem. 1.5.5]
, and the other side follows from the same
localization triangle after restricting to the reduced summand

\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m+1,-1}(X)
. Equivalently, this is the unshifted form of
Axiom
(wBM12)
. Hence the adjoint structure maps

\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m}\longrightarrow\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{m+1,-1}

are equivalences in
L_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\operatorname{Sh}_{{\mathrm{Nis}}}(\Sm_{S},\mathcal{D}(R))
.

It remains to recall why the levels are motivic. Nisnevich descent follows
from the pointwise decomposition of the Rost-Schmid complex for a Nisnevich
square, together with the smooth base-change compatibility of

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 1.5.1(3)]
.
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-invariance follows from
homotopy invariance for vector bundles,
[
DFJ25
, Thm. 2.1.4]
.
Therefore
\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})
is an
\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
-spectrum in
{\Der_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}(S,R)
.

Finally let
\alpha:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
be a morphism of MW-homodules. For every

S
-field
E
and every integer
n
, the map

\alpha_{E,n}:\mathcal{M}_{n}(E)\to\mathcal{N}_{n}(E)
commutes with the structure maps

(D1^{\prime}),\ldots,(D4^{\prime})
. Hence it induces morphisms of Rost-Schmid complexes

\alpha_{\#}:C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{M}_{m},\mathcal{L})\longrightarrow C^{\bullet}(X,\mathcal{N}_{m},\mathcal{L})

for all
X,m,\mathcal{L}
. These maps commute with smooth pullbacks, proper
pushforwards, multiplication by units,
\eta
, residues, localization, and the
contraction maps. They therefore assemble into a map of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-spectra

\mathbf{H}(\alpha):\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\to\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{N})
, functorially in

\alpha
.
∎

Proposition 6.2.4

(Cohomological Chow-Witt groups are represented)
.

Let
X\in\Sm_{S}
, let
v\in\underline{K}(X)
be a virtual vector bundle of rank
r

and determinant
\det(v)
, and let
p,q\in\mathbf{Z}
. There is a canonical
isomorphism

\phi_{X,v}:A^{p-r}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{q-r},\det(v)^{\vee}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H^{p,\{q\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v),\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\bigr).

It is natural in
X
for the lci pullbacks constructed in
Corollary
6.1.5
, and it is compatible with the
proper pushforwards on Rost-Schmid complexes whenever the latter are defined.

Proof.

It is enough, by additivity in
\underline{K}(X)
, to treat the case where
v
is an
actual vector bundle. Since
\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})
is an
\Omega_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
-spectrum,
cohomology with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-twist
q
is computed on the
q
-th level

\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})_{q}
. The Thom twist by a rank
r
bundle changes the
Milnor-Witt degree by
-r
and introduces the determinant twist

\det(v)^{\vee}
, exactly as in the Thom isomorphism for MW-homodules

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 2.4.2]
. Thus

H^{p,\{q\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v),\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)\simeq H^{p-r}\Bigl(C^{\bullet}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{q-r},\det(v)^{\vee}\bigr)\Bigr),

which is the displayed group by definition. Naturality follows from the
construction of
\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})
: for smooth maps it is the usual smooth
pullback on Rost-Schmid complexes, for closed-immersion factors it is the
coherent Gysin pullback of Sections
2
-
5
,
and the compatibility with proper pushforwards is the compatibility of the basic
maps in
[
DFJ25
, §1.5]
.
∎

Proposition 6.2.5

(SL-orientation)
.

The motivic complex
\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})
is canonically
\operatorname{SL}
-oriented. More
precisely, for every vector bundle
E\to X
of rank
r
on a smooth

S
-scheme, the Thom isomorphism of
[
DFJ25
, Prop. 2.4.2]

identifies the Thom isomorphism represented by
\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})
with

A^{p-r}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{q-r},\mathcal{L}\otimes\det(-E)\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }A^{p}_{X}\bigl(E,\mathcal{M}_{q},\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}\bigr).

Consequently an orientation
\det(E)\simeq\mathcal{L}_{0}^{\otimes 2}
gives the usual

\operatorname{SL}^{c}
-oriented Thom isomorphism.

Proof.

This is the Thom isomorphism of MW-homodule cohomology transported through the
representability isomorphism of Proposition
6.2.4
.
The compatibility with pullbacks and direct sums of vector bundles is part of

[
DFJ25
, Prop. 2.4.2]
and is preserved by the construction of

\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})
.
∎

Proposition 6.2.6

(Borel-Moore comparison)
.

Let
X
be a separated
S
-scheme essentially of finite type which is
Zariski-locally a closed subscheme of a smooth
S
-scheme. Let

v\in\underline{K}(X)
have rank
r
and determinant
\det(v)
. Then there is a
canonical isomorphism

\psi_{X,v}:A_{p+r}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{q-r},\det(v)^{\vee}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{p,\{-q\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v)/S,\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\bigr).

These isomorphisms are compatible with proper covariance and with lci
contravariance whenever the corresponding morphisms are defined.

Proof.

Both sides satisfy Zariski descent and localization in
X
. For the
Rost-Schmid side this is the localization sequence of

[
DFJ25
, 1.5.8]
; for the motivic side it is the localization
triangle for Borel-Moore homology. We may therefore assume that
X
admits a
closed immersion

i:X\hookrightarrow\Xi

with
\Xi
smooth over
S
. After refining the cover, we may also assume
that
v
is the restriction of a virtual vector bundle on
\Xi
. Let

j:U=\Xi-X\hookrightarrow\Xi
be the complementary open immersion.

The localization sequence for the Rost-Schmid complex gives a long exact
sequence

\cdots\to A_{*}(X,\mathcal{M}_{*},*)\xrightarrow{i_{*}}A_{*}(\Xi,\mathcal{M}_{*},*)\xrightarrow{j^{*}}A_{*}(U,\mathcal{M}_{*},*)\xrightarrow{\partial}A_{*-1}(X,\mathcal{M}_{*},*)\to\cdots.

The motivic localization triangle for
i
gives the corresponding Borel-Moore
long exact sequence

\cdots\to H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{*}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v)/S,\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)\to H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{*}\bigl(\Th_{\Xi}(v)/S,\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)\to H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{*}\bigl(\Th_{U}(j^{*}v)/S,\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)\to\cdots.

For the smooth schemes
\Xi
and
U
, the required comparison is the
cohomological representability of
Proposition
6.2.4
, after applying the
standard purity identification

H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{p,\{-q\}}\bigl(\Th_{Y}(w)/S,\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)\simeq H^{-p,\{q\}}\bigl(\Th_{Y}(w-\mathrm{L}_{Y/S}),\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)

for a smooth
S
-scheme
Y
, and the duality formula for cohomological and
homological Rost-Schmid complexes recalled at the beginning of this subsection.
The compatibility of Proposition
6.2.4

with residues and localization gives a morphism between the two long exact
sequences. Since it is an isomorphism on the two smooth terms
\Xi
and
U
,
it is an isomorphism on the closed term
X
. The independence of the chosen
embedding and the functoriality for proper maps and lci pullbacks follow from the
same localization argument, together with the base-change and functoriality
compatibilities of
[
DFJ25
, §1.5 and §2.2]
and the coherent
lci functoriality of Corollary
6.1.5
.
∎

6.3.
Representability of Chow and Chow-Witt groups

We now specialize the construction of
Subsection
6.2
to the standard
coefficient systems. Throughout this subsection,
S
is noetherian excellent
and equipped with the fixed dimension function
\delta
. When a pinning

\lambda
is used, it is understood in the sense of

[
DFJ25
, Def. 3.1.3]
.

Notations 6.3.1

(MW-modules and their associated MW-homodules)
.

Assume that
S
is equipped with a pinning
\lambda
. Following

[
DFJ25
, §4.1]
, we use the cohomological MW-modules

M\in\{\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*},\ \operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*}:=\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}/\eta,\ 2\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*},\ \operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*}/2,\ \operatorname{W}:=\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}[\eta^{-1}],\ \operatorname{I}^{*}:=\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}/h\}.

For such an
M
, put

\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(M):=\mathcal{D}_{\lambda}(M).

Thus
\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(M)
is the MW-homodule associated with
M
by the
pinning duality of
[
DFJ25
, Thm. 3.3.4]
. In particular, we
write

\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{\lambda}:=\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}),\qquad\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{M}}_{\lambda}:=\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*}),\qquad\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{I}}_{\lambda}:=\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(\operatorname{I}^{*}),

and

\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{MW},\lambda}:=\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{\lambda}),\qquad\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{M},\lambda}:=\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{M}}_{\lambda}),\qquad\mathbf{H}_{\operatorname{I},\lambda}:=\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{I}}_{\lambda}).

We do not write
\operatorname{K}^{W}_{*}
for the quotient
\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}/h
: in the notation of

[
DFJ25
, §3.4 and §4.1]
this quotient is

\operatorname{I}^{*}
, the fundamental-ideal MW-module. The periodic Witt
MW-module is denoted by
\operatorname{W}=\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}[\eta^{-1}]
.

Independently of the choice of a pinning, Quillen
K^{\prime}
-theory, equivalently
Thomason
G
-theory, defines an oriented MW-homodule over
S
, denoted

\mathcal{K}_{*}
, as in
[
DFJ25
, Ex. 1.1.7(1)]
. We put

\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{K}}:=\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{K}_{*}).

If
S
admits a dualizing complex, the hermitian Borel-Moore theories
recalled in
[
DFJ25
, Ex. 1.1.7(2)]
give a
4
-periodic
family of MW-homodules
\mathcal{GW}^{[i]}_{*}
,
0\leq i\leq 3
, and we put

\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{GW}}^{[i]}:=\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{GW}^{[i]}_{*}).

Notations 6.3.2

(Section 4 conventions of
[
DFJ25
]
)
.

Let
M
be one of the MW-modules in
Notation
6.3.1
. For an
S
-scheme
X

essentially of finite type and a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
, we set

A_{p}(X/S,M_{q},\mathcal{L}):=A_{p}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(M)_{q},\mathcal{L}\bigr).

Equivalently, this is the homology of the complex

C_{p}(X/S,M_{q},\mathcal{L})=\bigoplus_{x\in X_{(p)}}M_{q+p}\bigl(\kappa(x),\lambda_{x}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x}\bigr),

which is exactly the convention of
[
DFJ25
, §4.1.1]
. If

X/S
is essentially lci, we similarly set

A^{p}(X/S,M_{q},\mathcal{L}):=A^{p}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(M)_{q},\mathcal{L}\bigr),

that is, the cohomology of

C^{p}(X/S,M_{q},\mathcal{L})=\bigoplus_{x\in X^{(p)}}M_{q-p}\bigl(\kappa(x),\omega_{X/S,x}\otimes\lambda_{x}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\vee}\bigr),

in agreement with
[
DFJ25
, §4.2.1]
. When
X
is viewed as
its own regular base with the canonical pinning, we omit
/S
, as in

[
DFJ25
, §4.2.3]
.

Definition 6.3.3

(Chow and Chow-Witt groups)
.

With the conventions of Notation
6.3.2
, define

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}_{p}(X/S,\mathcal{L}):=A_{p}(X/S,\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{-p},\mathcal{L}),\qquad\CH_{p}(X/S):=A_{p}(X/S,\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{-p},\mathcal{O}_{X}).

If
X/S
is essentially lci, define the cohomological groups

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}^{p}(X/S,\mathcal{L}):=A^{p}(X/S,\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{p},\mathcal{L}),\qquad\CH^{p}(X/S):=A^{p}(X/S,\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{X}).

For a regular scheme
X
with its canonical pinning and dimension function,
we write simply

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}^{p}(X,\mathcal{L})=A^{p}(X,\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{p},\mathcal{L}),\qquad\CH^{p}(X)=A^{p}(X,\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{X}),

which is the notation of
[
DFJ25
, Def. 4.2.3 and Ex. 4.2.5]
.

Remark 6.3.4

(Relation with the classical theories)
.

The definition above is normalized so that

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}_{p}(X/S,\mathcal{L})
is the homology in the middle of the three-term complex

\bigoplus_{y\in X_{(p+1)}}\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{1}\bigl(\kappa(y),\lambda_{y}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{y}\bigr)\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{x\in X_{(p)}}\GW\bigl(\kappa(x),\lambda_{x}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x}\bigr)\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{s\in X_{(p-1)}}\operatorname{W}\bigl(\kappa(s),\lambda_{s}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{s}\bigr),

as in
[
DFJ25
, §4.1.1-4.1.2]
. The group

\CH_{p}(X/S)
is the usual group of cycles modulo rational equivalence, with
the grading induced by
\delta
. In the regular cohomological case,

\CH^{p}(X)
is the usual codimension
p
Chow group, and

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}^{p}(X,\mathcal{L})
is the Chow-Witt group of codimension
p
quadratic cycles
with coefficients in

\bigoplus_{x\in X^{(p)}}\GW(\kappa(x),\nu_{x}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\vee})
, as in

[
DFJ25
, Def. 4.2.3]
.

Over a field this recovers the Chow-Witt groups of Barge-Morel and Fasel. On
regular
\mathbf{Z}[1/2]
-schemes it agrees with the twisted Chow-Witt groups of
Fasel-Srinivas, up to the standard dual convention for the line-bundle twist;
this comparison is recalled in
[
DFJ25
, §4.2.4 and Prop. 4.2.6]
.
The construction of
[
DFJ25
]
extends these groups to excellent
bases and gives the Borel-Moore groups
\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}_{p}(X/S,\mathcal{L})
for singular

S
-schemes.

The point of the present paper is that these groups are not merely defined by
explicit Rost-Schmid complexes. They are represented by motivic objects
constructed from the higher coherent Gysin formalism. Thus they come with
cohomology on essentially lci schemes, Borel-Moore homology on singular
schemes, localization, homotopy invariance, Thom isomorphisms, and homotopy
coherent lci pullbacks. The remaining structure not constructed here is the
multiplicative one: the cup-product and intersection-product refinements should
come from a future enhancement of
\mathbf{H}(\mathcal{M})
to a commutative algebra
object.

Theorem 6.3.5

(Representability of Chow and Chow-Witt groups)
.

Assume that
S
admits a pinning
\lambda
. In each of the statements below,

X
is as specified there and
\mathcal{L}
is a line bundle on
X
. Put

v_{\mathcal{L}}:=\mathcal{L}^{\vee}-\mathcal{O}_{X}

for the rank-zero virtual vector bundle with
\det(v_{\mathcal{L}})^{\vee}=\mathcal{L}
.

(1)

If
X\in\Sm_{S}
, then for every
n\in\mathbf{Z}
there are canonical
isomorphisms

\CH^{n}(X/S)\simeq H^{n,\{n\}}\bigl(X,\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{M},\lambda}\bigr),

and

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}^{n}(X/S,\mathcal{L})\simeq H^{n,\{n\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v_{\mathcal{L}}),\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{MW},\lambda}\bigr).

(2)

If
X
is a separated
S
-scheme essentially of finite type and
Zariski-locally a closed subscheme of a smooth
S
-scheme, then there are
canonical Borel-Moore representability isomorphisms

\CH_{n}(X/S)\simeq H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{n,\{n\}}\bigl(X/S,\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{M},\lambda}\bigr),

and

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}_{n}(X/S,\mathcal{L})\simeq H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{n,\{n\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v_{\mathcal{L}})/S,\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{MW},\lambda}\bigr).

(3)

The same Chow groups are obtained from the Quillen
K^{\prime}
-theory
MW-homodule
\mathcal{K}_{*}
with the Quillen indexing convention:
for regular
X
,

\CH^{n}(X)\simeq A^{n}(X,\mathcal{K}_{n},\mathcal{O}_{X}),

and, in Borel-Moore degree,

\CH_{n}(X/S)\simeq A_{n}(X,\mathcal{K}_{-n},\mathcal{O}_{X}).

Consequently these groups are represented by
\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{K}}
in the
corresponding bidegrees determined by
Propositions
6.2.4
and

6.2.6
. In particular,

A^{n}(X,\mathcal{K}_{n},\mathcal{O}_{X})\simeq H^{n,\{n\}}\bigl(X,\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{K}}\bigr).

(4)

If
S
admits a dualizing complex, the same diagonal Chow-Witt groups
are obtained from the hermitian
K
-theory MW-homodules. With the convention
that
\mathcal{GW}^{[0]}_{0}(E,L)=\GW(E,L)
over fields, one has

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}^{n}(X/S,\mathcal{L})\simeq A^{n}\bigl(X,\mathcal{GW}^{[0]}_{n},\mathcal{L}\bigr),

and, in Borel-Moore degree,

\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}_{n}(X/S,\mathcal{L})\simeq A_{n}\bigl(X,\mathcal{GW}^{[0]}_{-n},\mathcal{L}\bigr),

with the evident adjustment of the superscript
[i]
under the
4
-periodic
convention of
[
DFJ25
, Ex. 1.1.7(2)]
. These groups are
represented by the corresponding objects
\mathbf{H}_{\mathcal{GW}}^{[i]}
.

Proof.

The first two assertions are direct specializations of
Propositions
6.2.4
and

6.2.6
. For the cohomological Chow-Witt group,
we apply Proposition
6.2.4
to the
rank-zero virtual bundle
v_{\mathcal{L}}=\mathcal{L}^{\vee}-\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Since

\det(v_{\mathcal{L}})^{\vee}=\mathcal{L}
, we get

H^{n,\{n\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v_{\mathcal{L}}),\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{MW},\lambda}\bigr)\simeq A^{n}(X/S,\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{n},\mathcal{L})=\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}^{n}(X/S,\mathcal{L}).

The untwisted Chow case is the same argument with
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}_{X}
and

\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}
replaced by
\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*}
. The Borel-Moore formulas follow from
Proposition
6.2.6
with
p=n
,
q=-n
, and
with the same virtual bundle
v_{\mathcal{L}}
:

H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{n,\{n\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v_{\mathcal{L}})/S,\mathbf{H}_{\mathrm{MW},\lambda}\bigr)\simeq A_{n}(X/S,\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{-n},\mathcal{L})=\widetilde{\operatorname{CH}}{}_{n}(X/S,\mathcal{L}).

For Quillen
K
-theory,
\mathcal{K}_{*}
is the MW-homodule associated with
Quillen
K^{\prime}
-theory, or Thomason
G
-theory, by

[
DFJ25
, Ex. 1.1.7(1)]
. On regular schemes the diagonal
Gersten-Quillen complex gives the usual Chow groups; the indexing

\CH_{n}(X/S)=A_{n}(X,\mathcal{K}_{-n},\mathcal{O}_{X})
is the convention used in

[
DFJ25
, §4.5]
. The representability statements then follow
from Propositions
6.2.4
and

6.2.6
.

For hermitian
K
-theory, the MW-homodules
\mathcal{GW}^{[i]}_{*}
are those
of
[
DFJ25
, Ex. 1.1.7(2)]
. The displayed indexing is chosen so
that the middle term of the diagonal Rost-Schmid complex is the group

\GW(\kappa(x),\lambda_{x}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x})
in homological degree and

\GW(\kappa(x),\omega_{X/S,x}\otimes\lambda_{x}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{x}^{\vee})
in
cohomological degree, exactly as in the Milnor-Witt definition of Chow-Witt
groups. The same representability propositions therefore apply.
∎

Example 6.3.6

(Witt-type quotients and localizations)
.

The same construction applies to the MW-modules obtained from
\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}
by
localization and quotient in
[
DFJ25
, §3.4]
. Thus the
MW-homodules associated with

\operatorname{W}=\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}[\eta^{-1}],\qquad\operatorname{I}^{*}=\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}/h,\qquad 2\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*},\qquad\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*}/2

give motivic objects representing the corresponding unramified Witt,
fundamental-ideal, and mod-two Milnor Rost-Schmid theories. The exact
sequences of
[
DFJ25
, Thm. 3.4.5 and Cor. 3.4.6]
become exact
triangles after applying
\mathbf{H}
.

Notations 6.3.7

(The standard objects over variable bases)
.

Fix the base
S
and let
\mathcal{M}
be one of the standard examples

\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{MW}}_{*}),\quad\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(\operatorname{K}^{\mathrm{M}}_{*}),\quad\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(\operatorname{W}),\quad\mathcal{M}_{\lambda}(\operatorname{I}^{*}),\quad\mathcal{K}_{*},\quad\mathcal{GW}^{[i]}_{*}.

If
T\to S
is essentially of finite type, we equip
T
with the induced
dimension function. In the pinned cases we also equip
T
with the pulled-back
pinning. We denote by

\mathbf{H}_{T}(\mathcal{M})\in{\Der_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}(T,R)

the object constructed above from the MW-homodule
\mathcal{M}
restricted over
T
.
Although this MW-homodule is formally the base-change of
\mathcal{M}
to
T
, we
keep the notation
\mathcal{M}
in the Chow-Witt groups below: the groups are
computed from the same coefficient system, evaluated on the
T
-fields, with
the dimension function induced from
S
.

More explicitly, if
X\in\Sm_{T}
, if
v\in\underline{K}(X)
has rank
r
and
determinant
\det(v)
, then Proposition
6.2.4

gives

(6.3.7.a)

H^{p,\{q\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v),\mathbf{H}_{T}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)\simeq A^{p-r}\bigl(X/T,\mathcal{M}_{q-r},\det(v)^{\vee}\bigr).

Here the cohomological Chow-Witt group is formed relative to
T
, so that
the relative determinant line is
\omega_{X/T}
.

Similarly, if
X
is a separated
T
-scheme essentially of finite type and
Zariski-locally a closed subscheme of a smooth
T
-scheme, then
Proposition
6.2.6
gives

(6.3.7.b)

H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{p,\{-q\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v)/T,\mathbf{H}_{T}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)\simeq A_{p+r}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{q-r},\det(v)^{\vee}\bigr),

where the homological Chow-Witt group is computed with the dimension function
on
X
induced from
T
, equivalently from
S
.

Proposition 6.3.8

(Exceptional base change for the standard examples)
.

Let
f:T\to S
be a separated morphism of finite type. Let
\mathcal{M}
be one of
the standard examples of Notation
6.3.7
.
Then there is a canonical equivalence in
{\Der_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}(T,R)

f^{!}\mathbf{H}_{S}(\mathcal{M})\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathbf{H}_{T}(\mathcal{M}).

Proof.

Let
X\to T
be separated of finite type and let
v\in\underline{K}(X)
have rank

r
. By the formalism of extraordinary pullback in the motivic derived
category, there is a canonical identification

H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{p,\{-q\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v)/T,f^{!}\mathbf{H}_{S}(\mathcal{M})\bigr)\simeq H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{p,\{-q\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v)/S,\mathbf{H}_{S}(\mathcal{M})\bigr).

Applying (
6.3.7.b
) over the base
S
, the right-hand side
is

A_{p+r}\bigl(X,\mathcal{M}_{q-r},\det(v)^{\vee}\bigr),

where
X
is viewed as an
S
-scheme. Since the dimension function on
T

is induced from that on
S
, the induced dimension function on
X
is the
same whether
X
is viewed over
T
or over
S
. Thus the preceding group
is also the Chow-Witt group appearing in (
6.3.7.b
) over
the base
T
, and hence it identifies canonically with

H^{\operatorname{BM}}_{p,\{-q\}}\bigl(\Th_{X}(v)/T,\mathbf{H}_{T}(\mathcal{M})\bigr).

The objects
g_{!}\Th_{X}(v)[n]
, with
g:X\to T
separated of finite type,
generate
{\Der_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}}(T,R)
. Therefore these identifications on Borel-Moore
realizations determine and detect the claimed equivalence.
∎
